Version](section-24-20190617.html "Link to previous version of section 24"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Application for Declaration of Relief Under Subsection 42.1(1) of the Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-685957 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[24.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may apply for a declaration of relief under
  subsection 42.1(1) of the Act if a decision has been made to refuse
  their application for permanent or temporary resident status, or a
  removal order has been issued against them, on the basis of a
  determination of inadmissibility under section 34, paragraph 35(1)(b)
  or subsection 37(1) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, if the foreign national has filed an
  application for leave to commence an application for judicial review
  under subsection 72(1) of the Act with respect to a decision or
  removal order referred to in subsection (1), the foreign national may
  only make an application under subsection (1) after the earliest of
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court refuses the application for
    leave,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application for leave is granted, the
    Federal Court refuses the application for judicial review and no
    question is certified for the Federal Court of Appeal,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a question is certified for the Federal Court of
    Appeal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an appeal to the Federal Court of Appeal is not
      filed within the time limit, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court of Appeal dismisses the appeal
      and an application to the Supreme Court of Canada for leave to
      appeal from that decision is not filed within the time limit,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an application is filed with the Supreme Court
    of Canada for leave to appeal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the application is refused,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the application is granted and an appeal is not
      filed within the time limit, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the Supreme Court of Canada dismisses the
      appeal, and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national discontinues their application
    for leave to commence an application for judicial review,
    application for judicial review, appeal to the Federal Court of
    Appeal, application to the Supreme Court of Canada for leave to
    appeal or appeal to the Supreme Court of Canada, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-38, s. 3
- [2023, c. 19, s. 16](#1411948-1414397){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1411948-1414397.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24.1-20170310.html "Link to previous version of section 24.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required information

- **[[24.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application under subsection 42.1(1) of the Act must include the
  following information in respect of the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their place of birth, gender and marital status and
    the names of any former spouses or common-law partners;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} their telephone number and email address, if any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} their former countries of citizenship or former
    countries of nationality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} their education, including the name and address of
    all elementary and secondary schools and post-secondary, technical
    and vocational institutions attended and the start and end dates for
    the periods during which they attended each school or institution;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} their work history, including volunteer work, from
    the age of 16 years, including start and end dates for each period
    of work, their job title and job description and the employer's name
    and address;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} their international travel history from the age of
    16 years, including a list of the countries visited, the purpose of
    the visits, the dates and duration of the visits and any immigration
    status sought from or granted by any country visited; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the provision of the Act under which they were
    determined to be inadmissible --- section 34, paragraph 35(1)(b) or
    subsection 37(1) --- as well as the date on which and the city and
    country in which the determination was made and whether the
    determination resulted in a decision or removal order referred to in
    subsection 24.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of paragraphs 10(2)(b)
  and (c)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 10(2)(b) and (c) do not apply to an
  application under subsection 42.1(1) of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-38, s. 3
- [2023, c. 19, s. 17](#1411950-1414399){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1411950-1414399.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24.2-20170310.html "Link to previous version of section 24.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of application

**[[24.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.3 .sectionLabel}** If the requirements
of sections 24.1 and 24.2 are not met, the application is not accepted
for processing and the application and all documents submitted in
support of it are returned to the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-38, s. 3
- SOR/2018-251, s. 3(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24.3-20170310.html "Link to previous version of section 24.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Closing of file

**[[24.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.4 .sectionLabel}** The processing of the
application is discontinued and the applicant's file is closed if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice has been sent to the applicant requiring
  that they confirm their intention to proceed with their application
  and the applicant fails to respond to the notice within 60 calendar
  days after the day on which it was sent;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has acquired permanent resident status;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the applicant withdraws their application in writing;
  or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has, since making their application,
  filed an application for leave to commence an application for judicial
  review under subsection 72(1) of the Act with respect to a decision or
  removal order referred to in subsection 24.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-38, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in information

**[[24.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.5 .sectionLabel}** The applicant must
notify the Minister without delay of any change in their address,
telephone number or email address and, if the applicant is represented,
the address, telephone number, fax number or email address of their
representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-38, s. 3
:::

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Procedures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-686005 .Part}

### [DIVISION 0.1]{.HLabel2}[Expiry Date]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686006 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

- **[[24.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The expiry date of any work permit,
  study permit --- including any renewal of them --- temporary resident
  permit having an expiry date, temporary resident visa, or document
  that indicates the temporary resident status of a person who is a
  member of the visitor class, issued to a person who is required to
  provide their biometric information under section 10.01 of the Act,
  must not be later than 10 years after the latest day on which the
  person provided their biometric information under section 10.01 of the
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of latest day

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The latest day on which the person provided their
  biometric information is determined at the time of the issuance of the
  permit, visa or other document.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-128, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Visa Issuance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686014 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When unenforced removal order

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** A visa shall not be
issued to a foreign national who is subject to an unenforced removal
order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General rule --- one-step process

- **[[25.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining
  whether a child is a dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of
  a child of a person who is a member of any of the classes set out in
  these Regulations, other than in those cases referred to in
  subsections (2) to (9), and who makes an application under Division 5,
  6 or 7 of Part 5 is the date on which the application is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificat de sélection --- distressful
  situation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who is referred to in section 71, to whom a *[Certificat de sélection
  du Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}* has been issued declaring that that
  person is in a particularly distressful situation and who makes an
  application under Division 6 of Part 5 is the date on which the
  application for selection was made to Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quebec economic candidate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who is referred to in section 86, 90, 97 or 101, to whom a Certificat
  de sélection du Québec has been issued and who makes an application
  under Division 6 of Part 5 is the date on which the application for
  selection was made to Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial nominee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who is a member of the provincial nominee class, who is nominated by
  the province and who makes an application under Division 6 of Part 5
  is the date on which the application for nomination was made to the
  province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Atlantic economic candidate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who is a member of the Atlantic immigration class and who makes an
  application under Division 6 of Part 5 is the date on which the
  application for endorsement was made to the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship --- refugee

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who is referred to in paragraph 139(1)(h), who makes an application
  under Division 6 of Part 5 and in respect of whom an undertaking
  application is made by a sponsor who meets the requirements of
  sponsorship set out in section 158 is the date on which the
  undertaking application was made to Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refugee

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who submits an application for a permanent resident visa under
  Division 1 of Part 8 along with one of the referrals set out in
  section 140.3 is the date on which the referral was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family member who does not accompany
  applicant

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child who
  submits an application under paragraph 141(1)(b) is the dependent
  child of a person who has submitted an application under paragraph
  139(1)(b), the lock-in date for the age of that child is the date on
  which that person submitted the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refugee protection

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of determining whether a child is a
  dependent child, the lock-in date for the age of a child of a person
  who has submitted a claim for refugee protection inside Canada under
  subsection 99(3) of the Act, who has acquired protected person status
  and who has made an application for permanent residence is the date on
  which the claim for refugee protection was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-133, s. 2
- SOR/2016-316, s. 1
- SOR/2017-78, s. 2
- [SOR/2021-242, s. 1](#1331465-1335927){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1331465-1335927.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25.1-20170505.html "Link to previous version of section 25.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1.1]{.HLabel2}[Issuance of Electronic Travel Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686037 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic travel authorization not to be
issued

**[[25.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25.2 .sectionLabel}** An electronic travel
authorization shall not be issued to a foreign national who is subject
to an unenforced removal order.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-77, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Authorization to Enter Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686042 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of ports of entry

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, on the
basis of the following factors, designate a place as a port of entry as
well as the port of entry's dates and hours of operation:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the frequency or anticipated frequency of persons
  arriving from abroad in the area under consideration;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the need for the services of the Canada Border
  Services Agency in that area;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operational requirements of commercial
  transporters;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} administrative arrangements with other departments or
  agencies of the Government of Canada;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operational capacity of the Canada Border
  Services Agency; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} any order or regulation made by the Governor in
  Council under the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the
  [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1) that prohibits the entry of certain
  persons into Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-214, s. 1
- [SOR/2020-55, s. 2](#1241233-1241828){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1241233-1241828.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20171018.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services at port of entry

- **[[26.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on the basis of
  the factors set out in section 26, specify whether the services
  provided at a port of entry include the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the collection of biometric information under
    section 12.3;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reception of a permanent resident visa that a
    foreign national presents, under section 71.1, in order to become a
    permanent resident;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for an extension of
    an authorization to remain in Canada as a temporary resident under
    section 181;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for the restoration
    of temporary resident status under section 182;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for a work permit
    under subsection 198(1);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for the renewal of
    a work permit under section 201;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for a study permit
    under section 214; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the reception of an application for the renewal of
    a study permit under section 217.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class of foreign nationals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may also, on the basis of those factors,
  specify whether a service set out in subsection (1) is provided to a
  specified class of foreign nationals at a specified port of entry.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-55, s. 3](#1241235-1241840){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1241235-1241840.html"}
- [SOR/2024-258, s. 1](#1499646-1505095){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1499646-1505095.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26.1-20200321.html "Link to previous version of section 26.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation on entry

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  these Regulations provide otherwise, for the purpose of the
  examination required by subsection 18(1) of the Act, a person must
  appear without delay before an officer at a port of entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seeking entry at a place other than a
  port of entry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless these Regulations provide otherwise, a person
  who seeks to enter Canada at a place other than a port of entry must
  appear without delay for examination at the port of entry that is
  nearest to that place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refused entry elsewhere

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 18 of the Act, every
  person who has been returned to Canada as a result of the refusal of
  another country to allow that person entry is a person seeking to
  enter Canada.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Conduct of Examination]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686058 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686059 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 15(1) of the Act, a person makes an application in accordance
with the Act by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} submitting an application in writing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} seeking to enter Canada;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} seeking to transit through Canada as provided in
  section 35; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} making a claim for refugee protection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical examination

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act, a medical examination includes any or all
of the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} physical examination;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} mental examination;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} review of past medical history;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} laboratory test;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} diagnostic test; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} medical assessment of records respecting the
  applicant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions from medical examination
requirement

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act, the following foreign
  nationals are exempt from the requirement to submit to a medical
  examination:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals other than

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (g), foreign nationals who
      are applying for a permanent resident visa or applying to remain
      in Canada as a permanent resident, as well as their family
      members, whether accompanying or not,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals who are seeking to work in
      Canada in an occupation in which the protection of public health
      is essential,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals who

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} are seeking to enter Canada or applying for
        renewal of their work or study permit or authorization to remain
        in Canada as a temporary resident for a period in excess of six
        consecutive months, including an actual or proposed period of
        absence from Canada of less than 14 days, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} have resided or stayed for a period of six
        consecutive months, at any time during the one-year period
        immediately preceding the date that they sought entry or made
        their application, in an area that the Minister determines,
        after consultation with the Minister of Health, has a higher
        incidence of serious communicable disease than Canada,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals who an officer, or the
      Immigration Division, has reasonable grounds to believe are
      inadmissible under subsection 38(1) of the Act,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals who claim refugee protection in
      Canada, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals who are seeking to enter or
      remain in Canada and who may apply to the Minister for protection
      under subsection 112(1) of the Act, other than foreign nationals
      who have not left Canada since their claim for refugee protection
      or application for protection was rejected;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person described in paragraph 186(b) who is
    entering or is in Canada to carry out official duties, unless they
    seek to engage or continue in secondary employment in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a family member of a person described in paragraph
    186(b), unless that family member seeks to engage or continue in
    employment in Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a member of the armed forces of a country that is a
    designated state as defined in the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2), who is entering or is in Canada to carry out
    official duties, other than a person who has been designated as a
    civilian component of those armed forces, unless that member seeks
    to engage or continue in secondary employment in Canada;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a family member of a protected person, if the
    family member is not included in the protected person's application
    to remain in Canada as a permanent resident; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a non-accompanying family member of a foreign
    national who has applied for refugee protection outside Canada.

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2017-78, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every foreign national who has undergone a medical
  examination as required under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act must
  submit to a new medical examination before entering Canada if, after
  being authorized to enter and remain in Canada, they have resided or
  stayed for a total period in excess of six months in an area that the
  Minister determines, after consultation with the Minister of Health,
  has a higher incidence of serious communicable disease than Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every foreign national who must submit to a medical
  examination, as required under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act, and who
  seeks to enter Canada must hold a medical certificate --- based on the
  most recent medical examination to which they were required to submit
  under that paragraph and which took place within the previous 12
  months --- that indicates that their health condition is not likely to
  be a danger to public health or public safety and, unless subsection
  38(2) of the Act applies, is not reasonably expected to cause
  excessive demand.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 9
- SOR/2010-78, s. 1
- SOR/2012-154, s. 3
- SOR/2017-78, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20120815.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public health

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** Before opining whether a
foreign national's health condition is likely to be a danger to public
health, an officer who is assessing the foreign national's health
condition shall consider

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any report made by a health practitioner or medical
  laboratory with respect to the foreign national;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the communicability of any disease that the foreign
  national is affected by or carries; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the impact that the disease could have on other
  persons living in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-39, s. 3](#1340408-1340682){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1340408-1340682.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** In addition to the
conditions that are imposed on a foreign national who makes an
application as a member of a class, an officer may impose, vary or
cancel the following conditions in respect of any foreign national who
is required to submit to a medical examination under paragraph 16(2)(b)
of the Act:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to report at the specified times and places for
  medical examination, surveillance or treatment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to provide proof, at the specified times and places,
  of compliance with the conditions imposed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public safety

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** Before opining whether a
foreign national's health condition is likely to be a danger to public
safety, an officer who is assessing the foreign national's health
condition shall consider

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any reports made by a health practitioner or medical
  laboratory with respect to the foreign national; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the risk of a sudden incapacity or of unpredictable
  or violent behaviour of the foreign national that would create a
  danger to the health or safety of persons living in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-39, s. 4](#1340413-1340685){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1340413-1340685.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excessive demand

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer who is assessing a foreign national's health condition shall
  analyze all relevant medical factors that apply to a determination of
  whether the foreign national's health condition might reasonably be
  expected to cause excessive demand and shall prepare an opinion based
  on their analysis.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Medical factors referred to in subsection (1) include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any reports made by a health practitioner or
    medical laboratory with respect to the foreign national;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any condition identified by a medical examination
    required under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the availability of and anticipated costs for
    health services and social services arising from the foreign
    national's health status; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a consideration of whether a mitigation plan, if
    any, submitted by the foreign national would provide for appropriate
    treatment for the health condition and would be permitted under the
    rules regulating the delivery of health care in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-medical factors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The officer shall not consider non-medical factors,
  including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national's intent and financial ability
    to mitigate any excessive demand; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the feasibility of a mitigation plan, if any,
    submitted by the foreign national.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-39, s. 5](#1340418-1340700){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1340418-1340700.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transit

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  following persons are not seeking to enter Canada but are making an
  application under subsection 15(1) of the Act to transit through
  Canada:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in airports where there are United States'
    in-transit preclearance facilities, in-transit preclearance
    passengers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any airport, passengers who are arriving from
    any country and who are transiting to a country other than Canada
    and remain in a sterile transit area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligatory examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person seeking to leave a sterile transit area
  must appear immediately for examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions not constituting a complete
examination

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** An inspection carried out
aboard a means of transportation bringing persons to Canada or the
questioning of persons embarking on or disembarking from a means of
transportation, or the examination of any record or document respecting
such persons before they appear for examination at a port of entry, is
part of an examination but does not constitute a complete examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of examination

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  examination of a person who seeks to enter Canada, or who makes an
  application to transit through Canada, ends only when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a determination is made that the person has a right
    to enter Canada, or is authorized to enter Canada as a temporary
    resident or permanent resident, the person is authorized to leave
    the port of entry at which the examination takes place and the
    person leaves the port of entry;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person is an in-transit passenger, the
    person departs from Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized to withdraw their
    application to enter Canada and an officer verifies their departure
    from Canada; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a decision in respect of the person is made under
    subsection 44(2) of the Act and the person leaves the port of entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of examination --- claim for
  refugee protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The examination of a person who makes a claim for
  refugee protection at a port of entry or inside Canada other than at a
  port of entry ends when the later of the following occurs:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer determines that their claim is
    ineligible under section 101 of the Act or the Refugee Protection
    Division accepts or rejects their claim under section 107 of the
    Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a decision in respect of the person is made under
    subsection 44(2) of the Act and, in the case of a claim made at a
    port of entry, the person leaves the port of entry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 10(F)
- SOR/2016-136, s. 3(F)
- SOR/2018-60, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20160613.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Alternative Means of Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686139 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 18(1) of the Act, the following persons may --- unless
otherwise directed by an officer --- be examined by the means indicated
as alternative to appearing for an examination by an officer at a port
of entry:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} persons who have previously been examined and hold an
  authorization issued under section 11.1 of the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6), in which case examination is effected by the
  presentation of the authorization by those persons at a port of entry;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} persons who are seeking to enter Canada at a port of
  entry where facilities are in place for automatic screening of persons
  seeking to enter Canada, in which case examination is performed by
  automatic screening;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} persons who leave Canada and proceed directly to a
  marine installation or structure to which the [Oceans
  Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4) applies, and who return directly to Canada from
  the installation or structure without entering the territorial waters
  of a foreign state, in which case examination is conducted by an
  officer by telephone or other means of telecommunication;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} members of a crew of a ship that transports oil or
  liquid natural gas and that docks at a marine installation or
  structure to which the [Oceans Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4) applies, for the
  purpose of loading oil or liquid natural gas, in which case
  examination is conducted by an officer by telephone or other means of
  telecommunication;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} members of a crew of a ship registered in a foreign
  country, other than members of a crew referred to in paragraph (d), in
  which case examination is conducted by an officer by telephone or
  other means of telecommunication;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} members of a crew of a ship registered in Canada, in
  which case examination is conducted by an officer by telephone or
  other means of telecommunication;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} citizens or permanent residents of Canada or the
  United States who are seeking to enter Canada at remote locations
  where no officer is assigned or where there are no means by which the
  persons may report for examination, in which case examination is
  conducted by an officer by telephone or other means of
  telecommunication; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} citizens or permanent residents of Canada or the
  United States who seek to enter Canada at places, other than a port of
  entry, where no officer is assigned, in which case examination is
  conducted by an officer by telephone or other means of
  telecommunication.

#### [Permitted Entry]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686150 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entry permitted

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall allow
the following persons to enter Canada following an examination:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} persons who have been returned to Canada as a result
  of a refusal of another country to allow them entry after they were
  removed from or otherwise left Canada after a removal order was made
  against them;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} persons returning to Canada under a transfer order
  made under the [Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal Matters
  Act](/eng/acts/M-13.6) and who, immediately before being transferred
  to a foreign state under the transfer order, were subject to an
  unenforced removal order; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} persons who are in possession of refugee travel
  papers issued to them by the Minister that are valid for return to
  Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-46, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-39-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 39"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Conduct of Examination Measures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686158 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction to leave

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  in the case of protected persons within the meaning of subsection
  95(2) of the Act and refugee protection claimants, an officer who is
  unable to examine a person who is seeking to enter Canada at a port of
  entry shall, in writing, direct the person to leave Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the direction shall be served on the person
  as well as on the owner or person in control of the means of
  transportation, if any, that brought the person to Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to have effect

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The direction ceases to have effect when the person
  appears again at a port of entry and an officer proceeds to examine
  the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direct back

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** Unless an authorization
has been given under section 23 of the Act, an officer who examines a
foreign national who is seeking to enter Canada from the United States
shall direct them to return temporarily to the United States if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} no officer is able to complete an examination;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is not available to consider, under
  subsection 44(2) of the Act, a report prepared with respect to the
  person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an admissibility hearing cannot be held by the
  Immigration Division; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is prohibited from entering
  Canada by an order or regulation made by the Governor in Council under
  the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine
  Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-55, s. 4](#1241237-1241845){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1241237-1241845.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 41"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawing application

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  officer who examines a foreign national who is seeking to enter Canada
  and who has indicated that they want to withdraw their application to
  enter Canada shall allow the foreign national to withdraw their
  application and leave Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a report is being prepared or has been prepared
  under subsection 44(1) of the Act in respect of a foreign national who
  indicates that they want to withdraw their application to enter
  Canada, the officer shall not allow the foreign national to withdraw
  their application or leave Canada unless the Minister decides either
  not to make a removal order or not to refer the report to the
  Immigration Division for an admissibility hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to confirm departure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is allowed to withdraw their
  application to enter Canada must appear without delay before an
  officer at a port of entry to confirm their departure from Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-5, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-42-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 42"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application of Section 23 of the Act]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686180 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer must impose the following conditions on every person
  authorized to enter Canada under section 23 of the Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to report in person at the time and place specified
    for the completion of the examination or the admissibility hearing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to not engage in any work in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to not attend any educational institution in
    Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to report in person to an officer at a port of
    entry if the person withdraws their application to enter Canada; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to comply with all requirements imposed on them by
    an order or regulation made under the [Emergencies
    Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of authorization to enter

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is authorized to enter Canada
  under section 23 of the Act does not, by reason only of that
  authorization, become a temporary resident or a permanent resident.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-91, s. 3](#1243441-1244398){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1243441-1244398.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-43-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 43"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Obligation to Appear at an Admissibility Hearing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686190 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  class of persons who are the subject of a report referred for an
  admissibility hearing under subsection 44(2) of the Act is prescribed
  as a class of persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of the class of persons who are the
  subject of a report referred for an admissibility hearing under
  subsection 44(2) of the Act are the persons who are the subject of
  such a report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every member of the class prescribed under
  subsection (1) must appear at their admissibility hearing before the
  Immigration Division if they are given notice of the hearing by the
  Division.

#### [Deposits or Guarantees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686198 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit or guarantee required on entry

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer can require, in respect of a person or group of persons
  seeking to enter Canada, the payment of a deposit or the posting of a
  guarantee, or both, to the Minister for compliance with any conditions
  imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The amount of the deposit or guarantee is fixed by an
  officer on the basis of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the financial resources of the person or group;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the obligations that result from the conditions
    imposed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the costs that would likely be incurred to locate
    and arrest the person or group, to detain them, to hold an
    admissibility hearing and to remove them from Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a guarantee, the costs that would
    likely be incurred to enforce it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 11(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** Sections 47 to 49 apply
to deposits and guarantees required under subsections 44(3), 56(1),
58(3) and 58.1(3) of the Act and section 45 of these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-92, s. 1](#1245072-1245188){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1245072-1245188.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-46-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirements

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who pays a deposit or posts a guarantee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must not have signed or co-signed another guarantee
    that is in default; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} must have the capacity to contract in the province
    where the deposit is paid or the guarantee is posted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements if guarantee posted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who posts a guarantee must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be a Canadian citizen or a permanent resident,
    physically present and residing in Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be able to ensure that the person or group of
    persons in respect of whom the guarantee is required will comply
    with the conditions imposed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} present to an officer evidence of their ability to
    fulfil the obligation arising from the guarantee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Money illegally obtained

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an officer has reasonable grounds to believe that
  a sum of money offered by a person as a deposit was not legally
  obtained, or that a sum of money that a person may be obliged to pay
  under a guarantee would not be legally obtained, the officer shall not
  allow that person to pay a deposit or post a guarantee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An officer, the Immigration Division or the Minister
  must consider the following factors in assessing whether the person
  who posts a guarantee has the ability to ensure that the person or
  group of persons in respect of whom the guarantee is required will
  comply with the conditions imposed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their relationship to the person or group of
    persons in respect of whom the guarantee is required;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} their financial situation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any previous history posting a guarantee;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} their criminal record; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor in determining their
    ability to ensure that the person or group of persons in respect of
    whom the guarantee is required will comply with the conditions
    imposed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 12(F)
- [SOR/2020-92, s. 2](#1245074-1245196){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1245074-1245196.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 47"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions if guarantee posted

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to any other conditions that are imposed, the following
  conditions are imposed on a person or group of persons in respect of
  whom a guarantee is required:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to provide the Department or the Canada Border
    Services Agency, depending on which one requires the information,
    with the address of the person posting the guarantee and to advise
    the Department or the Canada Border Services Agency, as the case may
    be, before any change in that address; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to present themself or themselves at the time and
    place that an officer or the Immigration Division requires them to
    appear to comply with any obligation imposed on them under the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions if deposit paid

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to any other conditions that are imposed,
  the following conditions are imposed on a person or group of persons
  in respect of whom a deposit is required:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to provide the Department or the Canada Border
    Services Agency, depending on which one requires the information,
    with their address and to advise the Department or the Canada Border
    Services Agency, as the case may be, before any change in that
    address; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to present themself or themselves at the time and
    place that an officer or the Immigration Division requires them to
    appear to comply with any obligation imposed on them under the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-195, s. 1(F)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-48-20100923.html "Link to previous version of section 48"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acknowledgment of consequences of failure
to comply with conditions

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who pays a deposit or posts a guarantee must acknowledge in
  writing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that they have been informed of the conditions
    imposed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that they have been informed that non-compliance
    with any conditions imposed will result in the forfeiture of the
    deposit or enforcement of the guarantee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Receipt

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall issue a receipt for the deposit or a
  copy of the guarantee, and a copy of the conditions imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of deposit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an officer determines that the person or group of
  persons in respect of whom the deposit was required has complied with
  the conditions imposed, the deposit shall be returned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Breach of condition

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A sum of money deposited is forfeited, or a guarantee
  posted becomes enforceable, on the failure of the person or any member
  of the group of persons in respect of whom the deposit or guarantee
  was required to comply with a condition imposed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 13(F)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-49-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 49"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Documents Required]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686250 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents --- permanent residents

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the permanent resident visa required of a foreign national
  who is a member of a class referred to in subsection 70(2), a foreign
  national seeking to become a permanent resident must hold

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a passport, other than a diplomatic, official or
    similar passport, that was issued by the country of which the
    foreign national is a citizen or national;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a travel document that was issued by the country of
    which the foreign national is a citizen or national;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an identity or travel document that was issued by a
    country to non-national residents, refugees or stateless persons who
    are unable to obtain a passport or other travel document from their
    country of citizenship or nationality or who have no country of
    citizenship or nationality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a travel document that was issued by the
    International Committee of the Red Cross in Geneva, Switzerland, to
    enable and facilitate emigration;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a passport or travel document that was issued by
    the Palestinian Authority;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an exit visa that was issued by the Government of
    the Union of Soviet Socialist Republics to its citizens who were
    compelled to relinquish their Soviet nationality in order to
    emigrate from that country;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British National (Overseas), as a person born, naturalized or
    registered in Hong Kong;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the Hong Kong Special
    Administrative Region of the People's Republic of China; or

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British Subject.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- protected persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a person who is a
  protected person within the meaning of subsection 95(2) of the Act and
  holds a permanent resident visa when it is not possible for the person
  to obtain a passport or an identity or travel document referred to in
  subsection (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2010-54, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-253, s. 1
- SOR/2010-54, s. 1
- SOR/2011-125, s. 1
- SOR/2014-139, s. 1(F)
- SOR/2016-298, s. 1(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50-20140529.html "Link to previous version of section 50"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of unreliable travel
documents

- **[[50.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may designate,
  individually or by class, passports or travel or identity documents
  that do not constitute reliable proof of identity or nationality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall consider the following factors in
  determining whether to designate any passport or travel or identity
  document, or class of passport or travel or identity document, as not
  being reliable proof of identity or nationality:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the adequacy of security features incorporated into
    the passport or document for the purpose of deterring its misuse or
    unauthorized alteration, reproduction or issuance; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information respecting the security or integrity of
    the process leading to the issuance of the passport or document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of designation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A passport or travel or identity document that has
  been designated under subsection (1) is not a passport or travel or
  identity document for the purpose of subsection 50(1) or 52(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall make available to the public a
  list of all passports or travel or identity documents designated under
  subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-54, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination --- permanent residents

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national who
holds a permanent resident visa and is seeking to become a permanent
resident must, at the time of their examination,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} inform the officer if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has become a spouse or
    common-law partner or has ceased to be a spouse, common-law partner
    or conjugal partner after the visa was issued, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} material facts relevant to the issuance of the
    visa have changed since the visa was issued or were not divulged
    when it was issued; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish that they and their family members, whether
  accompanying or not, meet the requirements of the Act and these
  Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-253, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-51-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 51"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents --- temporary residents

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the other requirements of these Regulations, a foreign
  national seeking to become a temporary resident must hold one of the
  following documents that is valid for the period authorized for their
  stay:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a passport that was issued by the country of which
    the foreign national is a citizen or national, that does not
    prohibit travel to Canada and that the foreign national may use to
    enter the country of issue;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a travel document that was issued by the country of
    which the foreign national is a citizen or national, that does not
    prohibit travel to Canada and that the foreign national may use to
    enter the country of issue;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an identity or travel document that was issued by a
    country, that does not prohibit travel to Canada, that the foreign
    national may use to enter the country of issue and that is of the
    type issued by that country to non-national residents, refugees or
    stateless persons who are unable to obtain a passport or other
    travel document from their country of citizenship or nationality or
    who have no country of citizenship or nationality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a laissez-passer that was issued by the United
    Nations;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a passport or travel document that was issued by
    the Palestinian Authority;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a document that was issued by the Organization of
    American States and is entitled "Official Travel Document";

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British Overseas Citizen;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British National (Overseas), as a person born, naturalized or
    registered in Hong Kong;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the Hong Kong Special
    Administrative Region of the People's Republic of China;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British Subject; or

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a laissez-passer that was issued by the European
    Union.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2003-260, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} citizens of the United States;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} persons seeking to enter Canada from the United
    States or St. Pierre and Miquelon who have been lawfully admitted to
    the United States for permanent residence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} residents of Greenland seeking to enter Canada from
    Greenland;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} persons seeking to enter Canada from St. Pierre and
    Miquelon who are citizens of France and residents of St. Pierre and
    Miquelon;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} members of the armed forces of a country that is a
    designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2) who are seeking entry in order to carry out
    official duties, other than persons who have been designated as a
    civilian component of those armed forces;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} persons who are seeking to enter Canada as, or in
    order to become, members of a crew of a means of air transportation
    and who hold an airline flight crew licence or crew member
    certificate issued in accordance with International Civil Aviation
    Organization specifications; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} persons seeking to enter Canada as members of a
    crew who hold a seafarer's identity document issued under
    International Labour Organization conventions and are members of the
    crew of the vessel that carries them to Canada.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2010-54, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}
