## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Permanent Residents]{.HTitleText1} {#h-686315 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Permanent Resident Cards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686316 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document indicating status

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 31(1) of the Act, the document indicating
  the status of a permanent resident is a permanent resident card that
  is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provided by the Department to a person who has
    become a permanent resident under the Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issued by the Department, on application, to a
    permanent resident who has become a permanent resident under the Act
    or a permanent resident who obtained that status under the
    Immigration Act, chapter I-2 of the Revised Statutes of Canada,
    1985, as it read immediately before the coming into force of section
    31 of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property of Her Majesty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident card remains the property of Her
  Majesty in right of Canada at all times and must be returned to the
  Department on the Department's request.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of validity

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  permanent resident card is valid for five years from the date of
  issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident card is valid for one year from
  the date of issue if, at the time of issue, the permanent resident

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is subject to the process set out in paragraph
    46(1)(b) of the Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of a report prepared under
    subsection 44(1) of the Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is subject to a removal order made by the Minister
    under subsection 44(2) of the Act and the period for filing an
    appeal from the decision has not expired or, if an appeal is filed,
    there has been no final determination of the appeal; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of a report referred to the
    Immigration Division under subsection 44(2) of the Act and the
    period for filing an appeal from the decision of the Immigration
    Division has not expired or, if an appeal is filed, there has been
    no final determination of the appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** A permanent resident card
shall only be provided or issued in Canada.

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-188, s.
  1\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for a card

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application for a permanent resident card must be
  made in Canada and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an application form that contains the following
    information, namely,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's name and date and place of birth,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's gender, height and eye colour,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which and the place where the
      applicant became a permanent resident,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's mailing address,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the addresses of all of the applicant's places of
      residence during the previous five years,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the names and addresses of the applicant's
      employers and educational institutions attended, during the
      previous five years,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the periods during the previous five years that
      the applicant was absent from Canada,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-188, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} whether a report under subsection 44(1) of the
      Act has been made in respect of the applicant or whether a
      decision was made outside of Canada that they have failed to
      comply with the residency obligation under section 28 of the Act,
      and

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} whether the applicant has lost their permanent
      resident status or has been issued a removal order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-188, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a copy of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any document described in paragraphs 50(1)(a)
      to (h) --- or, if the applicant does not hold one of those
      documents, any document described in paragraphs 178(1)(a) and (b)
      --- that is currently held by the applicant or was held by the
      applicant at the time they became a permanent resident,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a certificate of identity issued in Canada to
      the applicant by the Minister, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} refugee travel papers issued in Canada to the
      applicant by the Minister;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a copy of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the form IMM1000, entitled "Record of Landing",
      held by the applicant,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a provincial driver's license held by the
      applicant,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a photo-identity card held by the applicant and
      issued by a province,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a student card held by the applicant and issued
      by a provincially accredited college or university, or

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the most recent notice of assessment within the
      meaning of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) received in
      relation to the applicant's income tax return; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} two identical photographs that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} were taken not more than 12 months before the
      application was made,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-188, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} are in black and white or colour on paper,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} show a full front view of the applicant's head
      and shoulders and have a white background,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} have a view of the applicant's head that is at
      least 25 mm (one inch) and at most 35 mm (1.375 inches) in length,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} show the applicant's face unobscured by
      sunglasses or any other object, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} have a dimension of 35 mm (1.375 inches) by 45
      mm (1.75 inches).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-188, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 16
- SOR/2008-188, s. 1
- SOR/2015-46, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-56-20080605.html "Link to previous version of section 56"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicants

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), every
  person who applies for a permanent resident card must make and sign
  the application on their own behalf.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor applicants 14 years of age or
  more

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application of a child who is 14 years of age or
  more but less than 18 years of age must be signed by the applicant and
  one of their parents unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian court has made another person
    responsible for the child, in which case that person must co-sign
    the application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the parents are deceased, in which case the person
    legally responsible for the child must co-sign the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor applicants less than 14 years of
  age

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application of a child who is less than 14 years
  of age must be signed by one of their parents unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian court has made another person
    responsible for the child, in which case that person must sign the
    application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the parents are deceased, in which case the person
    legally responsible for the child must sign the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Providing address within 180 days

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  order to allow the Department to provide a permanent resident card, a
  permanent resident referred to in paragraph 53(1)(a) must provide to
  the Department, within 180 days after the day on which they become a
  permanent resident, their address in Canada and, on the request of an
  officer,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a photograph of the permanent resident that
    satisfies the requirements of subparagraphs 56(2)(e)(i) and (iii) to
    (vii); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the signature of the permanent resident or, if the
    permanent resident is a child less than 14 years of age, the
    signature of one of their parents unless

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian court has made another person
      responsible for the child, in which case the signature of that
      person must be provided, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the parents are deceased, in which case the
      signature of the person legally responsible for the child must be
      provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance after 180 days

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the permanent resident does not comply with
  subsection (1), they must make an application for a permanent resident
  card in accordance with section 56.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance required

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident who applies for a permanent
  resident card under section 56 must, in order to be provided with the
  card, attend at the time and place specified in a notice mailed by the
  Department. If the permanent resident fails to attend within 180 days
  after the Department first mails a notice, the card shall be destroyed
  and the applicant must make a new application in order to be issued a
  permanent resident card.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document verification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When attending in accordance with subsection (3), a
  permanent resident must produce the original documents copies of which
  were included in their application as required by paragraphs 56(2)(c)
  and (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 17
- SOR/2014-139, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-58-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 58"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of new permanent resident card

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer shall, on application, issue a new permanent resident card if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has not lost permanent resident
    status under subsection 46(1) of the Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has not been convicted under section
    123 or 126 of the Act for an offence related to the misuse of a
    permanent resident card, unless a pardon has been granted and has
    not ceased to have effect or been revoked under the [Criminal
    Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the applicant complies with the requirements of
    sections 56 and 57 and subsection 58(4);

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant referred to in
    paragraph 12.1(m) who is 14 years of age or older, the applicant has
    provided their biometric information in support of their
    application; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant returns their last permanent resident
    card, unless the card has been lost, stolen or destroyed, in which
    case the applicant must produce all relevant evidence in accordance
    with subsection 16(1) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of new permanent resident card
  --- effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A previously issued permanent resident card is
  revoked on the issuance of a new permanent resident card.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 18
- SOR/2018-128, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-59-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 59"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** A permanent resident card
is revoked if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the permanent resident becomes a Canadian citizen or
  otherwise loses permanent resident status;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the permanent resident card is lost, stolen or
  destroyed; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the permanent resident is deceased.

### [DIVISION 1.1]{.HLabel2}[Collection and Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686417 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of social insurance number

- **[[60.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may collect the social
  insurance number of a permanent resident card applicant or a travel
  document applicant to verify that the applicant has complied with the
  obligation set out in section 28 of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of social insurance number

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may disclose the social insurance number
  of the applicant to the Canada Revenue Agency for the purpose set out
  in subsection (1) if the Minister has entered into an arrangement with
  the Agency for the disclosure of that information.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-138, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Residency Obligation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686425 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian business

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), for the
  purposes of subparagraphs 28(2)(a)(iii) and (iv) of the Act and of
  this section, a Canadian business is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a corporation that is incorporated under the laws
    of Canada or of a province and that has an ongoing operation in
    Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an enterprise, other than a corporation described
    in paragraph (a), that has an ongoing operation in Canada and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that is capable of generating revenue and is
      carried on in anticipation of profit, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in which a majority of voting or ownership
      interests is held by Canadian citizens, permanent residents, or
      Canadian businesses as defined in this subsection; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an organization or enterprise created under the
    laws of Canada or a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a Canadian business does not
  include a business that serves primarily to allow a permanent resident
  to comply with their residency obligation while residing outside
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employment outside Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs 28(2)(a)(iii)
  and (iv) of the Act, the expression [[employed on a full-time basis by
  a Canadian business or in the public service of Canada or of a
  province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a permanent
  resident, that the permanent resident is an employee of, or under
  contract to provide services to, a Canadian business or the public
  service of Canada or of a province, and is assigned on a full-time
  basis as a term of the employment or contract to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a position outside Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an affiliated enterprise outside Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a client of the Canadian business or the public
    service outside Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying outside Canada

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs 28(2)(a)(ii)
  and (iv) of the Act and this section, a permanent resident is
  accompanying outside Canada a Canadian citizen or another permanent
  resident --- who is their spouse or common-law partner or, in the case
  of a child, their parent --- on each day that the permanent resident
  is ordinarily residing with the Canadian citizen or the other
  permanent resident.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraph 28(2)(a)(iv) of the
  Act, a permanent resident complies with the residency obligation as
  long as the permanent resident they are accompanying complies with
  their residency obligation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Child

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs 28(2)(a)(ii)
  and (iv) of the Act, [[child]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a child who
  is not a spouse or common-law partner and is less than 22 years of
  age.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-290, s. 1(E)
- SOR/2014-133, s. 3
- SOR/2017-60, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-61-20140801.html "Link to previous version of section 61"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation --- residency obligation

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  calculation of days under paragraph 28(2)(a) of the Act in respect of
  a permanent resident does not include any day after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a report is prepared under subsection 44(1) of the
    Act on the ground that the permanent resident has failed to comply
    with the residency obligation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a decision is made outside of Canada that the
    permanent resident has failed to comply with the residency
    obligation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the permanent resident is subsequently determined
  to have complied with the residency obligation, subsection (1) does
  not apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-139, s. 3(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-62-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 62"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Permit Holders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686458 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of permit's validity

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** A temporary resident
permit is valid until any one of the following events occurs:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the permit is cancelled under subsection 24(1) of the
  Act or under section 243.1;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the permit holder leaves Canada without obtaining
  prior authorization to re-enter Canada;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period of validity specified on the permit
  expires; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a period of three years elapses from its date of
  validity.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-11, s. 1](#1441338-1448674){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1441338-1448674.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Permit Holders Class]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686465 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permit holder class

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** The permit holder class
is prescribed as a class of foreign nationals who may become permanent
residents on the basis of the requirements of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 19(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national is a
permit holder and a member of the permit holder class if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have been issued a temporary resident permit
  under subsection 24(1) of the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have continuously resided in Canada as a permit
  holder for a period of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least three years, if they

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} are inadmissible on health grounds under
      subsection 38(1) of the Act,

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} are inadmissible under paragraph 42(1)(a) of the
      Act on grounds of an accompanying family member who is
      inadmissible under subsection 38(1) of the Act, or

    - [(C)]{.lawlabel} are inadmissible under paragraph 42(1)(b) of the
      Act on grounds of being an accompanying family member of a foreign
      national who is inadmissible

      - [(I)]{.lawlabel} under subsection 38(1) of the Act, or

      - [(II)]{.lawlabel} under paragraph 42(1)(a) of the Act on grounds
        of an accompanying family member who is inadmissible under
        subsection 38(1) of the Act,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at least five years, if they are inadmissible on
    any other grounds under the Act, except sections 34 to 35.1 and
    subsections 36(1) and 37(1) of the Act;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} they have not become inadmissible on any ground since
  the permit was issued; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who intends to
  reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of the family
  class or a person whom the Board has determined to be a Convention
  refugee, the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion
  that the foreign national meets the selection criteria of the
  Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 20
- SOR/2014-269, ss. 6, 7
- [2023, c. 19, s. 18](#1411952-1414401){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1411952-1414401.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20141121.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Becoming a permanent resident

- **[[65.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national in Canada who is a permit holder and a member of the
  permit holder class becomes a permanent resident if, following an
  examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have applied to remain in Canada as a
    permanent resident as a member of that class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are in Canada to establish permanent
    residence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they meet the selection criteria and other
    requirements applicable to that class;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they hold

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4), a document described
      in any of paragraphs 50(1)(a) to (h), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a medical certificate --- based on the most
      recent medical examination to which they were required to submit
      under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act and which took place within
      the previous 12 months --- that indicates that their health
      condition is not likely to be a danger to public health or public
      safety and is not reasonably expected to cause excessive demand;
      and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} they and their family members, whether accompanying
    or not, are not inadmissible on any ground other than the grounds on
    which an officer, at the time the permit was issued, formed the
    opinion that the foreign national was inadmissible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria in the Province of Quebec

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(c), the selection
  criterion applicable to a foreign national who intends to reside in
  the Province of Quebec as a permanent resident and who is not a person
  whom the Board has determined to be a Convention refugee is met by
  evidence that the competent authority of that Province is of the
  opinion that the foreign national meets the selection criteria of the
  Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign nationals without a passport or
  other travel document

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following foreign nationals who are not holders
  of a document described in any of paragraphs 50(1)(a) to (h) may
  submit with their application a document described in paragraph
  178(1)(a) or (b):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a protected person within the meaning of subsection
    95(2) of the Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who was determined to be a Convention
    refugee seeking resettlement under the Immigration Regulations,
    1978, as enacted by Order in Council P.C. 1978-486 dated February
    23, 1978 and registered as SOR/78-172, if under the Act or section
    69.2 of the former Act, within the meaning of section 187 of the
    Act,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} no determination has been made to vacate that
      determination, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} no determination has been made that the person
      ceased to be a Convention refugee; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a member of the country of asylum class or the
    source country class under the Humanitarian Designated Classes
    Regulations, as enacted by Order in Council P.C. 1997-477 dated
    April 8, 1997 and registered as SOR/97-183.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative documents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A document submitted under subsection (3) shall be
  accepted in lieu of a document described in any of paragraphs 50(1)(a)
  to (h) if it satisfies the requirements of paragraphs 178(2)(a) or
  (b).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 21
- SOR/2012-154, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 65.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Humanitarian and Compassionate Considerations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686508 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** A request made by a
foreign national under subsection 25(1) of the Act must be made as an
application in writing accompanied by an application to remain in Canada
as a permanent resident or, in the case of a foreign national outside
Canada, an application for a permanent resident visa.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant outside Canada

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** If an exemption from
paragraphs 70(1)(a), (c) and (d) is granted under subsection 25(1),
25.1(1) or 25.2(1) of the Act with respect to a foreign national outside
Canada who has made the applications referred to in section 66, a
permanent resident visa shall be issued to the foreign national if,
following an examination, it is established that the foreign national
meets the requirement set out in paragraph 70(1)(b) and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who intends to
  reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of the family
  class, the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion that
  the foreign national meets the selection criteria of the Province;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not otherwise inadmissible;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the family members of the foreign national, whether
  accompanying or not, are not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 80(F)
- SOR/2010-252, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant in Canada

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** If an exemption from
paragraphs 72(1)(a), (c) and (d) is granted under subsection 25(1),
25.1(1) or 25.2(1) of the Act with respect to a foreign national in
Canada who has made the applications referred to in section 66, the
foreign national becomes a permanent resident if, following an
examination, it is established that the foreign national meets the
requirements set out in paragraphs 72(1)(b) and (e) and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who intends to
  reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of the family
  class or a person whom the Board has determined to be a Convention
  refugee, the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion
  that the foreign national meets the selection criteria of the
  Province;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not otherwise inadmissible;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the family members of the foreign national, whether
  accompanying or not, are not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 22
- SOR/2010-252, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying family member outside Canada

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national who is an accompanying family member of a foreign
  national to whom a permanent resident visa is issued under section 67
  shall be issued a permanent resident visa if, following an
  examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accompanying family member is not inadmissible;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an accompanying family member who
    intends to reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of
    the family class or a person whom the Board has determined to be a
    Convention refugee, the competent authority of that Province is of
    the opinion that the family member meets the selection criteria of
    the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying family member in Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is an accompanying family
  member of a foreign national who becomes a permanent resident under
  section 68 shall become a permanent resident if the accompanying
  family member is in Canada and, following an examination, it is
  established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accompanying family member is not inadmissible;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an accompanying family member who
    intends to reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of
    the family class or a person whom the Board has determined to be a
    Convention refugee, the competent authority of that Province is of
    the opinion that the family member meets the selection criteria of
    the Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 23
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements --- family member

**[[69.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69.1 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
25.1(1), to be considered a family member of the applicant, a person
shall be a family member of an applicant both at the time the
application under section 66 is made and at the time of the
determination of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-133, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Permanent Resident Visa]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686540 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer shall issue a permanent resident visa to a foreign national
  if, following an examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has applied in accordance with
    these Regulations for a permanent resident visa as a member of a
    class referred to in subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is coming to Canada to
    establish permanent residence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is a member of that class;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national meets the selection criteria
    and other requirements applicable to that class; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national and their family members,
    whether accompanying or not, are not inadmissible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The classes are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the family class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the economic class, consisting of the federal
    skilled worker class, the Quebec skilled worker class, the
    provincial nominee class, the Canadian experience class, the federal
    skilled trades class, the Atlantic immigration class, the Quebec
    investor class, the Quebec entrepreneur class, the start-up business
    class, the self-employed persons class and the Quebec self-employed
    persons class; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Convention refugees abroad class and the
    country of asylum class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria in the Province of Quebec

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(d), the selection
  criterion for a foreign national who intends to reside in the Province
  of Quebec as a permanent resident and is not a member of the family
  class is met by evidence that the competent authority of that Province
  is of the opinion that the foreign national complies with the
  provincial selection criteria.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying family members

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is an accompanying family
  member of a foreign national who is issued a permanent resident visa
  shall be issued a permanent resident visa if, following an
  examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accompanying family member is not inadmissible;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a family member who intends to
    reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of the family
    class, the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion
    that the family member complies with the provincial selection
    criteria.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family member

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a permanent resident visa is not issued to a child
  as an accompanying family member of a foreign national or the foreign
  national's spouse or common-law partner, a permanent resident visa
  shall not be issued to a child of that child as an accompanying family
  member of the foreign national.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2003-383, s. 1
- SOR/2008-254, s. 1
- SOR/2011-222, s. 1
- SOR/2012-274, s. 2
- SOR/2016-316, s. 2
- SOR/2018-72, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-174, s. 7](#1135479-1138037){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1135479-1138037.html"}
- [SOR/2021-242, s. 2](#1331467-1335929){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1331467-1335929.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-70-20190603.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance --- particular Quebec selection
cases

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall issue a
permanent resident visa to a foreign national outside Canada who intends
to reside in the Province of Quebec as a permanent resident and does not
satisfy the requirements of paragraphs 70(1)(a), (c) and (d) if,
following an examination, it is established that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has applied for a permanent
  resident visa in accordance with these Regulations, other than
  paragraph 10(2)(c);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national may not be issued a permanent
  resident visa under subsection 176(2) and is not a member of any class
  of persons prescribed by these Regulations who may become permanent
  residents or be issued permanent resident visas;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is named in a *[Certificat de
  sélection du Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}* issued by that Province
  indicating that the foreign national, under the regulations made under
  An Act respecting immigration to Québec, R.S.Q., c. I-0.2, as amended
  from time to time, is a foreign national in a particularly distressful
  situation; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national and their family members,
  whether accompanying or not, are not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 24(F)
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Becoming a Permanent Resident]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686572 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign nationals outside Canada

- **[[71.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national who is a member of a class referred to in subsection
  70(2) and is outside Canada must, to become a permanent resident,
  present their permanent resident visa to an officer at a port of
  entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign nationals in Canada as
  temporary residents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is a member of a class
  referred to in paragraph 70(2)(a) or (b) and who is a temporary
  resident in Canada must, to become a permanent resident, present their
  permanent resident visa to an officer at a port of entry or at an
  office of the Department in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-253, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obtaining status

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national in Canada becomes a permanent resident if, following
  an examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have applied to remain in Canada as a
    permanent resident as a member of a class referred to in subsection
    (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are in Canada to establish permanent
    residence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they are a member of that class;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they meet the selection criteria and other
    requirements applicable to that class;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a foreign national who has
    submitted a document accepted under subsection 178(2) or of a member
    of the protected temporary residents class,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they and their family members, whether
      accompanying or not, are not inadmissible,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} they hold a document described in any of
      paragraphs 50(1)(a) to (h), and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} they hold a medical certificate --- based on
      the most recent medical examination to which they were required to
      submit under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act and which took place
      within the previous 12 months --- that indicates that their health
      condition is not likely to be a danger to public health or public
      safety and, unless subsection 38(2) of the Act applies, is not
      reasonably expected to cause excessive demand; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a member of the protected temporary
    residents class, they are not inadmissible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The classes are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2017-78, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the spouse or common-law partner in Canada class;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the protected temporary residents class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria in the Province of Quebec

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(d), the selection
  criterion applicable to a foreign national who intends to reside in
  the Province of Quebec as a permanent resident, and who is not a
  member of the family class or a person whom the Board has determined
  to be a Convention refugee, is met by evidence that the competent
  authority of that Province is of the opinion that the foreign national
  meets the selection criteria of the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying family members

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is an accompanying family
  member of a foreign national who becomes a permanent resident under
  this section shall be issued a permanent resident visa or become a
  permanent resident, as the case may be, if following an examination it
  is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accompanying family member is not inadmissible;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a family member who intends to
    reside in the Province of Quebec and is not a member of the family
    class or a person whom the Board has determined to be a Convention
    refugee, the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion
    that the family member meets the selection criteria of the Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 26
- SOR/2008-253, s. 5
- SOR/2012-154, s. 6
- SOR/2017-78, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 9]{.HLabel2}[Application to Renounce Permanent Resident Status]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686610 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate application

**[[72.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.5 .sectionLabel}** Despite subsection
10(3), a separate application must be made for each family member that
would like to renounce their permanent resident status.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-269, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- conditions

**[[72.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.6 .sectionLabel}** An officer may
approve a person's application to renounce their permanent resident
status if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has provided evidence of their
  citizenship, nationality or permanent legal resident status in another
  country; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application in respect of a person
  who is less than 18 years of age, the application is signed by every
  person who has custody of that person or who is empowered to act on
  their behalf by virtue of a court order or written agreement or by
  operation of law, unless otherwise ordered by a court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-269, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship application suspended

**[[72.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.7 .sectionLabel}** If a permanent
resident makes an application to renounce their permanent resident
status, any sponsorship application made by them is suspended until a
decision is made on the application to renounce permanent residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-269, s. 2
:::

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[Economic Classes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-686625 .Part}

### [DIVISION 0.1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686626 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements --- family member

**[[72.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.8 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsections 25.1(3) to (5) and for the purposes of this Part, to be
considered a family member of an applicant, a person must be a family
member of the applicant both at the time the application under Division
6 of Part 5 is made and at the time of the determination of the
application.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-133, s. 5
- SOR/2017-78, s. 5

  ajax-after="1331469-1335932.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Skilled Workers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-686631 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686632 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Division.

  [[Atlantic province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Atlantic province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Nova Scotia, New
      Brunswick, Prince Edward Island or Newfoundland and
      Labrador. ([province de l'Atlantique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Canadian educational credential]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Canadian educational credential]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
      secondary school diploma or any post-secondary diploma,
      certificate or credential that is issued on the completion of a
      Canadian program of study or training at an educational or
      training institution that is recognized by the provincial
      authorities responsible for registering, accrediting, supervising
      and regulating such institutions. ([diplôme
      canadien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[educational credential]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[educational credential]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      SOR/2012-274, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

  [[equivalency assessment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[equivalency assessment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      determination, issued by an organization or institution designated
      under subsection 75(4), that a foreign diploma, certificate or
      credential is equivalent to a Canadian educational credential and
      an assessment, by the organization or institution, of the
      authenticity of the foreign diploma, certificate or
      credential. ([attestation d'équivalence]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[former Regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[former Regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in subsection 316(1). ([ancien règlement]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[full-time work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[full-time work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means at least 30 hours of
      work over a period of one week. ([travail à temps
      plein]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[language skill area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[language skill area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means speaking, oral
      comprehension, reading or writing. ([habileté
      langagière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[restricted occupation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[restricted occupation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an occupation
      designated as a restricted occupation by the Minister, taking into
      account labour market activity on both an area and a national
      basis, following consultation with the Department of Employment
      and Social Development, provincial governments and any other
      relevant organizations or institutions. ([profession d'accès
      limité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition
  [[work]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite the definition [[work]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  in section 2, for the purposes of this Division,
  [[work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity for which wages are
  paid or commission is earned.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2003-383, s. 2
- SOR/2008-254, s. 2
- SOR/2010-172, s. 5
- SOR/2010-195, s. 3(F)
- SOR/2012-274, s. 3
- 2013, c. 40, s. 237
- SOR/2016-298, s. 2
- [SOR/2021-242, s. 4](#1331471-1335934){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1331471-1335934.html"}
:::

Version](section-73-20161119.html "Link to previous version of section 73"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686647 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraphs 75(2)(d), 79(3)(a), 87.1(2)(d) and (e),
  87.2(3)(a) and 87.3(2)(f), the Minister shall fix, by class prescribed
  by these Regulations or by occupation, and make available to the
  public, minimum language proficiency thresholds on the basis of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of applications in all classes under
    this Part that are being processed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of immigrants who are projected to
    become permanent residents according to the report to Parliament
    referred to in section 94 of the Act; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the potential, taking into account the applicants'
    linguistic profiles and economic and other relevant factors, for the
    establishment in Canada of applicants under the federal skilled
    worker class, the Canadian experience class, the federal skilled
    trades class and the Atlantic immigration class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum language proficiency thresholds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The minimum language proficiency thresholds fixed by
  the Minister shall be established in reference to the benchmarks
  described in the Canadian Language Benchmarks and the Niveaux de
  compétence linguistique canadiens.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation of language proficiency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may designate, for any period he or she
  specifies, any organization or institution to be responsible for
  evaluating language proficiency and approve the language test to be
  used to evaluate that proficiency if the organization or institution

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has expertise in evaluating language proficiency;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has provided the Minister with an equivalency
    between its language test results and the benchmarks set out in the
    Canadian Language Benchmarks or the [Niveaux de compétence
    linguistique canadiens]{.otherLang lang="fr"}, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall make available to the public the
  names of the designated organizations or institutions and the approved
  language tests.

- Definition [[service agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (6), [[service
  agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement concluded between
  the Government of Canada and an organization or institution for the
  purpose of having the organization or institution supply the service
  of evaluating the language proficiency of foreign nationals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may revoke a designation of an
  organization or institution or the approval of a language test if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution no longer meets the
    criteria set out in subsection (3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution submitted false,
    misleading or inaccurate information or has contravened any
    provision of a federal or provincial law or regulation that is
    relevant to the service provided by the organization or institution;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} either the Government of Canada or the organization
    or institution has terminated the service agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conclusive evidence

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The results of an evaluation of language proficiency
  by a designated organization or institution using an approved language
  test are conclusive evidence of an applicant's language proficiency in
  respect of the federal skilled worker class, the Canadian experience
  class, the federal skilled trades class or the Atlantic immigration
  class, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-274, s. 4
- SOR/2016-298, s. 3
- [SOR/2021-242, s. 5](#1331473-1335939){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1331473-1335939.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20161119.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Federal Skilled Workers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686673 .Subheading}

##### [Federal Skilled Worker Class]{.HTitleText4} {#h-686674 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the federal skilled
  worker class is hereby prescribed as a class of persons who are
  skilled workers and who may become permanent residents on the basis of
  their ability to become economically established in Canada and who
  intend to reside in a province other than the Province of Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Skilled workers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a skilled worker if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within the 10 years before the date on which their
    application for a permanent resident visa is made, they have
    accumulated, over a continuous period, at least one year of
    full-time work experience, or the equivalent in part-time work, in
    the occupation that they identified in their application as their
    primary occupation, other than a restricted occupation, that is
    listed in TEER Category 0, 1, 2 or 3 of the National Occupational
    Classification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed the
    actions described in the lead statement for the occupation as set
    out in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed a
    substantial number of the main duties of the occupation as set out
    in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification, including all of the essential duties;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they have submitted the results of a language test
    that is approved under subsection 74(3), which results must be
    provided by an organization or institution that is designated under
    that subsection, must be less than two years old on the date on
    which their application for a permanent resident visa is made and
    must indicate that they have met or exceeded the applicable language
    proficiency threshold in either English or French that is fixed by
    the Minister under subsection 74(1) for each of the four language
    skill areas; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} they have submitted one of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their Canadian educational credential, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} their foreign diploma, certificate or credential
      and the equivalency assessment, which assessment must be less than
      five years old on the date on which their application is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If professional body designated

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If a professional body has been designated under
  subsection (4) in respect of the occupation identified by the foreign
  national in their application as their primary occupation, the foreign
  diploma, certificate or credential submitted by the foreign national
  must be relevant to that occupation and the equivalency assessment ---
  which must be less than five years old on the date on which their
  application is made and must be issued by the designated professional
  body --- must establish that the foreign diploma, certificate or
  credential is equivalent to the Canadian educational credential
  required to practise that occupation in at least one of the provinces
  in which the equivalency assessments issued by this professional body
  are recognized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimal requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the foreign national fails to meet the
  requirements of subsection (2), the application for a permanent
  resident visa shall be refused and no further assessment is required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation for equivalency assessment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(e) and subsection
  (2.1), the Minister may designate, for a period specified by the
  Minister, any organization or institution to be responsible for
  issuing equivalency assessments

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the organization or institution has the
    recognized expertise to assess the authenticity of foreign diplomas,
    certificates and credentials and their equivalency to Canadian
    educational credentials; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if, in the case of a professional body, its
    equivalency assessments are recognized by at least two provincial
    professional bodies that regulate an occupation listed in TEER
    Category 1, 2 or 3 of the National Occupational Classification for
    which licensing by a provincial regulatory body is required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall make available to the public a
  list of the designated organizations or institutions.

- Definition [[service agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (7), [[service
  agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement concluded between
  the Government of Canada and an organization or institution for the
  purpose of having the organization or institution supply the service
  of assessing the authenticity of foreign diplomas, certificates and
  credentials and their equivalency to Canadian educational credentials.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of designation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may revoke a designation if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution no longer meets the
    criteria set out in subsection (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution submitted false,
    misleading or inaccurate information or has contravened any
    provision of federal or provincial legislation relevant to the
    service provided by the organization or institution; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} either the Government of Canada or the organization
    or institution has terminated the service agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conclusive evidence

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(e), subsection
  (2.1) and section 78, an equivalency assessment is conclusive evidence
  that the foreign diplomas, certificates or credentials are equivalent
  to Canadian educational credentials.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Selection criteria

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of determining whether a skilled worker, as a member of
  the federal skilled worker class, will be able to become economically
  established in Canada, they must be assessed on the basis of the
  following criteria:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker must be awarded not less than
    the minimum number of required points referred to in subsection (2)
    on the basis of the following factors, namely,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} education, in accordance with section 78,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} proficiency in the official languages of Canada,
      in accordance with section 79,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} experience, in accordance with section 80,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} age, in accordance with section 81,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} arranged employment, in accordance with section
      82, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} adaptability, in accordance with section 83; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} have in the form of transferable and available
      funds, unencumbered by debts or other obligations, an amount equal
      to one half of the minimum necessary income applicable in respect
      of the group of persons consisting of the skilled worker and their
      family members, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} be awarded points under paragraph 82(2)(a), (b)
      or (d) for arranged employment, as defined in subsection 82(1), in
      Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of points

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall fix and make available to the
  public the minimum number of points required of a skilled worker, on
  the basis of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of applications by skilled workers as
    members of the federal skilled worker class currently being
    processed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of skilled workers projected to become
    permanent residents according to the report to Parliament referred
    to in section 94 of the Act; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the potential, taking into account economic and
    other relevant factors, for the establishment of skilled workers in
    Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances for officer's substituted
  evaluation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Whether or not the skilled worker has been awarded
  the minimum number of required points referred to in subsection (2),
  an officer may substitute for the criteria set out in paragraph (1)(a)
  their evaluation of the likelihood of the ability of the skilled
  worker to become economically established in Canada if the number of
  points awarded is not a sufficient indicator of whether the skilled
  worker may become economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An evaluation made under subsection (3) requires the
  concurrence of a second officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 28
- SOR/2010-195, s. 4(F)
- SOR/2012-274, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conformity --- applicable times

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of Part
5, the requirements and criteria set out in sections 75 and 76 must be
met on the date on which an application for a permanent resident visa is
made and on the date on which it is issued.

- SOR/2012-274, s. 7

##### [Selection Grid]{.HTitleText4} {#h-686736 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Education (25 points)

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Points
  shall be awarded, to a maximum of 25, for a skilled worker's Canadian
  educational credential or equivalency assessment submitted in support
  of an application, as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 5 points for a secondary school credential;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 15 points for a one-year post-secondary program
    credential;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 19 points for a two-year post-secondary program
    credential;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 21 points for a post-secondary program credential
    of three years or longer;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 22 points for two or more post-secondary program
    credentials, one of which must be a credential issued on completion
    of a post-secondary program of three years or longer;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 23 points for a university-level credential at the
    master's level or an entry-to-practice professional degree for an
    occupation listed in TEER Category 1 of the National Occupational
    Classification for which licensing by a provincial regulatory body
    is required; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} 25 points for a university-level credential at the
    doctoral level.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one educational credential

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), points

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except as set out in paragraph (1)(e), shall not be
    awarded cumulatively on the basis of more than one educational
    credential; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be awarded on the basis of the Canadian
    educational credentials or equivalency assessments submitted in
    support of an application for a permanent resident visa that result
    in the highest number of points.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-195, s. 5(F)
- SOR/2012-274, s. 7
- [SOR/2022-220, s. 3](#1377929-1379297){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1377929-1379297.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-78-20130504.html "Link to previous version of section 78"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  skilled worker must identify in their application for a permanent
  resident visa which language --- English or French --- is to be
  considered their first official language in Canada. They must have
  their proficiency in that language evaluated by an organization or
  institution that is designated under subsection 74(3) using a language
  test that is approved under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proficiency in second language

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the skilled worker wishes to claim points for
  proficiency in their second official language, they must submit, in
  support of the application for a permanent resident visa, the results
  of a language test that is approved under subsection 74(3) , which
  results must be provided by an organization or institution that is
  designated under that subsection and must be less than two years old
  on the date on which their application is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proficiency in English and French (28
  points)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Points for proficiency in the official languages of
  Canada shall be awarded up to a maximum of 24 points for the skilled
  worker's first official language and a maximum of 4 points for the
  applicant's second official language based on benchmarks set out in
  Canadian Language Benchmarks and the Niveaux de compétence
  linguistique canadiens, as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the four language skill areas in the skilled
    worker's first official language,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 4 points per language skill area if the skilled
      worker's proficiency meets the threshold fixed by the Minister
      under subsection 74(1) for that language skill area,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 5 points per language skill area if the skilled
      worker's proficiency exceeds the threshold fixed by the Minister
      under subsection 74(1) for that language skill area by one
      benchmark level, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} 6 points per language skill area if the skilled
      worker's proficiency exceeds the threshold fixed by the Minister
      under subsection 74(1) for that language skill area by at least
      two benchmark levels; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the four language skill areas in the skilled
    worker's second official language, 4 points if the skilled worker's
    proficiency in that language meets or exceeds benchmark level 5 in
    each of the four language skill areas.

::: HistoricalNote

:::

Version](section-79-20130504.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Experience (15 points)

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Points
  shall be awarded, up to a maximum of 15 points, to a skilled worker
  for full-time work experience, or the equivalent in part-time work,
  within the 10 years before the date on which their application is
  made, as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 9 points for one year of work experience;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 11 points for two to three years of work
    experience;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 13 points for four to five years of work
    experience; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 15 points for six or more years of work experience.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Listed occupation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), points are
  awarded for work experience in occupations, other than a restricted
  occupation, that are listed in TEER Category 0, 1, 2 or 3 of the
  National Occupational Classification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Occupational experience

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a skilled worker
  is considered to have experience in an occupation, regardless of
  whether they meet the employment requirements of the occupation as set
  out in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
  Classification, if they performed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the actions described in the lead statement for the
    occupation as set out in the occupational descriptions of the
    National Occupational Classification; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least a substantial number of the main duties of
    the occupation as set out in the occupational descriptions of the
    National Occupational Classification, including all the essential
    duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work in excess

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A period of work experience in one occupation that
  exceeds 30 hours per week, or simultaneous periods of work experience
  in more than one full-time occupation, is to be evaluated as a single
  period of full-time work experience in a single occupation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification code

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A skilled worker must specify in their application
  for a permanent resident visa the five-digit code in the National
  Occupational Classification that corresponds to each of the
  occupations that is engaged in by the applicant and that constitutes
  the skilled worker's work experience.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer's duty

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An officer is not required to consider occupations
  that have not been specified in the application.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-274, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-195, s. 7
- SOR/2012-274, s. 9

  ajax-after="1377931-1379302.html"}

Version](section-80-20221116.html "Link to previous version of section 80"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age (12 points)

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** Points shall be awarded,
up to a maximum of 12, for a skilled worker's age on the date on which
their application is made, as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} 12 points for a skilled worker 18 years of age or
  older but less than 36 years of age;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 11 points for a skilled worker 36 years of age;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} 10 points for a skilled worker 37 years of age;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} 9 points for a skilled worker 38 years of age;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} 8 points for a skilled worker 39 years of age;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} 7 points for a skilled worker 40 years of age;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} 6 points for a skilled worker 41 years of age;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} 5 points for a skilled worker 42 years of age;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} 4 points for a skilled worker 43 years of age;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} 3 points for a skilled worker 44 years of age;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} 2 points for a skilled worker 45 years of age;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} 1 point for a skilled worker 46 years of age; and

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} 0 points for a skilled worker under 18 years of age
  or 47 years of age or older.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-274, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[arranged
employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[arranged employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
  offer of employment that is made by a single employer other than an
  embassy, high commission or consulate in Canada or an employer who is
  referred to in subparagraphs 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii), that is for
  continuous full-time work in Canada having a duration of at least one
  year after the date on which a permanent resident visa is issued and
  that is with respect to an occupation that is listed in TEER Category
  0, 1, 2 or 3 of the National Occupational Classification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arranged employment (10 points)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Ten points shall be awarded to a skilled worker for
  arranged employment if they are able to perform and are likely to
  accept and carry out the employment and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker is in Canada and holds a work
    permit that is valid on the date on which their application for a
    permanent resident visa is made and, on the date on which the visa
    is issued, holds a valid work permit or is authorized to work in
    Canada under section 186 and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the work permit was issued based on a positive
      determination made by an officer under subsection 203(1) with
      respect to the skilled worker's employment with their current
      employer in an occupation that is listed in TEER Category 0, 1, 2
      or 3 of the National Occupational Classification and the
      assessment by the Department of Employment and Social Development
      on the basis of which the determination was made is not suspended
      or revoked,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker is working for an employer
      specified on the work permit, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} that employer has offered arranged employment
      to the skilled worker;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker is in Canada and holds a work
    permit that was issued under the circumstances described in
    paragraph 204(a) or (c) or in section 205 and is valid on the date
    on which their application for a permanent resident visa is made
    and, on the date on which the visa is issued, holds a valid work
    permit or is authorized to work in Canada under section 186 and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker is working for an employer
      specified on the work permit,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that employer has offered an arranged employment
      to the skilled worker, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker has accumulated at least one
      year of full-time work experience, or the equivalent in part-time
      work, over a continuous period of work for that employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker does not hold a valid work
    permit, is not authorized to work in Canada under section 186 on the
    date on which their application for a permanent resident visa is
    made and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an employer has offered arranged employment to
      the skilled worker, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an officer has approved the offer of employment
      based on a valid assessment --- provided to the officer by the
      Department of Employment and Social Development, on the same basis
      as an assessment provided for the issuance of a work permit, at
      the request of the employer or an officer --- that the
      requirements set out in subsection 203(1) with respect to the
      offer have been met; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} on the date on which their application for a
    permanent resident visa is made and on the date on which the visa is
    issued, the skilled worker holds a valid work permit or is
    authorized to work in Canada under section 186 and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in subparagraph
      (a)(ii) or (iii) do not apply,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in paragraph (b)
      do not apply, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in subparagraphs
      (c)(i) and (ii) apply.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adaptability (10 points)

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  maximum of 10 points for adaptability shall be awarded to a skilled
  worker on the basis of any combination of the following elements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the language proficiency of the skilled
    worker's accompanying spouse or common-law partner, other than a
    permanent resident residing in Canada or a Canadian citizen, in
    either official language, evaluated at at least benchmark level 4
    for each of the four language skill areas, as set out in the
    Canadian Language Benchmarks or the [Niveaux de compétence
    linguistique canadiens]{.otherLang lang="fr"}, and demonstrated by
    the results of a language test that is approved under subsection
    74(3) from an organization or institution designated under that
    subsection, which results must be less than two years old on the
    date on which the application for a permanent resident visa is made,
    5 points;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a period of full-time study in Canada by the
    skilled worker of at least two academic years in a program of at
    least two years in duration whether or not they obtained an
    educational credential for completing the program and during which
    period they remained in good academic standing as defined by the
    institution, 5 points;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} for a period of full-time study in Canada by the
    skilled worker's accompanying spouse or common-law partner, other
    than a permanent resident residing in Canada or a Canadian citizen,
    of at least two academic years in a program of at least two years in
    duration whether or not the accompanying spouse or common-law
    partner obtained an educational credential for completing the
    program, and during which period the accompanying spouse or
    common-law partner remained in good academic standing as defined by
    the institution, 5 points;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for any previous period of full-time work under a
    work permit or authorized under section 186 of at least one year in
    Canada by the skilled worker in an occupation that is listed in TEER
    Category 0, 1, 2 or 3 of the National Occupational Classification,
    10 points;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} for any previous period of full-time work under a
    work permit or authorized under section 186 of at least one year in
    Canada by the skilled worker's accompanying spouse or common-law
    partner, other than a permanent resident residing in Canada or a
    Canadian citizen, 5 points;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for being related to, or for having an accompanying
    spouse or accompanying common-law partner who is related to, a
    person living in Canada who is described in subsection (5), 5
    points; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for being awarded points for arranged employment in
    Canada under subsection 82(2), 5 points.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full-time study

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (1)(b) and (b.1),
  full-time study means at least 15 hours of instruction per week during
  the academic year, authorized under a study permit or under section
  188, at a secondary or post-secondary institution in Canada that is
  recognized by the provincial authorities responsible for registering,
  accrediting, supervising and regulating such institutions, including
  any period of training in the workplace that forms part of the course
  of instruction.

- [(3) and (4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-274, s.
  12\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family relationships in Canada

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(d), a skilled
  worker shall be awarded 5 points if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the skilled worker or the skilled worker's
    accompanying spouse or accompanying common-law partner is related by
    blood, marriage, common-law partnership or adoption to a person who
    is 18 years or older, a Canadian citizen or permanent resident
    living in Canada and who is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their father or mother,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the father or mother of their father or mother,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} their child,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a child of their child,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a child of their father or mother,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a child of the father or mother of their father
      or mother, other than their father or mother, or

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a child of the child of their father or mother.

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-274, s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent resident status

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national who is
an accompanying family member of a person who makes an application as a
member of the federal skilled worker class shall become a permanent
resident if, following an examination, it is established that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who made the application has become a
  permanent resident; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-202, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}

#### [Quebec Skilled Worker Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686893 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the Quebec skilled worker
  class is hereby prescribed as a class of persons who may become
  permanent residents on the basis of their ability to become
  economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of the class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Quebec skilled
  worker class if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intend to reside in the Province of Quebec; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are named in a *[Certificat de sélection du
    Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}* issued to them by that Province.

- [(3) and (4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-253, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for accompanying family
  members

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is an accompanying family
  member of a person who makes an application as a member of the Quebec
  skilled worker class shall become a permanent resident if, following
  an examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who made the application has become a
    permanent resident; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 80(F)
- SOR/2008-202, s. 3
- SOR/2008-253, s. 9
:::
