#### [Provincial Nominee Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686908 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the provincial nominee
  class is hereby prescribed as a class of persons who may become
  permanent residents on the basis of their ability to become
  economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of the class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the provincial
  nominee class if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (5), they are named in a
    nomination certificate issued by the government of a province under
    a provincial nomination agreement between that province and the
    Minister; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they intend to reside in the province that has
    nominated them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution of evaluation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the fact that the foreign national is named in a
  certificate referred to in paragraph (2)(a) is not a sufficient
  indicator of whether they may become economically established in
  Canada and an officer has consulted the government that issued the
  certificate, the officer may substitute for the criteria set out in
  subsection (2) their evaluation of the likelihood of the ability of
  the foreign national to become economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An evaluation made under subsection (3) requires the
  concurrence of a second officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), a foreign national who is
  named in a certificate referred to in paragraph (2)(a) shall not be
  considered a member of the provincial nominee class if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nomination was based on the provision of
    capital by the foreign national; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national intends to participate in, or
    has participated in, an immigration-linked investment scheme.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (5) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the capital is provided by the foreign national to
    a business in the province that nominated them, other than a
    business operated primarily for the purpose of deriving investment
    income such as interest, dividends or capital gains;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national controls or will control

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a percentage of equity in the business equal to
      or greater than 33 1/3 per cent, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an equity investment in the business of at least
      \$1,000,000;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national provides or will provide
    active and ongoing management of the business from within the
    province that nominated them; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the terms of the investment in the business do not
    include a redemption option.

- [(7) and (8)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-253, s.
  10\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[immigration-linked investment scheme]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[immigration-linked investment scheme]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      a strategy or plan

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where one of the objectives of the strategy or
        plan is to facilitate immigration to Canada and one of the
        objectives of the promoters of the strategy or plan is to raise
        capital; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the agreement or arrangement in respect
        of the strategy or plan was entered into primarily for the
        purpose of acquiring a status or privilege under the
        Act. ([projet de placement lié à l'immigration]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[percentage of equity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[percentage of equity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a sole proprietorship, 100% of
        the equity of the sole proprietorship controlled by a foreign
        national or their spouse or common-law partner;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a corporation, the percentage of
        the issued and outstanding voting shares of the capital stock of
        the corporation controlled by a foreign national or their spouse
        or common-law partner; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a partnership or joint venture,
        the percentage of the profit or loss of the partnership or joint
        venture to which a foreign national or their spouse or
        common-law partner is entitled. ([pourcentage des capitaux
        propres]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (5), (6) and (9) do not apply in respect
  of a foreign national who is issued a nomination certificate referred
  to in paragraph (2)(a) before September 2, 2008.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (5) and (6) as they read immediately
  before September 2, 2008 apply in respect of a foreign national
  referred to in subsection (10).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for accompanying family
  members

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is an accompanying family
  member of a person who makes an application as a member of the
  provincial nominee class shall become a permanent resident if,
  following an examination, it is established that

  - [a)]{.lawlabel} the person who made the application has become a
    permanent resident; and

  - [b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not inadmissible.

#### [Canadian Experience Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686952 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[87.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 12(2)
  of the Act, the Canadian experience class is prescribed as a class of
  persons who may become permanent residents on the basis of their
  ability to become economically established in Canada, their experience
  in Canada, and their intention to reside in a province other than the
  Province of Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of the class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Canadian
  experience class if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have acquired in Canada, within the three
    years before the date on which their application for permanent
    residence is made, at least one year of full-time work experience,
    or the equivalent in part-time work experience, in one or more
    occupations, other than a restricted occupation, that are listed in
    TEER Category 0, 1, 2 or 3 of the National Occupational
    Classification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed the
    actions described in the lead statement for the occupation as set
    out in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed a
    substantial number of the main duties of the occupation as set out
    in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification, including all of the essential duties;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they have had their proficiency in the English or
    French language evaluated by an organization or institution that is
    designated under subsection 74(3) using a language test that is
    approved under that subsection, the results of which must indicate
    that the foreign national has met the applicable threshold that is
    fixed by the Minister under subsection 74(1) for each of the four
    language skill areas; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case where they have acquired the work
    experience referred to in paragraph (a) in more than one occupation,
    they meet the threshold for proficiency in the English or French
    language, fixed by the Minister under subsection 74(1), for the
    occupation in which they have acquired the greater amount of work
    experience in the three years referred to in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any period of employment during which the foreign
    national was engaged in full-time study shall not be included in
    calculating a period of work experience;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any period of self-employment or unauthorized work
    shall not be included in calculating a period of work experience;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national must have had temporary
    resident status during their period of work experience.

  - [(d) to (g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-274, s.
    13\]]{.Repealed}

- [(4) and (5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-274, s.
  13\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Federal Skilled Trades Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-686972 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[skilled trade
occupation]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[87.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[skilled trade occupation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
  an occupation, other than a restricted occupation, in any of the
  following groups listed in the National Occupational Classification:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 72, technical trades and transportation
    officers and controllers, excluding Sub-major Group 726,
    transportation officers and controllers;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 73, general trades;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 82, supervisors in natural resources,
    agriculture and related production;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 83, occupations in natural resources
    and related production;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 92, processing, manufacturing and
    utilities supervisors and utilities operators and controllers;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} Major Group 93, central control and process
    operators and aircraft assembly assemblers and inspectors, excluding
    Sub-major Group 932, aircraft assemblers and aircraft assembly
    inspectors;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} Minor Group 6320, cooks, butchers and bakers; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} Unit Group 62200, chefs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the
  federal skilled trades class is prescribed as a class of persons who
  are skilled trades workers and who may become permanent residents on
  the basis of their ability to become economically established in
  Canada in a skilled trade occupation and their intention to reside in
  a province other than the Province of Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the federal skilled
  trades class if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have had their proficiency in the English or
    French language evaluated by an organization or institution that is
    designated under subsection 74(3) using a language test that is
    approved under that subsection, the results of which must indicate
    that the foreign national has met the applicable threshold that is
    fixed by the Minister under subsection 74(1) for each of the four
    language skill areas;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have, during the five years before the date on
    which their permanent resident visa application is made, acquired at
    least two years of full-time work experience, or the equivalent in
    part-time work, in the skilled trade occupation specified in the
    application after becoming qualified to independently practice the
    occupation, and during that period of employment has performed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the actions described in the lead statement for
      the occupation as set out in the occupational descriptions of the
      National Occupational Classification, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a substantial number of the main duties listed
      in the description of the occupation set out in the National
      Occupational Classification, including all of the essential
      duties;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they have met the relevant employment requirements
    of the skilled trade occupation specified in the application as set
    out in the National Occupational Classification, except for the
    requirement to obtain a certificate of qualification issued by a
    competent provincial authority; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they meet at least one of the following
    requirements:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they hold a certificate of qualification issued
      by a competent provincial or federal authority in the skilled
      trade occupation specified in the application for a permanent
      resident visa,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} they are in Canada and hold a work permit that
      is valid on the date on which their application for a permanent
      resident visa is made and, on the date on which the visa is
      issued, hold a valid work permit or are authorized to work in
      Canada under section 186 and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the work permit was issued based on a positive
        determination made by an officer under subsection 203(1) with
        respect to their employment with their current employer in a
        skilled trade occupation and the assessment by the Department of
        Employment and Social Development on the basis of which the
        determination was made is not suspended or revoked,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} they are working for an employer specified on
        the work permit,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} they have an offer of employment that is for
        continuous full-time work having a total duration of at least
        one year after the date on which a permanent resident visa is
        issued and that is in the skilled trade occupation that is
        specified in the application and is in the same minor group set
        out in the National Occupational Classification as the
        occupation specified on their work permit, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} the offer is made by up to two employers who
        are specified on the work permit, none of whom is an embassy,
        high commission or consulate in Canada or an employer who is
        referred to in subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} they are in Canada and hold a work permit that
      was issued under the circumstances described in paragraph 204(a)
      or (c) or in section 205 and that is valid on the date on which
      their application for a permanent resident visa is made and, on
      the date on which the visa is issued, hold a valid work permit or
      are authorized to work in Canada under section 186 and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} they are working for an employer specified on
        the work permit,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} they have an offer of employment that is for
        continuous full-time work having a total duration of at least
        one year after the date on which a permanent resident visa is
        issued and that is in the skilled trade occupation that is
        specified in the application and is in the same minor group set
        out in the National Occupational Classification as the
        occupation specified on their work permit,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the offer is made by up to two employers who
        are specified on the work permit, none of whom is an embassy,
        high commission or consulate in Canada or an employer who is
        referred to in subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii), and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} they have accumulated at least one year of
        full-time work experience, or the equivalent in part-time work,
        over a continuous period of work for the employers who made the
        offer,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} they do not hold a valid work permit, are not
      authorized to work in Canada under section 186 on the date on
      which their application for a permanent resident visa is made and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} they have an offer of employment that is for
        continuous full-time work having a total duration of at least
        one year after the date on which a permanent resident visa is
        issued and that is in the skilled trade occupation specified in
        the application,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the offer is made by up to two employers, none
        of whom is an embassy, high commission or consulate in Canada or
        an employer who is referred to in subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or
        (iii), and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} an officer has approved the offer of employment
        based on a valid assessment --- provided to the officer by the
        Department of Employment and Social Development, on the same
        basis as an assessment provided for the issuance of a work
        permit, at the request of up to two employers or an officer ---
        that the requirements set out in subsection 203(1) with respect
        to the offer have been met, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} on the date on which their application for a
      permanent resident visa is made and on the date on which it is
      issued, they either hold a valid work permit or are authorized to
      work in Canada under section 186 and

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in clause (ii)(B)
        or (C) do not apply,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in
        subparagraph (iii) do not apply, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in clauses
        (iv)(A), (B) and (C) apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution of officer's evaluation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the requirements referred to in subsection (3),
  whether or not they are met, are not sufficient indicators of whether
  the foreign national will become economically established in Canada,
  an officer may substitute their evaluation for the requirements. This
  decision requires the concurrence of another officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for funds

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} With the exception of the foreign nationals referred
  to in subparagraphs (3)(d)(ii), (iii) and (v), the foreign national
  must have, in the form of transferable and available funds,
  unencumbered by debts or other obligations, an amount equal to one
  half of the minimum necessary income applicable in respect of the
  group of persons consisting of the skilled trades worker and their
  family members.

#### [Atlantic Immigration Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1335945 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[87.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 12(2)
  of the Act, the Atlantic immigration class is prescribed as a class of
  persons who may become permanent residents on the basis of their
  ability to become economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Atlantic
  immigration class if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are named in an endorsement certificate issued by
    the government of an Atlantic province --- on or after the day on
    which this section comes into force --- under an Atlantic
    immigration agreement between that province and the Minister, which
    must be valid on the date on which their application for a permanent
    resident visa is made and must not subsequently have been revoked;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} intend to reside in the Atlantic province that
    issued the endorsement certificate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} meet either the work experience requirements set
    out in subsections (3) and (4) or the recent graduate requirements
    set out in subsection (5);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} have an offer of employment that meets the
    requirements set out in subsections (6) and (7);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} meet the educational requirements set out in
    subsection (9);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} have had their proficiency in the English or French
    language evaluated by an organization or institution that is
    designated under subsection 74(3) using a language test that is
    approved under that subsection, the results of which must be less
    than two years old on the date on which their application for a
    permanent resident visa is made and must indicate that the foreign
    national has met the applicable threshold that is fixed by the
    Minister under subsection 74(1) for each of the four language skill
    areas; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} have --- unless they are authorized to work and
    already working in Canada --- in the form of transferable and
    available funds, unencumbered by debts or other obligations, an
    amount equal to one eighth of the amount identified, in the most
    recent edition of the publication concerning low-income cut-offs
    published annually by Statistics Canada under the [Statistics
    Act](/eng/acts/S-19), for urban areas of residence of 500,000
    persons or more, as the minimum amount of before-tax annual income
    that is necessary to support a group of persons equal in number to
    the total number of the applicant and their family members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work experience

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national meets the work experience
  requirements if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have acquired, within the five years before
    the date on which their application for a permanent resident visa is
    made, at least one year of full-time work experience, or the
    equivalent in part-time work experience, in at least one of the
    occupations, other than a restricted occupation, that are listed in
    TEER Category 0, 1, 2, 3 or 4 of the National Occupational
    Classification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed the
    actions described in the lead statement for the occupation as set
    out in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} during that period of employment they performed a
    substantial number of the main duties of the occupation as set out
    in the occupational descriptions of the National Occupational
    Classification, including all of the essential duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded work experience

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (3)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any period of self-employment is not included in
    calculating a period of work experience; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any period of work acquired in Canada is not
    included in calculating a period of work experience, unless the
    foreign national was authorized to work and had temporary resident
    status during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recent graduate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national meets the recent graduate
  requirements if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtained, as a full-time student, within the two
    years before the date on which their application for a permanent
    resident visa is made, a post-secondary educational credential
    issued by an institution in an Atlantic province that is set out in
    the document entitled Recognized Post-Secondary Institutions --
    Atlantic Immigration Program, published by the Minister, as amended
    from time to time, on the completion of a program of study that was

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} of at least two years in duration,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not devoted primarily to the study of English or
      French as a second language, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} undertaken primarily as in-person learning;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} were physically present in the Atlantic province
    for at least 16 months during the two years preceding the day on
    which that credential was issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} had temporary resident status for the entire period
    of study working toward the credential and any work or study
    completed during that period was authorized; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} were not in receipt of a scholarship or fellowship
    that was conditional on their return to another country at the
    conclusion of their studies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer of employment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The offer of employment must meet the following
  requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must be for continuous full-time work having a
    duration that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} indeterminate, in the case of an offer for
      employment in an occupation that is listed in TEER Category 4 of
      the National Occupational Classification, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} of at least one year after the date on which a
      permanent resident visa is issued, in any other case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it must be for employment that the foreign national
    is able to perform and is likely to accept and carry out;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it must be made by the employer named in the
    endorsement certificate, and that employer must not be referred to
    in any of subparagraphs 200(3)(h)(i) to (iii) or more than 50% owned
    or controlled, directly or indirectly, by the foreign national or
    their spouse or common-law partner; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it must be for employment in an occupation that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is listed in TEER Category 0, 1, 2, 3 or 4 of the
      National Occupational Classification, in the case of a foreign
      national who meets the recent graduate requirements,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} corresponds to unit group 33102 or 44101 of the
      National Occupational Classification, in the case of a foreign
      national who meets the work experience requirements set out in
      paragraph (3)(a) based on experience in an occupation that
      corresponds to unit group 31301 or 32101 of the National
      Occupational Classification, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is listed in a TEER category in the National
      Occupational Classification that is at the same level or a higher
      level than the occupation in which most of the work experience
      relied on for the purpose of paragraph (3)(a) was acquired, in any
      other case.

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-220, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}TEER category hierarchy

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraph (6)(d)(iii),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} TEER Category 0 in the National Occupational
    Classification is at a higher level than TEER Category 1;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} TEER Category 1 is at a higher level than TEER
    Categories 2 and 3;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} TEER Categories 2 and 3 are at the same level; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} TEER Categories 2 and 3 are at a higher level than
    TEER Category 4.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-220, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Educational requirements

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national meets the educational requirements
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who has an offer
    of employment in an occupation that is listed in TEER Category 0 or
    1 of the National Occupational Classification, they hold a
    post-secondary Canadian educational credential from a program of
    study of a duration of at least one year or a foreign diploma,
    certificate or credential supported by an equivalency assessment ---
    which must be less than five years old on the date on which their
    application is made --- establishing that the foreign diploma,
    certificate or credential is equivalent to such a Canadian
    educational credential; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who has an offer
    of employment in an occupation that is listed in TEER Category 2, 3
    or 4 of the National Occupational Classification, they hold a
    Canadian educational credential or a foreign diploma, certificate or
    credential supported by an equivalency assessment, which must be
    less than five years old on the date on which their application is
    made.

::: HistoricalNote

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Business Immigrants]{.HTitleText2} {#h-687017 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687018 .Subheading}

  definitions in this subsection apply in this Division.

  [[qualifying business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[qualifying business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,

  [[qualifying Canadian business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [[relevant experience]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[relevant experience]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a self-employed person, other than a
        self-employed person selected by a province, means a minimum of
        two years of experience, during the period beginning five years
        before the date of application for a permanent resident visa and
        ending on the day a determination is made in respect of the
        application, consisting of

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of cultural activities,

          - [(A)]{.lawlabel} two one-year periods of experience in
            self-employment in cultural activities,

          - [(B)]{.lawlabel} two one-year periods of experience in
            participation at a world class level in cultural activities,
            or

          - [(C)]{.lawlabel} a combination of a one-year period of
            experience described in clause (A) and a one-year period of
            experience described in clause (B),

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of athletics,

          - [(A)]{.lawlabel} two one-year periods of experience in
            self-employment in athletics,

          - [(B)]{.lawlabel} two one-year periods of experience in
            participation at a world class level in athletics, or

          - [(C)]{.lawlabel} a combination of a one-year period of
            experience described in clause (A) and a one-year period of
            experience described in clause (B), and

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the purchase and management
          of a farm, two one-year periods of experience in the
          management of a farm; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a self-employed person selected by a province,
        has the meaning provided by the laws of the
        province. ([expérience utile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[self-employed person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[self-employed person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a foreign
      national who has relevant experience and has the intention and
      ability to be self-employed in Canada and to make a significant
      contribution to specified economic activities in
      Canada. ([travailleur autonome]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[self-employed person selected by a province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[self-employed person selected by a
      province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, SOR/2016-316, s.
      4\]]{.Repealed}

  [[specified economic activities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[specified economic activities]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect
      of

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a self-employed person, other than a
        self-employed person selected by a province, means cultural
        activities, athletics or the purchase and management of a farm;
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a self-employed person selected by a province,
        has the meaning provided by the laws of the
        province. ([activités économiques déterminées]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- [(2) and (3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2016-316, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687064 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Artificial transactions

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Division, an applicant in the self-employed persons class or an
applicant in the start-up business class is not considered to have met
the applicable requirements of this Division if the fulfillment of those
requirements is based on one or more transactions that were entered into
primarily for the purpose of acquiring a status or privilege under the
Act rather than

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant in the self-employed
  class, for the purpose of self-employment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant in the start-up business
  class, for the purpose of engaging in the business activity for which
  a commitment referred to in paragraph 98.01(2)(a) was intended.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-316, s. 5
- SOR/2018-72, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-89-20161216.html "Link to previous version of section 89"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent resident status

**[[89.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.1 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national
who is an accompanying family member of a person who makes an
application as a member of the Quebec investor class, the Quebec
entrepreneur class, the start-up business class, the self-employed
persons class or the Quebec self-employed persons class shall become a
permanent resident if, following an examination, it is established that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who made the application has become a
  permanent resident; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is not inadmissible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 2
:::

#### [Quebec Investor Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687077 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the Quebec investor class
  is prescribed as a class of persons who may become permanent residents
  on the basis of their ability to become economically established in
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Quebec investor
  class if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intend to reside in Quebec; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are named in a Certificat de sélection du Québec
    issued by Quebec.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-316, s. 5
:::

#### [Quebec Entrepreneur Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687093 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the Quebec entrepreneur
  class is prescribed as a class of persons who may become permanent
  residents on the basis of their ability to become economically
  established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Quebec
  entrepreneur class if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intend to reside in Quebec; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are named in a [Certificat de sélection du
    Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"} issued by Quebec.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-316, s. 6
:::

Version](section-97-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 97"){.PITLink}

#### [Start-up Business Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687104 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[98.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 12(2)
  of the Act, the start-up business class is prescribed as a class of
  persons who may become permanent residents on the basis of their
  ability to become economically established in Canada, who meet the
  requirements of subsection (2) and who intend to reside in a province
  other than Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the start-up
  business class if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have obtained a commitment that is made by one
    or more entities designated under subsection 98.03(1), that is less
    than six months old on the date on which their application for a
    permanent resident visa is made and that meets the requirements of
    section 98.04;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have submitted the results of a language test
    that is approved under subsection 102.3(4), which results must be
    provided by an organization or institution that is designated under
    that subsection, be less than two years old on the date on which
    their application for a permanent resident visa is made and indicate
    that the foreign national has met at least benchmark level 5 in
    either official language for all four language skill areas, as set
    out in the Canadian Language Benchmarks or the [Niveaux de
    compétence linguistique canadiens]{.otherLang lang="fr"}, as
    applicable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they have, excluding any investment made by a
    designated entity into their business, transferable and available
    funds unencumbered by debts or other obligations of an amount that
    is equal to one half of the amount identified, in the most recent
    edition of the publication concerning low income cut-offs published
    annually by Statistics Canada under the [Statistics
    Act](/eng/acts/S-19), for urban areas of residence of 500,000
    persons or more, as the minimum amount of before-tax annual income
    that is necessary to support a group of persons equal in number to
    the total number of the applicant and their family members; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they have started a qualifying business within the
    meaning of section 98.06.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Size

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No more than five applicants are to be considered
  members of the start-up business class in respect of the same
  business.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements with organizations

- **[[98.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may enter into an
  agreement with an organization to provide for any matter related to
  the start-up business class, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the making of recommendations and the provision of
    advice to the Minister on the designation of an entity and on the
    revocation of those designations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of criteria, standards of conduct
    and best practices for the making of commitments or the performance
    of other activities related to the start-up business class by an
    entity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the making of recommendations and the provision of
    advice to the Minister on the operation of these Regulations with
    respect to the start-up business class;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of peer review panels referred to
    in section 98.09; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the submission of reports to the Minister on the
    activities of designated entities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In order to exercise the functions referred to in
  paragraphs (1)(a), (b), (d) and (e), the organization must have
  expertise in relation to the type of entity in question, namely,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} business incubators;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} angel investor groups; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} venture capital funds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An organization may exercise the functions referred
  to in subsection (1) only when the following conditions apply:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the organization is in compliance with the
    agreement and the agreement remains in force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections 98.12(2) and 98.13(4), the
    organization complies with requirements imposed under subsection
    98.12(1) and paragraphs 98.13(2)(b), (c), and (f) and requests made
    under subsection 98.13(3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the organization is in compliance with these
    Regulations; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the organization has expertise in relation to at
    least one of the entity types referred to in paragraphs (2)(a) to
    (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[98.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must designate the
  entities referred to in subsection 98.01(2) according to the following
  categories:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} business incubators;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} angel investor groups; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} venture capital funds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may only designate an entity if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is recognized for its expertise in assessing the
    potential for and assisting in the success of start-up business
    opportunities in Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it has the ability to assess the potential for and
    assist in the success of start-up business opportunities in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A designated entity must respect the following
  conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must continue to meet the requirements of
    subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it must enter only into commitments that respect
    these Regulations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it must provide the Minister upon request with
    information on its activities related to the start-up business
    class, including information on foreign nationals with whom it has
    made commitments and the businesses referred to in those
    commitments;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it must, subject to subsections 98.12(2) and
    98.13(4), comply with requirements imposed under subsection 98.12(1)
    and paragraphs 98.13(2)(b), (c) and (f) and requests made under
    subsection 98.13(3);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} it must comply with the terms of its commitments
    and with these Regulations; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} it must comply with any federal or provincial law
    or regulation relevant to the service it provides.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is reason to suspect that an entity does not
  meet the conditions or has submitted false, misleading or inaccurate
  information to the Minister, the Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} suspend the entity's ability to make commitments;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} refuse to consider applications associated with
    commitments made by that entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An action taken under subsection (4) comes into force
  on the day on which the Minister issues a notice of the action to the
  entity and remains in effect until the earlier of the day on which the
  situation is resolved and the day that is nine months after the day on
  which the notice is issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If there are reasonable grounds to believe that an
  entity does not meet the conditions set out in subsection (3) or has
  submitted false, misleading or inaccurate information to the Minister,
  the Minister may revoke the entity's designation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice\

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish on the Department's website
  a list of all entities that are designated and those that are
  suspended under subsection (4).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of commitment

- **[[98.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A commitment must be in a written or
  electronic form that is acceptable to the Minister and must be
  provided by a person who has the authority to bind the designated
  entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No fee for commitment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A commitment does not respect these Regulations if
  the entity that made it charges a fee to review and assess the
  business proposal or to assess the business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple applicants

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there is more than one applicant in respect of a
  commitment, the commitment must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include information on each applicant; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify those applicants that the entity making
    the commitment considers essential to the business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditional commitment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is more than one applicant in respect of a
  commitment, the commitment may be conditional on the issuance of a
  permanent resident visa to one or more of those applicants.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum investment

- **[[98.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must establish the
  minimum total amount that the designated entities involved in the
  commitment must invest by buying shares or other forms of equity in
  the applicant's business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of entities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may establish different minimum
  investment amounts based on whether the commitment was made by a
  business incubator, an angel investor group or a venture capital fund
  and, for commitments involving more than one type of entity, may
  specify different minimum investment amounts for commitments involving
  different combinations of entity types.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must establish the minimum investment
  amount on the basis of the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number or projected number of start-up business
    class applicants and designated entities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proportion per year of start-up business class
    applications approved, withdrawn or refused, as well as the grounds
    for refusal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount of investment received per year by
    start-up business class applicants from designated entities as part
    of a commitment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number, type and industry sector of businesses
    started in Canada by approved start-up business class applicants per
    year and the average investment needs of start-up businesses of
    those types in those industry sectors;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the average amount of investment and number of
    investments made in start-up companies in Canada per year;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the level of investment usually required for a
    business started in Canada by a member of the start-up business
    class to be successful and for the member of the class to become
    economically established in Canada; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} industry standards and best practices, as
    determined in consultation with organizations referred to in section
    98.02, designated entities or other relevant stakeholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish the amounts established
  under subsection (1) on the Department's website.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifying business

- **[[98.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.06
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph
  98.01(2)(d), a qualifying business with respect to an applicant is one

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in which the applicant provides active and ongoing
    management from within Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for which an essential part of its operations is
    conducted in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that is incorporated in Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that has an ownership structure that complies with
    the percentages established under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- intention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A business that fails to meet one or more of the
  requirements of paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) is nevertheless a qualifying
  business if the applicant intends to have it meet those requirements
  after they have been issued a permanent resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ownership structure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must establish

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the minimum percentage of voting rights that are
    attached to all the outstanding shares of the corporation that must
    be held by the applicant; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum percentage of the total amount of the
    voting rights attached to all the outstanding shares of the
    corporation that may be held by persons or entities, other than
    qualified participants.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualified participant

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (3)(b), a qualified
  participant is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an applicant in respect of the business;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who has been issued a permanent
    resident visa as a member of the start-up business class in respect
    of the business; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a designated entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must establish the percentages referred
  to in subsection (3) on the basis of the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number or projected number of start-up business
    class applicants and designated entities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proportion per year of start-up business class
    applications approved, withdrawn or refused, as well as the grounds
    for refusal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the average size of businesses for which
    commitments are made and the average percentage of voting rights
    held by applicants and designated entities;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the average size of start-up companies in Canada
    and the average percentage of voting rights held by the founders of
    those companies and other investors in those companies;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the number, type and industry sector of businesses
    successfully started in Canada by approved start-up business class
    applicants per year and the most common ownership structures for
    businesses of those types in those industry sectors;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the ownership share usually required for a business
    started in Canada by a member of the start-up business class to be
    successful and for the member of the class to become economically
    established in Canada; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} industry standards and best practices, as
    determined in consultation with organizations referred to in section
    98.02, designated entities or other relevant stakeholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish the percentages established
  under subsection (3) on the Department's website.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

- **[[98.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An applicant must provide
  documentation to establish that they are a member of the start-up
  business class, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} written or electronic evidence, provided by an
    entity that was designated on the day on which the application is
    made, that indicates that the entity made a commitment with the
    applicant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the results of a language proficiency evaluation
    referred to in paragraph 98.01(2)(b); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} written or electronic evidence that they have the
    funds required under paragraph 98.01(2)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of other documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In evaluating an application in the start-up business
  class, an officer may, in addition to these documents, require the
  production of documents that are in the possession or control of the
  applicant or of the entity making the commitment and that relate to
  the applicant, the commitment or the business.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of commitment

- **[[98.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.08
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the officer is not satisfied that
  the entity assessed the applicant and the applicant's business in a
  manner consistent with industry standards or is not satisfied that the
  terms of the commitment are consistent with industry standards, the
  officer may refuse to issue the permanent resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple applicants

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is more than one applicant in respect of the
  same business and one of the applicants who was identified in the
  commitment as being essential to the business is refused a permanent
  resident visa for any reason or withdraws their application, the other
  applicants must be considered not to have met the requirements of
  subsection 98.01(2) and their permanent resident visa must also be
  refused.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Peer review

- **[[98.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.09
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An officer may request that a
  commitment, the applicants and designated entities party to the
  commitment and the qualifying business to which the commitment relates
  be independently assessed by a peer review panel established under an
  agreement referred to in section 98.02 by an organization that has
  expertise with respect to the type of entity making the commitment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request may be made if the officer is of the
  opinion that an independent assessment would assist in the application
  process. The request may also be made on a random basis.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Independent assessment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The peer review panel's assessment must be
  independent and take industry standards into account.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment not binding

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An officer who requests an independent assessment is
  not bound by it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substituted evaluation

- **[[98.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An officer may substitute their
  evaluation of the applicant's ability to become economically
  established in Canada for the requirements set out in subsection
  98.01(2), if meeting or failing to meet those requirements is not a
  sufficient indicator of whether the applicant will become economically
  established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, a substitute evaluation must not be
  conducted for an applicant who did not have a commitment from a
  designated entity on the day on which they made their application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A substitute evaluation made under subsection (1)
  requires the written concurrence of a second officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- organization

- **[[98.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An officer may inspect any
  organization that has entered into an agreement referred to in
  subsection 98.02(1) in any of the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer has a reason to suspect that the
    organization is not complying or has not complied with any of the
    conditions set out in subsection 98.02(3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the organization has not complied with those
    conditions in the past;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the organization is chosen as part of a random
    verification of compliance with those conditions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as part of routine monitoring activities, the
    organization is monitored to ensure that it respects those
    conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- designated entity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may inspect an entity designated under
  subsection 98.03(1) in any of the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer has a reason to suspect that the entity
    is not complying or has not complied with any of the conditions set
    out in subsection 98.03(3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the entity has not complied with those conditions
    in the past;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the entity is chosen as part of a random
    verification of compliance with those conditions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as part of routine monitoring activities, the
    entity is monitored to ensure that it respects those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other inspection

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition, the powers set out in sections 98.12 and
  98.13 may be exercised with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a designated entity, if an officer is inspecting an
    organization in accordance with subsection (1) and an inspection of
    the designated entity is necessary to verify that the organization
    is in compliance with the conditions set out in subsection 98.02(3);
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an organization that has entered into an agreement
    referred to in subsection 98.02(1), if an officer is inspecting a
    designated entity in accordance with subsection (2) and an
    inspection of the organization is necessary to verify that the
    designated entity is in compliance with the conditions set out in
    subsection 98.03(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- business

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The powers set out in sections 98.12 and 98.13 may be
  exercised with respect to a business that is the subject of a
  commitment by a designated entity with respect to the start-up
  business class if an officer is inspecting that designated entity in
  accordance with subsection (2) and an inspection of the business is
  necessary to verify that the designated entity is in compliance with
  the conditions set out in subsection 98.03(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- third party

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The powers set out in section 98.12 may be exercised
  with respect to a third party if an officer is inspecting an
  organization in accordance with subsection (1) or a designated entity
  in accordance with subsection (2) and an inspection of the third party
  is necessary to verify that that organization or that designated
  entity is in compliance with the conditions set out in subsection
  98.02(3) or 98.03(3), as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answering questions and providing
documents

- **[[98.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When conducting an inspection in
  accordance with section 98.11, an officer may, for the purpose of
  verifying that the organization or designated entity is in compliance
  with the relevant conditions,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require the organization, the designated entity, a
    business that is the subject of a commitment or a third party to
    provide the officer with any relevant document; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel}  require any employee or representative of the
    organization, designated entity, or a business that is the subject
    of a commitment or a third party to report at any specified time and
    place to answer questions and provide relevant documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with an obligation under
  paragraph (1)(a) or (b) is justified if the person, organization,
  entity or business made all reasonable efforts to comply with the
  obligation or if the failure results from anything done or omitted to
  be done by them in good faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entry to verify compliance

- **[[98.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (7), when
  conducting an inspection in accordance with section 98.11, an officer
  may, for the purpose of verifying that the organization or designated
  entity is in compliance with the relevant conditions, enter and
  inspect any premises or place in which business is conducted by the
  organization, the designated entity or a business that is the subject
  of a commitment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers in place or on premises

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When on the premises or in the place the officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ask any relevant questions of an employee or
    representative of the organization, designated entity or business
    that is the subject of a commitment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require from the organization, the designated
    entity or the business that is the subject of a commitment any
    relevant documents found in the premises or the place for
    examination;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} use copying equipment, or require that the
    organization, the designated entity or the business that is the
    subject of a commitment provide copies of those documents to be
    examined away from the premises or the place or, if it is not
    possible to make copies in the premises or the place, remove the
    relevant documents to make copies;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} take photographs and make video or audio
    recordings;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} examine anything in the premises or the place;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} require the organization, the designated entity or
    the business that is the subject of a commitment to use any computer
    or other electronic device in the premises or the place to allow the
    officer to examine any relevant document contained in or available
    to it; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied or assisted in the premises or the
    place by any person required by the officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An organization, entity, or a business that is the
  subject of a commitment, must, on request, provide an employee or
  representative to attend any inspection referred to in subsection (1),
  to give all reasonable assistance to the officer conducting that
  inspection and to provide that officer with any document or
  information that the officer requires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with an obligation under
  paragraph (2)(b), (c) or (f) or subsection (3) is justified if the
  organization, entity, or a business that is the subject of a
  commitment made all reasonable efforts to comply with the obligation
  or if the failure results from anything done or omitted to be done by
  them in good faith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entering private property

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An officer and any person accompanying the officer
  may enter on and pass through private property, other than a
  dwelling-house, to gain entry to the premises or the place. For
  greater certainty, they are not liable for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person accompanying officer

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person may, at an officer's request, accompany the
  officer to assist them to access the premises or the place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dwelling-house

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a dwelling-house, an officer may enter
  it without the occupant's consent only under the authority of a
  warrant issued under subsection (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of warrant

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application, a
  justice of the peace may issue a warrant authorizing an officer who is
  named in it to enter a dwelling-house, subject to any conditions
  specified in the warrant, if the justice of the peace is satisfied by
  information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds to believe that the
    dwelling-house is a premises or place referred to in subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry into the dwelling-house is necessary to
    verify compliance with the conditions set out in subsection 98.02(3)
    or 98.03(3), as the case may be; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry was refused by the occupant or there are
    reasonable grounds to believe that entry will be refused or that
    consent to entry cannot be obtained from the occupant.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-72, s. 3
:::

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2016-316, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Self-employed Persons]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687330 .Subheading}

##### [Self-employed Persons Class]{.HTitleText4} {#h-687331 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of the class

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the self-employed persons
  class is hereby prescribed as a class of persons who may become
  permanent residents on the basis of their ability to become
  economically established in Canada and who are self-employed persons
  within the meaning of subsection 88(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimal requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a foreign national who applies as a member of the
  self-employed persons class is not a self-employed person within the
  meaning of subsection 88(1), the application shall be refused and no
  further assessment is required.

##### [Quebec Self-employed Persons Class]{.HTitleText4} {#h-687337 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 12(2) of the Act, the Quebec self-employed
  persons class is prescribed as a class of persons who may become
  permanent residents on the basis of their ability to become
  economically established in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a member of the Quebec
  self-employed persons class if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intend to reside in Quebec; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are named in a [Certificat de sélection du
    Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"} issued by Quebec.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, ss. 36, 80(F)
- SOR/2016-316, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Selection Criteria]{.HTitleText3} {#h-687347 .Subheading}

##### [General]{.HTitleText4} {#h-687348 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of determining whether a foreign national, as a member of
  the self-employed persons class, and their family members will be able
  to become economically established in Canada, an officer shall assess
  the foreign national on the basis of the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} age, in accordance with section 102.1;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} education, in accordance with section 102.2;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} proficiency in the official languages of Canada, in
    accordance with section 102.3;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} experience, in accordance with section 103; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} adaptability, in accordance with section 105.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Units of assessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is assessed on the basis of
  the factors set out in paragraphs (1)(a) to (e) shall be awarded the
  applicable number of assessment points for each factor set out in the
  provision referred to in each of those paragraphs, subject to the
  maximum number set out in that provision for that factor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 37
- SOR/2012-274, s. 16
- SOR/2016-316, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-102-20130102.html "Link to previous version of section 102"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age (10 points)

**[[102.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102.1 .sectionLabel}** Points shall be
awarded, up to a maximum of 10, for a foreign national's age on the date
on which their application is made, as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} 10 points for a foreign national 21 years of age or
  older but less than 50 years of age;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 8 points for a foreign national 20 or 50 years of
  age;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} 6 points for a foreign national 19 or 51 years of
  age;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} 4 points for a foreign national 18 or 52 years of
  age;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} 2 points for a foreign national 17 or 53 years of
  age; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} 0 points, for a foreign national under 17 years of
  age or 54 years of age or older.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-274, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[102.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  this section.

  [[full-time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[full-time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a program
      of study leading to an educational credential, at least 15 hours
      of instruction per week during the academic year, including any
      period of training in the workplace that forms part of the course
      of instruction. ([temps plein]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[full-time equivalent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[full-time equivalent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of
      part-time or accelerated studies, the number of years that would
      have been required to complete the equivalent of those studies on
      a full-time basis. ([équivalent temps plein]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Education (25 points)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Points shall be awarded, to a maximum of 25, for a
  foreign national's education as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 5 points for a secondary school educational
    credential;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 12 points for a one-year post-secondary educational
    credential, other than a university educational credential, and a
    total of at least 12 years of completed full-time or full-time
    equivalent studies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 15 points for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a one-year post-secondary educational credential,
      other than a university educational credential, and a total of at
      least 13 years of completed full-time or full-time equivalent
      studies, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a one-year university educational credential at
      the bachelor's level and a total of at least 13 years of completed
      full-time or full-time equivalent studies;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 20 points for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a two-year post-secondary educational credential,
      other than a university educational credential, and a total of at
      least 14 years of completed full-time or full-time equivalent
      studies, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a two-year university educational credential at
      the bachelor's level and a total of at least 14 years of completed
      full-time or full-time equivalent studies;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 22 points for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a three-year post-secondary educational
      credential, other than a university educational credential, and a
      total of at least 15 years of completed full-time or full-time
      equivalent studies, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} two or more university educational credentials
      at the bachelor's level and a total of at least 15 years of
      completed full-time or full-time equivalent studies; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} 25 points for a university educational credential
    at the master's or doctoral level and a total of at least 17 years
    of completed full-time or full-time equivalent studies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one educational credential

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), points

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall not be awarded cumulatively on the basis of
    more than one educational credential; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be awarded

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of paragraphs (2)(a) to (d),
      subparagraph (2)(e)(i) and paragraph (2)(f), on the basis of the
      educational credential that results in the highest number of
      points, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subparagraph (2)(e)(ii), on
      the basis of the combined educational credentials referred to in
      that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special circumstances

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), if a foreign
  national has an educational credential referred to in any of
  paragraphs (2)(b) to (f), but not the total number of years of
  full-time or full-time equivalent studies required, the foreign
  national shall be awarded the same number of points as the number of
  years of completed full-time or full-time equivalent studies set out
  in the paragraph.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-274, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Official languages

- **[[102.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national must specify in
  their application for a permanent resident visa which language ---
  English or French --- is to be considered their first official
  language in Canada.They must have their proficiency in that language
  evaluated by an organization or institution designated under
  subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proficiency in second language

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the foreign national wishes to claim points for
  proficiency in their second official language they must, with the
  application for a permanent resident visa, submit the results of an
  evaluation --- which must be less than two years old on the date on
  which their application is made --- of their proficiency by an
  organization or institution designated under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proficiency in English and French (24
  points)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Points for proficiency in the official languages of
  Canada shall be awarded up to a maximum of 24 points based on the
  benchmarks referred to in the Canadian Language Benchmarks and the
  Niveaux de compétence linguistique canadiens, as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for high proficiency

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the first official language, 4 points for each
      language skill area if the foreign national's proficiency
      corresponds to a benchmark of 8 or higher, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the second official language, 2 points for
      each language skill area if the foreign national's proficiency
      corresponds to a benchmark of 8 or higher;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for moderate proficiency

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the first official language, 2 points for each
      language skill area if the foreign national's proficiency
      corresponds to a benchmark of 6 or 7, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the second official language, 2 points for
      each language skill area if the foreign national's proficiency
      corresponds to a benchmark of 6 or 7;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for basic proficiency in either official language,
    1 point for each language skill area, up to a maximum of 2 points,
    if the foreign national's proficiency corresponds to a benchmark of
    4 or 5; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for no proficiency in either official language, 0
    points if the foreign national's proficiency corresponds to a
    benchmark of 3 or lower.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evaluation of language proficiency

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may designate, for any period he or she
  specifies, any organization or institution to be responsible for
  evaluating language proficiency and approve the language test to be
  used to evaluate that proficiency if the organization or institution

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has expertise in evaluating language proficiency;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has provided the Minister with an equivalency
    between its language test results and the benchmarks set out in the
    Canadian Language Benchmarks or the [Niveaux de compétence
    linguistique canadiens]{.otherLang lang="fr"}, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must make available to the public the
  names of the designated organizations or institutions and the approved
  language tests.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition [[service
  agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (7), [[service
  agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement concluded between
  the Government of Canada and an organization or institution for the
  purpose of having the organization or institution supply the service
  of evaluating the language proficiency of foreign nationals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of designation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may revoke a designation of an
  organization or institution or the approval of a language test if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution no longer meets the
    criteria set out in subsection (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the organization or institution submitted false,
    misleading or inaccurate information or has contravened any
    provision of a federal or provincial law or regulation that is
    relevant to the service provided by the organization or institution;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} either the Government of Canada or the organization
    or institution has terminated the service agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conclusive evidence

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The results of an evaluation of language proficiency
  by a designated organization or institution using an approved language
  test are conclusive evidence of an applicant's language proficiency
  for the purposes of subsection (1) and of paragraph 98.01(2)(b).

- SOR/2018-72, s. 4
