SOR/2012-225, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel document

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall issue
a temporary travel document to a foreign national who has been
determined to be a member of a class prescribed by this Division and who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} holds a permanent resident visa or a temporary
  resident permit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not hold a valid passport or travel document
  issued by their country of nationality or the country of their present
  or former habitual residence;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not hold a valid travel document issued by the
  United Nations or the International Committee of the Red Cross and is
  unable to obtain such a document within a reasonable time; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} would be unable to travel to Canada if the temporary
  travel document were not issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 6(F)
:::

Version](section-151-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 151"){.PITLink}

#### [Protected Temporary Residents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688004 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protected temporary residents class

- **[[151.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The protected temporary residents
  class is prescribed as a class of persons who may become permanent
  residents on the basis of the requirements of this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Member of the class

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is a protected temporary resident
  and a member of the protected temporary residents class if the foreign
  national holds a temporary resident permit and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} became a temporary resident under a temporary
    resident permit for protection reasons after making a claim for
    refugee protection outside Canada under section 99 of the Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was issued a Minister's permit under section 37 of
    the former Act after seeking admission to Canada under section 7 of
    the former Regulations or section 4 of the Humanitarian Designated
    Classes Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national destined for Quebec does not
  become a member of the protected temporary residents class described
  in paragraph (2)(a) if they have not received a selection certificate
  from the Province of Quebec.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former Act and Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (2), [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  has the same meaning as in section 187 of the Act and [[former
  Regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and [[Humanitarian Designated
  Classes Regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} have the same meaning as in
  subsection 316(1) of these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 48
- SOR/2009-163, s. 7(F)
:::

[Previous
Version](section-151.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 151.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Sponsorship]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688018 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship agreements

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may enter into a sponsorship agreement with a sponsor for the
  purpose of facilitating the processing of sponsorship applications.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A sponsorship agreement shall include provisions
  relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} settlement plans;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} financial requirements;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} assistance to be provided by the Department;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the standard of conduct expected of the sponsor;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} reporting requirements; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the grounds for suspending or cancelling the
    agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sponsorship requirements

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  order to sponsor a foreign national and their family members who are
  members of a class prescribed by Division 1, a sponsor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must reside or have representatives in the expected
    community of settlement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} must make a sponsorship application that includes a
    settlement plan, an undertaking and, if the sponsor has not entered
    into a sponsorship agreement with the Minister, a document issued by
    the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees or a foreign state
    certifying the status of the foreign national as a refugee under the
    rules applicable to the United Nations High Commissioner for
    Refugees or the applicable laws of the foreign state, as the case
    may be; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} must not be --- or include --- an individual, a
    corporation or an unincorporated organization or association that
    was a party to a sponsorship in which they defaulted on an
    undertaking and remain in default.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of paragraphs 13(1)(a)
  and (b)

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 13(1)(a) and (b) do not apply to the
  document referred to in paragraph (1)(b) issued by the United Nations
  High Commissioner for Refugees or a foreign state.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of application

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If the foreign national has chosen to have their
  application for a permanent resident visa attached to the sponsorship
  application in accordance with paragraph 140.2(1)(b), the sponsor must
  send the sponsorship application and the application for a permanent
  resident visa to the Department's Case Processing Centre in Canada for
  processing those applications.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undertaking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The undertaking referred to in paragraph (1)(b) shall
  be signed by each party to the sponsorship.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint and several or solidary liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All parties to the undertaking are jointly and
  severally or solidarily liable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of default

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A party or a sponsor who defaults on an undertaking
  ceases to be in default

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a sponsor who defaults on a
    financial obligation, when the sponsor has reimbursed the government
    concerned, in full or in accordance with an agreement with that
    government, for amounts paid by the government;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a party, other than an organization
    or association, who defaults on a financial obligation, when the
    defaulting party has reimbursed any other party to the sponsorship,
    in full or in accordance with an agreement with that party, for
    amounts paid by that party;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a sponsor who defaults on a
    non-financial obligation, when the sponsor satisfies an officer that
    they are in compliance with the obligation; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an organization or association that
    was a party to a sponsorship and defaulted for any reason, when a
    period of five years has elapsed from the date of default.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 8(F)
- SOR/2012-225, s. 9
- SOR/2014-140, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-153-20121018.html "Link to previous version of section 153"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of application

- **[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer shall approve an application referred to in paragraph
  153(1)(b) if, on the basis of the documentation submitted with the
  application, the officer determines that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sponsor has the financial resources to fulfil
    the settlement plan for the duration of the undertaking, unless
    subsection 157(1) applies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the sponsor has made adequate arrangements in
    anticipation of the arrival of the foreign national and their family
    members in the expected community of settlement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of sponsor's undertaking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the duration of an
  undertaking is one year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer's determination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, on the basis of the assessment made
  under paragraph 139(1)(g), require that the duration of the
  undertaking be more than one year but not more than three years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revoking approval

**[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall revoke
an approval given in respect of an application under section 154 if the
officer determines that the sponsor no longer meets the requirements of
paragraph 154(1)(a) or (b) or is ineligible under subsection 156(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ineligibility to be a party to a
sponsorship

- **[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons are ineligible to be a party to a sponsorship:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who has been convicted in Canada of the
    offence of murder or an offence set out in Schedule I or II to the
    [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6),
    regardless of whether it was prosecuted by indictment, if a period
    of five years has not elapsed since the completion of the person's
    sentence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who has been convicted of an offence
    outside Canada that, if committed in Canada, would constitute an
    offence referred to in paragraph (a), if a period of five years has
    not elapsed since the completion of the person's sentence imposed
    under a foreign law;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is in default of any support payment
    obligations ordered by a court;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who is subject to a removal order;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person who is subject to a revocation proceeding
    under the [Citizenship Act](/eng/acts/C-29); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person who is detained in any penitentiary, jail,
    reformatory or prison.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception if pardoned

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), a sponsorship
  application may not be refused on the basis of a conviction in respect
  of which a pardon has been granted and has not ceased to have effect
  or been revoked under the [Criminal Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47), or
  in respect of which there has been a final determination of an
  acquittal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-195, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-156-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 156"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint assistance sponsorship

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an officer determines that special needs exist in respect of a member
  of a class prescribed by Division 1, the Department shall endeavour to
  identify a sponsor in order to make the financial assistance of the
  Government of Canada available for the purpose of sponsorship. A
  sponsor identified by the Department is exempt from the financial
  requirements of paragraph 154(1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[special
  needs]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[special
  needs]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means that a person has greater need of
  settlement assistance than other applicants for protection abroad
  owing to personal circumstances, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a large number of family members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} trauma resulting from violence or torture;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} medical disabilities; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the effects of systemic discrimination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Settlement in the Province of Quebec

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** If the foreign national
and their family members intend to reside in the province of Quebec, the
sponsor must meet the requirements for sponsorship under the Québec
Immigration Regulation, CQLR, c. I-0.2.1, r. 3, as amended from time to
time. In such a case, the requirements of this Division, other than
section 156, do not apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 80(F)
- [SOR/2023-249, s. 9](#1429154-1429246){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1429154-1429246.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 158"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Determination of Eligibility of Claim]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688089 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Working day

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsections 100(1) and (3) of the Act,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a working day does not include Saturdays or holidays;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a day that is not a working day is not included in
  the calculation of the three-day period; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the three-day period begins from the day on which the
  claim is received.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of prior claim

**[[159.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.01 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes
of paragraph 101(1)(c) of the Act, [[prior claim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
means a claim for refugee protection that was made under section 99 of
the Act, other than a claim that was determined to be ineligible to be
referred to the Refugee Protection Division under paragraph 101(1)(e) of
the Act if the foreign national who made that claim is subsequently
seeking to re-enter Canada in circumstances where they have been refused
entry to the United States without having a refugee claim adjudicated
there.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-58, s. 1](#1394879-1395193){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1394879-1395193.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[159.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this section and sections 159.2 to 159.7.

[[Agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Agreement done at
    Washington, D.C. on December 5, 2002 between the Government of
    Canada and the Government of the United States of America for
    Cooperation in the Examination of Refugee Status Claims from
    Nationals of Third Countries, including any modifications or
    additions made in accordance with its
    terms. ([Accord]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[claimant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[claimant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a claimant referred to in
    paragraph 101(1)(e) of the Act. ([demandeur]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[designated country]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated country]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a country
    designated by section 159.3. ([pays désigné]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[family member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[family member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a claimant,
    means their spouse or common-law partner, their legal guardian, and
    any of the following persons, namely, their child, father, mother,
    brother, sister, grandfather, grandmother, grandchild, uncle, aunt,
    nephew or niece. ([membre de la famille]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[legal guardian]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[legal guardian]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a claimant who
    has not attained the age of 18 years, means a person who has custody
    of the claimant or who is empowered to act on the claimant's behalf
    by virtue of a court order or written agreement or by operation of
    law. ([tuteur légal]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[stateless person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[stateless person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is not
    considered a national by any state under the operation of its
    law. ([apatride]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[United States]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[United States]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the United States of
    America, but does not include Puerto Rico, the Virgin Islands, Guam
    or any other United States of America possession or
    territory. ([États-Unis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
- [SOR/2023-58, s. 2](#1394881-1395196){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1394881-1395196.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 159.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- former habitual
residence

**[[159.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.2 .sectionLabel}** .Paragraph
101(1)(e) of the Act does not apply to a claimant who is a stateless
person who comes directly or indirectly to Canada from a designated
country that is their country of former habitual residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation --- United States

**[[159.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.3 .sectionLabel}** The United States
is designated under paragraph 102(1)(a) of the Act as a country that
complies with Article 33 of the Refugee Convention and Article 3 of the
Convention Against Torture, and is a designated country for the purpose
of the application of paragraph 101(1)(e) of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- ports of entry other
than land ports of entry

- **[[159.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 101(1)(e) of the Act does
  not apply to a claimant who seeks to enter Canada at

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (1.1), a location that is not
    a port of entry;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a port of entry that is a harbour port, including a
    ferry landing; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), a port of entry that is
    an airport.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- land border other than
  ports of entry

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 101(1)(e) of the Act applies to a
  claimant who enters Canada at a location along the Canada -- United
  States land border --- including the waters along or across that
  border --- that is not a port of entry and makes a claim for refugee
  protection less than 14 days after the day on which the claimant
  enters Canada unless the claimant establishes that any of paragraphs
  159.5(a) to (h) applies to the claimant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In transit exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 101(1)(e) of the Act applies to a claimant
  who has been ordered removed from the United States and who seeks to
  enter Canada at a port of entry that is an airport while they are in
  transit through Canada from the United States in the course of the
  enforcement of that order.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
- [SOR/2023-58, s. 3](#1394886-1395200){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1394886-1395200.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 159.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- claimants at land
ports of entry

**[[159.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.5 .sectionLabel}** Paragraph 101(1)(e)
of the Act does not apply if a claimant who seeks to enter Canada at a
location other than one identified in paragraphs 159.4(1)(a) to (c)
establishes that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a family member of the claimant is in Canada and is a
  Canadian citizen;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a family member of the claimant is in Canada and is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a protected person within the meaning of subsection
    95(2) of the Act,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a permanent resident under the Act, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a person in favour of whom a removal order has
    been stayed in accordance with section 233;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a family member of the claimant who has attained the
  age of 18 years is in Canada and has made a claim for refugee
  protection that has been referred to the Board for determination,
  unless

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the claim has been withdrawn by the family member,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the claim has been abandoned by the family member,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the claim has been rejected, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any pending proceedings or proceedings respecting
    the claim have been terminated under subsection 104(2) of the Act or
    any decision respecting the claim has been nullified under that
    subsection;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a family member of the claimant who has attained the
  age of 18 years is in Canada and is the holder of a work permit or
  study permit other than

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a work permit that was issued under paragraph
    206(b) or that has become invalid as a result of the application of
    section 209, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a study permit that has become invalid as a result
    of the application of section 222;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the claimant is a person who

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has not attained the age of 18 years and is not
    accompanied by their mother, father or legal guardian,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has neither a spouse nor a common-law partner, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has neither a mother or father nor a legal
    guardian in Canada or the United States;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the claimant is the holder of any of the following
  documents, excluding any document issued for the sole purpose of
  transit through Canada, namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a permanent resident visa or a temporary resident
    visa referred to in section 6 and subsection 7(1), respectively,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a temporary resident permit issued under
    subsection 24(1) of the Act,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a travel document referred to in subsection 31(3)
    of the Act,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} refugee travel papers issued by the Minister, or

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a temporary travel document referred to in section
    151;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the claimant is a person

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} who may, under the Act or these Regulations, enter
    Canada without being required to hold a visa, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} who would, if the claimant were entering the
    United States, be required to hold a visa; or

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the claimant is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is seeking to re-enter
    Canada in circumstances where they have been refused entry to the
    United States without having a refugee claim adjudicated there, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a permanent resident who has been ordered removed
    from the United States and is being returned to Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
- SOR/2009-290, s. 2(E)
- SOR/2015-46, s. 1
- [SOR/2023-58, s. 4](#1394891-1395202){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1394891-1395202.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.5-20150220.html "Link to previous version of section 159.5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- claimants at land
ports of entry and in transit

**[[159.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.6 .sectionLabel}** Paragraph 101(1)(e)
of the Act does not apply if a claimant establishes that the claimant

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is charged in the United States with, or has been
  convicted there of, an offence that is punishable with the death
  penalty in the United States; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is charged in a country other than the United States
  with, or has been convicted there of, an offence that is punishable
  with the death penalty in that country.

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2009-210, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
- SOR/2009-210, s. 1
- [SOR/2023-58, s. 5](#1394893-1395204){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1394893-1395204.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.6-20090723.html "Link to previous version of section 159.6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporal operation

- **[[159.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph
  101(1)(e) of the Act, the application of all or part of sections 159.1
  to 159.6 and this section is discontinued, in accordance with
  subsections (2) to (6), if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice of suspension of the Agreement setting out
    the period of suspension is publicized broadly in the various
    regions of Canada by the Minister via information media and on the
    website of the Department;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a notice of renewal of the suspension of the
    Agreement setting out the period of renewal of suspension is
    published in accordance with subsection (6);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a notice of suspension of a part of the Agreement
    is issued by the Government of Canada and the Government of the
    United States; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a notice of termination of the Agreement is issued
    by the Government of Canada or the Government of the United States.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraph (1)(a) --- notice of
  suspension of Agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), if a notice of suspension
  of the Agreement is publicized under paragraph (1)(a), sections 159.2
  to 159.6 are rendered inoperative for a period of up to three months
  that shall be set out in the notice, which period shall begin on the
  day after the day on which the notice is publicized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraph (1)(b) --- notice of renewal
  of suspension of Agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a notice of renewal of the suspension of the
  Agreement is published under paragraph (1)(b), sections 159.2 to 159.6
  are rendered inoperative for the further period of up to three months
  set out in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraph (1)(c) --- suspension of part
  of Agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a notice of suspension of part of the Agreement is
  issued under paragraph (1)(c), those provisions of these Regulations
  relating to the application of the Agreement that are referred to in
  the notice are rendered inoperative for a period that shall be set out
  in the notice. All other provisions of these Regulations continue to
  apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraph (1)(d) --- termination of
  Agreement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a notice of termination of the Agreement is issued
  under paragraph (1)(d), sections 159.1 to 159.6 and this section cease
  to have effect on the day set out in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication requirement --- [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any notice referred to in paragraph (1)(b), (c)
  or (d) shall be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), Part I, not less than seven days
  before the day on which the renewal, suspension in part or termination
  provided for in the notice is effective.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-217, s. 2
:::

### [DIVISION 3.1]{.HLabel2}[Claim for Refugee Protection --- Time Limits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688182 .Subheading}

#### [Documents and Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688183 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- provision of documents and
information to officer

- **[[159.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection
  99(3.1) of the Act, a person who makes a claim for refugee protection
  inside Canada other than at a port of entry must provide an officer
  with the documents and information referred to in that subsection not
  later than the day on which the officer determines the eligibility of
  their claim under subsection 100(1) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- provision of documents
  and information to Refugee Protection Division

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), for the purpose of
  subsection 100(4) of the Act, a person who makes a claim for refugee
  protection inside Canada at a port of entry must provide the Refugee
  Protection Division with the documents and information referred to in
  subsection 100(4) not later than 15 days after the day on which the
  claim is referred to that Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the documents and information cannot be provided
  within the time limit set out in subsection (2), the Refugee
  Protection Division may, for reasons of fairness and natural justice,
  extend that time limit by the number of days that is necessary in the
  circumstances.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-252, s. 1
:::

#### [Hearing Before Refugee Protection Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688193 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits for hearing

- **[[159.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  for the purpose of subsection 100(4.1) of the Act, the date fixed for
  the hearing before the Refugee Protection Division must be not later
  than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a claimant referred to in subsection
    111.1(2) of the Act,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 30 days after the day on which the claim is
      referred to the Refugee Protection Division, if the claim is made
      inside Canada other than at a port of entry, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 45 days after the day on which the claim is
      referred to the Refugee Protection Division, if the claim is made
      inside Canada at a port of entry; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other claimant, 60 days after
    the day on which the claim is referred to the Refugee Protection
    Division, whether the claim is made inside Canada at a port of entry
    or inside Canada other than at a port of entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the time limit set out in subparagraph (1)(a)(i)
  or (ii) or paragraph (1)(b) ends on a Saturday, that time limit is
  extended to the next working day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the hearing cannot be held within the time limit
  set out in subparagraph (1)(a)(i) or (ii) or paragraph (1)(b) for any
  of the following reasons, the hearing must be held as soon as feasible
  after that time limit:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for reasons of fairness and natural justice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} because of a pending investigation or inquiry
    relating to any of sections 34 to 37 of the Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} because of operational limitations of the Refugee
    Protection Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-252, s. 1
:::

#### [Appeal to Refugee Appeal Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688210 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for appeal

- **[[159.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), for the
  purpose of subsection 110(2.1) of the Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the time limit for a person or the Minister to file
    an appeal to the Refugee Appeal Division against a decision of the
    Refugee Protection Division is 15 days after the day on which the
    person or the Minister receives written reasons for the decision;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time limit for a person or the Minister to
    perfect such an appeal is 30 days after the day on which the person
    or the Minister receives written reasons for the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the appeal cannot be filed within the time limit
  set out in paragraph 1)(a) or perfected within the time limit set out
  in paragraph (1)(b), the Refugee Appeal Division may, for reasons of
  fairness and natural justice, extend each of those time limits by the
  number of days that is necessary in the circumstances.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-252, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for decision

- **[[159.92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.92
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), for the
  purpose of subsection 110(3.1) of the Act, except when a hearing is
  held under subsection 110(6) of the Act, the time limit for the
  Refugee Appeal Division to make a decision on an appeal is 90 days
  after the day on which the appeal is perfected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not possible for the Refugee Appeal Division
  to make a decision on an appeal within the time limit set out in
  subsection (1), the decision must be made as soon as feasible after
  that time limit.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-252, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Pre-Removal Risk Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688227 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for protection

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2) and for
  the purposes of subsection 112(1) of the Act, a person may apply for
  protection after they are given notification to that effect by the
  Department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No notification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person described in section 165 or 166 may apply
  for protection in accordance with that section without being given
  notification to that effect by the Department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notification shall be given

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who is subject to a removal
    order that is in force, before removal from Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person named in a certificate
    described in subsection 77(1) of the Act, when the summary of
    information and other evidence is filed under subsection 77(2) of
    the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When notification is given

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notification is given

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} when the person is given the application for
    protection form by hand; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application for protection form is sent by
    mail, seven days after the day on which it was sent to the person at
    the last address provided by them to the Department.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-193, s. 1
- SOR/2009-290, s. 3(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-160-20080611.html "Link to previous version of section 160"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criterion --- exemption from application
of paragraphs 112(2)(b.1) or (c) of the Act

**[[160.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 112(2.1) of the Act, the Minister must consider, when an
exemption is made, any event having arisen in a country that could place
all or some of its nationals or former habitual residents referred to in
that subsection in a situation similar to those referred to in section
96 or 97 of the Act for which a person may be determined to be a
Convention refugee or a person in need of protection.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submissions

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 166, a person
  applying for protection may make written submissions in support of
  their application and for that purpose may be assisted, at their own
  expense, by a barrister or solicitor or other counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes written submissions must identify
  the evidence presented that meets the requirements of paragraph 113(a)
  of the Act and indicate how that evidence relates to them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-139, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 161"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application within 15-day period

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** An application received
within 15 days after notification was given under section 160 shall not
be decided until at least 30 days after notification was given. The
removal order is stayed under section 232 until the earliest of the
events referred to in paragraphs 232(c) to (f) occurs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications after the 15-day period

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** A person who has
remained in Canada since being given notification under section 160 may
make an application after a period of 15 days has elapsed from
notification being given under that section, but the application does
not result in a stay of the removal order. Written submissions, if any,
must accompany the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application that must be received within
15-day period --- certificate

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 163, an
application by a person who is named in a certificate described in
subsection 77(1) of the Act must be received within 15 days after
notification was given under section 160. An application received within
that period shall not be decided until at least 30 days after
notification was given.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-290, s. 4(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-164-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 164"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent application

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** A person whose
application for protection was rejected and who has remained in Canada
since being given notification under section 160 may make another
application. Written submissions, if any, must accompany the
application. For greater certainty, the application does not result in a
stay of the removal order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application at port of entry

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** An application for
protection by a foreign national against whom a removal order is made at
a port of entry as a result of a determination of inadmissibility on
entry into Canada must, if the order is in force, be received as soon as
the removal order is made. Written submissions, if any, must accompany
the application. For greater certainty, the application does not result
in a stay of the removal order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing --- prescribed factors

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
determining whether a hearing is required under paragraph 113(b) of the
Act, the factors are the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether there is evidence that raises a serious issue
  of the applicant's credibility and is related to the factors set out
  in sections 96 and 97 of the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the evidence is central to the decision with
  respect to the application for protection; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the evidence, if accepted, would justify
  allowing the application for protection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing procedure

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** A hearing is subject to
the following provisions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notice shall be provided to the applicant of the time
  and place of the hearing and the issues of fact that will be raised at
  the hearing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hearing is restricted to matters relating to the
  issues of fact stated in the notice, unless the officer conducting the
  hearing considers that other issues of fact have been raised by
  statements made by the applicant during the hearing;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the applicant must respond to the questions posed by
  the officer and may be assisted for that purpose, at their own
  expense, by a barrister or solicitor or other counsel; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any evidence of a person other than the applicant
  must be in writing and the officer may question the person for the
  purpose of verifying the evidence provided.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** An application for
protection is declared abandoned

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant who fails to appear at a
  hearing, if the applicant is given notice of a subsequent hearing and
  fails to appear at that hearing; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant who voluntarily departs
  Canada, when the applicant's removal order is enforced under section
  240 or the applicant otherwise departs Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-290, s. 5(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 169"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** An application for
protection may be withdrawn by the applicant at any time by notifying
the Minister in writing. The application is declared to be withdrawn on
receipt of the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of abandonment and withdrawal

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** An application for
protection is rejected when a decision is made not to allow the
application or when the application is declared withdrawn or abandoned.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant described in s. 112(3) of the
Act

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before making a decision to allow or
  reject the application of an applicant described in subsection 112(3)
  of the Act, the Minister shall consider the assessments referred to in
  subsection (2) and any written response of the applicant to the
  assessments that is received within 15 days after the applicant is
  given the assessments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following assessments shall be given to the
  applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written assessment on the basis of the factors
    set out in section 97 of the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written assessment on the basis of the factors
    set out in subparagraph 113(d)(i) or (ii) of the Act, as the case
    may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), no assessments shall be
  given to an applicant who is named in a certificate until a judge
  under section 78 of the Act determines whether the certificate is
  reasonable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When assessments given

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The assessments are given to an applicant when they
  are given by hand to the applicant or, if sent by mail, are deemed to
  be given to an applicant seven days after the day on which they are
  sent to the last address that the applicant provided to the
  Department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant not described in s. 97 of the
  Act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (3), if the Minister
  decides on the basis of the factors set out in section 97 of the Act
  that the applicant is not described in that section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} no written assessment on the basis of the factors
    set out in subparagraph 113(d)(i) or (ii) of the Act need be made;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the application is rejected.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2008-193, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 172"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-examination of stay --- procedure

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person in respect of whom a stay of a removal order, with respect to a
  country or place, is being re-examined under subsection 114(2) of the
  Act shall be given

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice of re-examination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written assessment on the basis of the factors
    set out in section 97 of the Act; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a written assessment on the basis of the factors
    set out in subparagraph 113(d)(i) or (ii) of the Act, as the case
    may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessments and response

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making a decision to cancel or maintain the
  stay of the removal order, the Minister shall consider the assessments
  and any written response of the person in respect of whom the stay is
  being re-examined that is received within 15 days after the
  assessments are given to that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When assessments given

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The assessments are given to an applicant when they
  are given by hand to the applicant or, if sent by mail, are deemed to
  be given to an applicant seven days after the day on which they are
  sent to the last address that the applicant provided to the
  Department.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-290, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-173-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 173"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for decision

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** On request, an
applicant shall be given a copy of the file notes that record the
justification for the decision on their application for protection.

### [DIVISION 4.1]{.HLabel2}[Designated Foreign National --- Requirement to Report to an Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688318 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular reporting intervals

- **[[174.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection
  98.1(1) of the Act, a designated foreign national referred to in that
  subsection who has not become a permanent resident under subsection
  21(2) of the Act must report to an officer as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in person, not more than 30 days after refugee
    protection is conferred on the designated foreign national under
    paragraph 95(1)(b) or (c) of the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} once a year in each year after the day on which the
    foreign national first reports to an officer under paragraph (a), on
    a date fixed by the officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting on request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The designated foreign national must also report to
  an officer if requested to do so by the officer because the officer
  has reason to believe that any of the circumstances referred to in
  paragraphs 108(1)(a) to (e) of the Act may apply in respect of the
  designated foreign national.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional reporting requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirements of
  subsections (1) and (2), the designated foreign national must report
  to an officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any change in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their address, not more than 10 working days
      after the day on which the change occurs, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} their employment status, not more than 20
      working days after the day on which the change occurs;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any departure from Canada, not less than 10 working
    days before the day of their departure; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any return to Canada, not more than 10 working days
    after the day of their return.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of reporting requirements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The reporting requirements in subsections (1) to (3)
  cease to apply to the designated foreign national on the day on which
  they become a permanent resident.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-244, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Protected Persons --- Permanent Residence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688337 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial review

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 21(2) of the Act, an officer shall not be
  satisfied that an applicant meets the conditions of that subsection if
  the determination or decision is subject to judicial review or if the
  time limit for commencing judicial review has not elapsed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-154, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quebec

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 21(2) of the Act, an
  applicant who makes an application to remain in Canada as a permanent
  resident --- and the family members included in the application ---
  who intend to reside in the Province of Quebec as permanent residents
  and who are not persons whom the Board has determined to be Convention
  refugees, may become permanent residents only if it is established
  that the competent authority of that Province is of the opinion that
  they meet the selection criteria of the Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-175-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 175"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family members

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant may include in their application to remain in Canada as a
  permanent resident any of their family members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One-year time limit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A family member who is included in an application to
  remain in Canada as a permanent resident and who is outside Canada at
  the time the application is made shall be issued a permanent resident
  visa if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the family member makes an application outside
    Canada to an officer within one year after the day on which the
    applicant becomes a permanent resident; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the family member is not inadmissible on the
    grounds referred to in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadmissibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A family member who is inadmissible on any of the
  grounds referred to in subsection 21(2) of the Act shall not be issued
  a permanent resident visa and shall not become a permanent resident.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed classes

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 21(2) of the Act, the following are prescribed as classes of
persons who cannot become permanent residents:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the class of persons who have been the subject of a
  decision under section 108 or 109 or subsection 114(3) of the Act
  resulting in the rejection of a claim for refugee protection or
  nullification of the decision that led to conferral of refugee
  protection;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the class of persons who are permanent residents at
  the time of their application to remain in Canada as a permanent
  resident;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the class of persons who have been recognized by any
  country, other than Canada, as Convention refugees and who, if removed
  from Canada, would be allowed to return to that country;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the class of nationals or citizens of a country,
  other than the country that the person left, or outside of which the
  person remains, by reason of fear of persecution; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the class of persons who have permanently resided in
  a country, other than the country that the person left, or outside of
  which the person remains, by reason of fear of persecution, and who,
  if removed from Canada, would be allowed to return to that country.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-140, s. 16(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-177-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 177"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity documents

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant who does not hold a document described in any of paragraphs
  50(1)(a) to (h) may submit with their application

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any identity document issued outside Canada before
    the person's entry into Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there is a reasonable and objectively verifiable
    explanation related to circumstances in the applicant's country of
    nationality or former habitual residence for the applicant's
    inability to obtain any identity documents, a statutory declaration
    made by the applicant attesting to their identity, accompanied by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a statutory declaration attesting to the
      applicant's identity made by a person who, before the applicant's
      entry into Canada, knew the applicant, a family member of the
      applicant or the applicant's father, mother, brother, sister,
      grandfather or grandmother, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a statutory declaration attesting to the
      applicant's identity made by an official of an organization
      representing nationals of the applicant's country of nationality
      or former habitual residence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document submitted under subsection (1) shall be
  accepted in lieu of a document described in any of paragraphs 50(1)(a)
  to (h) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an identity document, the identity
    document

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is genuine,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} identifies the applicant, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} constitutes credible evidence of the
      applicant's identity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a statutory declaration, the
    declaration

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is consistent with any information previously
      provided by the applicant to the Department or the Board, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} constitutes credible evidence of the applicant's
      identity.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 49
- SOR/2011-126, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-178-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 178"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[Temporary Residents]{.HTitleText1} {#h-688382 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Temporary Resident Visa]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688383 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance

**[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall issue
a temporary resident visa to a foreign national if, following an
examination, it is established that the foreign national

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has applied in accordance with these Regulations for
  a temporary resident visa as a member of the visitor, worker or
  student class;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} will leave Canada by the end of the period authorized
  for their stay under Division 2;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds a passport or other document that they may use
  to enter the country that issued it or another country;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements applicable to that class;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} is not inadmissible;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of subsections 30(2) and (3),
  if they must submit to a medical examination under paragraph 16(2)(b)
  of the Act; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} is not the subject of a declaration made under
  subsection 22.1(1) of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 9
- SOR/2013-210, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20120815.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Holders of Temporary Resident Visas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688395 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national who
holds a temporary resident visa is not authorized to enter and remain in
Canada as a temporary resident unless, following an examination, it is
established that the foreign national and their accompanying family
members

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} met the requirements for issuance of their temporary
  resident visa at the time it was issued; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} continue to meet these requirements at the time of
  the examination on entry into Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-212, s. 8](#1138237-1145982){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138237-1145982.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-180-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 180"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Cancellation --- Temporary Resident Visa]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1508740 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

**[[180.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.1 .sectionLabel}** An officer may
cancel a temporary resident visa that was issued to a foreign national
if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is inadmissible;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national did not meet one or more of the
  requirements under section 179 at the time the temporary resident visa
  was issued or no longer meets one or more of those requirements;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national was issued, after the issuance
  of the temporary resident visa, a temporary resident permit under
  subsection 24(1) of the Act;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the officer has reasonable grounds to believe that
  the foreign national will not leave Canada by the end of the period
  authorized for their stay under Division 2;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national was refused, after the issuance
  of the temporary resident visa, an electronic travel authorization,
  work permit or study permit or another temporary resident visa;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is the subject of a declaration
  made under subsection 22.1(1) of the Act; or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the officer is satisfied that the temporary resident
  visa was issued based on an administrative error.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 4](#1508531-1508755){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508531-1508755.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

**[[180.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.2 .sectionLabel}** A temporary
resident visa that was issued to a foreign national is cancelled if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national becomes a permanent resident;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the passport or other travel document in respect of
  which the temporary resident visa was issued is lost, stolen or
  destroyed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the passport or other travel document in respect of
  which the temporary resident visa was issued is abandoned by the
  foreign national; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is deceased.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 4](#1508531-1508763){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508531-1508763.html"}
:::

#### [Applications for Extension of Authorization to Remain in Canada as a Temporary Resident]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688400 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may apply for an extension of their authorization to
  remain in Canada as a temporary resident if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made by the end of the period
    authorized for their stay; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have complied with all conditions imposed on
    their entry into Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall extend the foreign national's
  authorization to remain in Canada as a temporary resident if,
  following an examination, it is established that the foreign national
  continues to meet the requirements of section 179.

#### [Restoration of Temporary Resident Status]{.HTitleText3} {#h-688408 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restoration

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application made by a visitor, worker or student within 90 days after
  losing temporary resident status as a result of failing to comply with
  a condition imposed under paragraph 185(a), any of subparagraphs
  185(b)(i) to (iii) or paragraph 185(c), an officer shall restore that
  status if, following an examination, it is established that the
  visitor, worker or student meets the initial requirements for their
  stay, has not failed to comply with any other conditions imposed and
  is not the subject of a declaration made under subsection 22.1(1) of
  the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), an officer shall not restore
  the status of a student who is not in compliance with a condition set
  out in subsection 220.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-210, s. 2
- SOR/2014-14, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-182-20131125.html "Link to previous version of section 182"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1.1]{.HLabel2}[Declaration Under Subsection 22.1(1) of the Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688416 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

**[[182.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182.1 .sectionLabel}** If the Minister
makes a declaration under subsection 22.1(1) of the Act in respect of a
foreign national, the foreign national is considered to have been given
notice of the declaration if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notice is sent by mail to their last known address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notice is sent to their last known email address;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} notice is provided to them by hand, including at a
  port of entry; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} notice is sent or provided by other reasonable means,
  if it is not possible to give notice by one of the means referred to
  in paragraphs (a) to (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-210, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written submissions

- **[[182.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is the
  subject of a declaration made under subsection 22.1(1) of the Act may
  make written submissions to the Minister as to why the declaration
  should be revoked or why its effective period should be shortened.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The written submissions must be made within 60 days
  after the day on which the notice of the declaration is sent or
  provided to the foreign national, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-210, s. 2
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Conditions on Temporary Residents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688432 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General conditions

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 185, the
  following conditions are imposed on all temporary residents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to leave Canada by the end of the period authorized
    for their stay;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to not work, unless authorized by this Part or Part
    11;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} if authorized to work by this Part or Part 11, to
    not enter into an employment agreement, or extend the term of an
    employment agreement, with an employer who, on a regular basis,
    offers striptease, erotic dance, escort services or erotic massages;

  - [(b.2)]{.lawlabel} if authorized to work by this Part or Part 11, to
    not enter into an employment agreement, or extend the term of an
    employment agreement, with an employer who is referred to in
    subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to not study, unless authorized by the Act, this
    Part or Part 12; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to comply with all requirements imposed on them by
    an order or regulation made under the [Emergencies
    Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized period of stay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (5), the period
  authorized for the stay of a temporary resident is six months or any
  other period that is fixed by an officer on the basis of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the temporary resident's means of support in
    Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the temporary resident applies
    to stay; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of the temporary resident's passport or
    other travel document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized period begins

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The period authorized for the stay of a temporary
  resident begins on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if they are authorized to enter and remain in
    Canada on a temporary basis, the day on which they first enter
    Canada after they are so authorized;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} if they have become a temporary resident in
    accordance with subsection 46(1.1) of the Act, the day on which
    their application to renounce their permanent resident status is
    approved; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the day on which they enter
    Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized period ends

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The period authorized for a temporary resident's stay
  ends on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the temporary resident leaves
    Canada without obtaining prior authorization to re-enter Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their permit becomes invalid, in
    the case of a temporary resident who has been issued either a work
    permit or a study permit;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the second of their permits
    becomes invalid, in the case of a temporary resident who has been
    issued a work permit and a study permit;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the day on which any temporary resident permit
    issued to the temporary resident is no longer valid under section
    63;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who is required by
    section 10.01 of the Act to provide their biometric information, the
    day on which the period of 10 years following the latest day on
    which the person provided their biometric information under section
    10.01 of the Act ends; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the period authorized under
    subsection (2) ends, if paragraphs (a) to (c) do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period authorized for stay

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5.1), if a temporary resident
  has applied for an extension of the period authorized for their stay
  and a decision is not made on the application by the end of the period
  authorized for their stay, the period is extended until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a decision is made, if the
    application is refused; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the end of the new period authorized for their
    stay, if the application is allowed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (5) does not apply in respect of a
  foreign national who is the subject of a declaration made under
  subsection 22.1(1) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of status and conditions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the period authorized for the stay of a temporary
  resident is extended by operation of paragraph (5)(a) or extended
  under paragraph (5)(b), the temporary resident retains their status,
  subject to any other conditions imposed, during the extended period.

::: HistoricalNote

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition imposed on members of a crew

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national who enters Canada as a member of a crew must leave
  Canada within 72 hours after they cease to be a member of a crew.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions imposed on foreign nationals
  who enter to become members of a crew

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following conditions are imposed on a foreign
  national who enters Canada to become a member of a crew:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2004-167, s. 50\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to join the means of transportation within the
    period imposed as a condition of entry or, if no period is imposed,
    within 48 hours after they enter Canada; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to leave Canada within 72 hours after they cease to
    be a member of a crew.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 50
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific conditions

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** An officer may impose,
vary or cancel the following specific conditions on a temporary
resident:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period authorized for their stay;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the work that they are permitted to engage in, or are
  prohibited from engaging in, in Canada, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the type of work,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer,

  - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the work for any one employer,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the location of the work,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the times and periods of the work, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a member of a crew, the period
    within which they must join the means of transportation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the studies that they are permitted to engage in, or
  are prohibited from engaging in, in Canada, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the type of studies or course,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the educational institution,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the location of the studies, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the times and periods of the studies;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the area within which they are permitted to travel or
  are prohibited from travelling in Canada; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the times and places at which they must report for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} medical examination, surveillance or treatment, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the presentation of evidence of compliance with
    applicable conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 51(F)
- [SOR/2024-240, s. 1](#1496878-1497820){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1496878-1497820.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-185-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 185"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Work Without a Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688499 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No permit required

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national may
work in Canada without a work permit

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} as a business visitor to Canada within the meaning of
  section 187;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} as a foreign representative, if they are properly
  accredited by the Department of Foreign Affairs and International
  Trade and are in Canada to carry out official duties as a diplomatic
  agent, consular officer, representative or official of a country other
  than Canada, of the United Nations or any of its agencies or of any
  international organization of which Canada is a member;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is a family member of a
  foreign representative in Canada who is accredited with diplomatic
  status by the Department of Foreign Affairs and International Trade
  and that Department has stated in writing that it does not object to
  the foreign national working in Canada;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} as a member of the armed forces of a country that is
  a designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
  Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as a
  civilian component of those armed forces;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} as an officer of a foreign government sent, under an
  exchange agreement between Canada and one or more countries, to take
  up duties with a federal or provincial agency;

- [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} as a cross-border maritime law enforcement officer
  designated by the United States under the Framework Agreement on
  Integrated Cross-Border Maritime Law Enforcement Operations between
  the Government of Canada and the Government of the United States of
  America, signed on May 26, 2009;

- [(e.2)]{.lawlabel} as an in-flight security officer employed by a
  foreign government with which Canada has concluded an arrangement in
  respect of commercial passenger aircraft security;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} if they are a full-time student, on the campus of the
  university or college at which they are a full-time student, for the
  period for which they hold a study permit to study at that university
  or college;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} as a performing artist appearing alone or in a group
  in an artistic performance --- other than a performance that is
  primarily for a film production or a television or radio broadcast ---
  or as a member of the staff of such a performing artist or group who
  is integral to the artistic performance, if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they are part of a foreign production or group, or
    are a guest artist in a Canadian production or group, performing a
    time-limited engagement, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} they are not in an employment relationship with
    the organization or business in Canada that is contracting for their
    services;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} as a participant in sports activities or events, in
  Canada, either as an individual participant or as a member of a
  foreign-based team or Canadian amateur team;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} as an employee of a foreign news company for the
  purpose of reporting on events in Canada;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} as a guest speaker for the sole purpose of making a
  speech or delivering a paper at a dinner, graduation, convention or
  similar function, or as a commercial speaker or seminar leader
  delivering a seminar that lasts no longer than five days;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} as a member of the executive of a committee that is
  organizing a convention or meeting in Canada or as a member of the
  administrative support staff of such a committee;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} as a person who is responsible for assisting a
  congregation or group in the achievement of its spiritual goals and
  whose main duties are to preach doctrine, perform functions related to
  gatherings of the congregation or group or provide spiritual
  counselling;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} as a judge, referee or similar official at an
  international amateur sports competition, an international cultural or
  artistic event or competition or an animal or agricultural
  competition;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} as an examiner or evaluator of research proposals or
  university projects, programs or theses;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} as an expert who conducts surveys or analyses that
  are to be used as evidence before a federal or provincial regulatory
  body, a tribunal or a court of law or as an expert witness before such
  a body, tribunal or court of law;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} as a student in a health field, including as a
  medical elective or clinical clerk at a medical teaching institution
  in Canada, for the primary purpose of acquiring training, if they have
  written approval from the body that regulates that field;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} as a civil aviation inspector of a national
  aeronautical authority conducting inspections of the flight operation
  procedures or cabin safety of a commercial air carrier operating
  international flights;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} as an accredited representative or adviser
  participating in an aviation accident or incident investigation
  conducted under the [Canadian Transportation Accident Investigation
  and Safety Board Act](/eng/acts/C-23.4);

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} as a member of a crew who is employed by a foreign
  company aboard a means of transportation that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is foreign-owned and not registered in Canada, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is engaged primarily in international
    transportation;

- [(t)]{.lawlabel} as a provider of emergency services, including
  medical services, for the protection or preservation of life or
  property;

- [(u)]{.lawlabel} until a decision is made on an application made by
  them under subsection 201(1), if they have remained in Canada after
  the expiry of their work permit and they have continued to comply with
  the conditions set out on the expired work permit, other than the
  expiry date;

- [(v)]{.lawlabel} if they are the holder of a study permit and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they are a full-time student enrolled at a
    designated learning institution as defined in section 211.1,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the program in which they are enrolled is a
    post-secondary academic, vocational or professional training
    program, or a vocational training program at the secondary level
    offered in Quebec, in each case, of a duration of six months or more
    that leads to a degree, diploma or certificate, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} although they are permitted to engage in
    full-time work during a regularly scheduled break between academic
    sessions, they work no more than 24 hours per week during a regular
    academic session;

- [(w)]{.lawlabel} if they are or were the holder of a study permit who
  has completed their program of study and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they met the requirements set out in paragraph (v),
    and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} they applied for a work permit before the expiry
    of that study permit and a decision has not yet been made in respect
    of their application; or

- [(x)]{.lawlabel} if they are an Indian.

- SOR/2014-14, s. 5
- SOR/2014-170, s. 1
