[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business visitors

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 186(a), a business visitor to Canada is a
  foreign national who is described in subsection (2) or who seeks to
  engage in international business activities in Canada without directly
  entering the Canadian labour market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific cases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following foreign nationals are business
  visitors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals purchasing Canadian goods or
    services for a foreign business or government, or receiving training
    or familiarization in respect of such goods or services;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals receiving or giving training
    within a Canadian parent or subsidiary of the corporation that
    employs them outside Canada, if any production of goods or services
    that results from the training is incidental; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} foreign nationals representing a foreign business
    or government for the purpose of selling goods for that business or
    government, if the foreign national is not engaged in making sales
    to the general public in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a foreign national
  seeks to engage in international business activities in Canada without
  directly entering the Canadian labour market only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the primary source of remuneration for the business
    activities is outside Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the principal place of business and actual place of
    accrual of profits remain predominately outside Canada.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Study Without a Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688551 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No permit required

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may study in Canada without a study permit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if they are a family member or a member of the
    private staff of a foreign representative who is properly accredited
    by the Department of Foreign Affairs and International Trade and who
    is in Canada to carry out official duties as a diplomatic agent,
    consular officer, representative or official of a country other than
    Canada, of the United Nations or any of its agencies or of any
    international organization of which Canada is a member;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as a member of the armed forces of a country that
    is a designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as a
    civilian component of those armed forces;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the duration of their course or program of
    studies is six months or less and will be completed within the
    period for their stay authorized upon entry into Canada; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if they are an Indian.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(c), a foreign national may
  apply for a study permit before entering Canada for a course or
  program of studies of a duration of six months or less.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 52(F)
- SOR/2014-14, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expired study permits

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national who
has made an application under subsection 217(1) is authorized to study
without a study permit until a decision is made on the application if
they have remained in Canada since the expiry of their study permit and
continue to comply with the conditions, other than the expiry date, set
out on the expired study permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New study permit application

**[[189.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.1 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national
who has submitted an application for a new study permit in accordance
with section 217.1 is authorized to study at the [[designated learning
institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 211.1, that
is named in that application until a decision is made on the application
if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has remained in Canada since
  they received their letter of acceptance from the designated learning
  institution named in the application;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national continues to comply with the
  conditions set out in their previous study permit, other than the
  condition that they remain enrolled at the designated learning
  institution named in that permit; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} before the foreign national completed their course or
  program of study, the designated learning institution named in the
  previous study permit

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} closed,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} discontinued the course or program of study in
    question,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} was placed on the suspension list referred to in
    subsection 222.6(1), or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} lost its designated status.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Temporary Resident Visa Exemptions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688565 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visa exemption --- nationality

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national is exempt from the requirement to obtain a temporary
  resident visa if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are a citizen of a country listed in Schedule 1.1;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a British citizen,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a British overseas citizen who is re-admissible
      to the United Kingdom, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a citizen of a British overseas territory who
      derives that citizenship through birth, descent, naturalization or
      registration in one of the British overseas territories of
      Anguilla, Bermuda, British Virgin Islands, Cayman Islands,
      Falkland Islands, Gibraltar, Montserrat, Pitcairn Island, Saint
      Helena or Turks and Caicos Islands; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are a national of the United States or a person who
    has been lawfully admitted to the United States for permanent
    residence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visa exemption --- documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is exempt from the requirement to
  obtain a temporary resident visa if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold a passport that contains a diplomatic
    acceptance, a consular acceptance or an official acceptance issued
    by the Chief of Protocol for the Department of Foreign Affairs and
    International Trade on behalf of the Government of Canada and are a
    properly accredited diplomat, consular officer, representative or
    official of a country other than Canada, of the United Nations or
    any of its agencies, or of any international organization of which
    Canada is a member;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hold a passport or travel document issued by the
    Holy See;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hold a national Israeli passport;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} hold a passport issued by the Hong Kong Special
    Administrative Region of the People's Republic of China;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} hold a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British National (Overseas), as a person born, naturalized or
    registered in Hong Kong;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} hold a passport issued by the United Kingdom to a
    British Subject which contains the observation that the holder has
    the right of abode in the United Kingdom; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} hold an ordinary passport issued by the Ministry of
    Foreign Affairs in Taiwan that includes the personal identification
    number of the individual.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visa exemption --- holders of certain
  Romanian passports

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is a citizen of Romania is
  exempt from the requirement to obtain a temporary resident visa if
  they hold a machine-readable passport that contains a contactless
  integrated circuit chip and that is issued by Romania.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visa exemption --- purpose of entry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is exempt from the requirement to
  obtain a temporary resident visa if they are seeking to enter and
  remain in Canada solely

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to an agreement between Canada and one or
    more foreign countries respecting the obligation to hold such a
    visa,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} as a member of a crew of a means of
      transportation other than a vessel or to become a member of a crew
      of a means of transportation other than a vessel, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to transit through Canada after working, or to
      work, as a member of a crew of a means of transportation other
      than a vessel, if they possess a ticket for departure from Canada
      within 24 hours after their arrival in Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to transit through Canada as a passenger on a
    flight stopping in Canada for the sole purpose of refuelling and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they are in possession of the documents required
      in order to enter the United States and their flight is bound for
      that country, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} they were lawfully admitted to the United States
      and their flight originated in that country;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} to transit through Canada as a passenger on a
    flight that, owing to an emergency or other unforeseen
    circumstances, makes an unscheduled stop in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to transit through Canada as a passenger on a
    flight if the foreign national

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is transported by a commercial transporter and
      there is a memorandum of understanding referred to in
      subsection (4) in effect between the Minister and the commercial
      transporter concerning the transit of passengers through Canada
      without a Canadian visa,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} holds a passport or travel document that was
      issued by the country of which the foreign national is a citizen
      or national and that country is listed in the memorandum of
      understanding, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is in possession of any visa required to enter
      the country of destination;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to carry out official duties as a member of the
    armed forces of a country that is a designated state for the
    purposes of the [Visiting Forces Act](/eng/acts/V-2), unless they
    have been designated under that Act as a civilian component of those
    armed forces;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2015-77, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} to re-enter Canada following a visit solely to the
    United States or St. Pierre and Miquelon, if they

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} held a study permit or a work permit that was
      issued before they left Canada on such a visit or were authorized
      to enter and remain in Canada as a temporary resident, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} return to Canada by the end of the period
      initially authorized for their stay or any extension to it;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} to conduct inspections of the flight operation
    procedures or cabin safety of a commercial air carrier operating
    international flights, if they are a civil aviation inspector of a
    national aeronautical authority and possess valid documentation to
    that effect; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} to participate as an accredited representative or
    as an adviser to an aviation accident or incident investigation
    conducted under the [Canadian Transportation Accident Investigation
    and Safety Board Act](/eng/acts/C-23.4), if they possess valid
    documentation to that effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visa exemption --- crew member

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is a member of a crew and
  who is carried to Canada by a vessel is exempt from the requirement to
  obtain a temporary resident visa if they are seeking

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to enter Canada as a member of the crew of the
    vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to remain in Canada solely as a member of the crew
    of that vessel or any other vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of memorandum of understanding

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A memorandum of understanding referred to in
  paragraph (3)(c) shall include provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the countries to which the memorandum of
    understanding applies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the scheduled flights to which the memorandum of
    understanding applies; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the commercial transporter's obligation to control
    the movement of in-transit passengers.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Visitors]{.HTitleText1} {#h-688617 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** The visitor class is
prescribed as a class of persons who may become temporary residents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visitor

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national is a
visitor and a member of the visitor class if the foreign national has
been authorized to enter and remain in Canada as a visitor.

- SOR/2011-126, s. 4(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** A visitor is subject to
the conditions imposed under Part 9.

## [PART 11]{.HLabel1}[Workers]{.HTitleText1} {#h-688632 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General Rules]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688633 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** The worker class is
prescribed as a class of persons who may become temporary residents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Worker

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national is a
worker and a member of the worker class if the foreign national has been
authorized to enter and remain in Canada as a worker.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit required

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national must
not work in Canada unless authorized to do so by a work permit or these
Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions

**[[196.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.1 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national
must not enter into an employment agreement, or extend the term of an
employment agreement, with an employer

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} who, on a regular basis, offers striptease, erotic
  dance, escort services or erotic massages; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} referred to in subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-245, s. 4
- SOR/2015-144, s. 4
- [SOR/2022-142, s. 4](#1361874-1374706){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1361874-1374706.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196.1-20151201.html "Link to previous version of section 196.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abuse

- **[[196.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this Part abuse
  consists of any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} physical abuse, including assault and forcible
    confinement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sexual abuse, including sexual contact without
    consent;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} psychological abuse, including threats and
    intimidation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} financial abuse, including fraud and extortion; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} reprisals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reprisal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1) reprisal consists
  of any measure, including the following, that is taken by or on behalf
  of an employer against a foreign national referred to in subparagraph
  200(1)(c)(ii.1) or (iii) -- and that adversely affects the foreign
  national's employment or working conditions -- because the foreign
  national has reported that the conditions set out in section 209.2 or
  209.3 have not been complied with or has in good faith cooperated with
  an inspection conducted under section 209.7, 209.8 or 209.9:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a disciplinary measure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a demotion;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a dismissal; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any threat to take any of the measures mentioned
    above.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-56, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-142, s. 5](#1361876-1374725){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1361876-1374725.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Application for Work Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688654 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application before entry

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national may
apply for a work permit at any time before entering Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application on entry

- **[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a foreign
  national may apply for a work permit when entering Canada if the
  foreign national is exempt under Division 5 of Part 9 from the
  requirement to obtain a temporary resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national may not apply for a work permit
  when entering Canada if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a determination under section 203 is required,
    unless

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Department of Employment and Social
      Development has provided an assessment under paragraph 203(2)(a)
      in respect of an offer of employment --- other than seasonal
      agricultural employment --- to the foreign national, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is a national or permanent
      resident of the United States or is a resident of Greenland or St.
      Pierre and Miquelon;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national does not hold a medical
    certificate that they are required to hold under subsection 30(3);
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is a participant in an
    international youth exchange program, unless they are a national or
    permanent resident of the United States or their application for a
    work permit was approved before their entry into Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 54
- SOR/2010-172, s. 5
- 2013, c. 40, s. 237
- SOR/2015-147, s. 1
- SOR/2016-136, s. 4
- SOR/2017-78, s. 7
- [SOR/2019-212, s. 11(F)](#1138244-1145986){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138244-1145986.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-198-20170505.html "Link to previous version of section 198"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application after entry

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national may
apply for a work permit after entering Canada if they

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold a work permit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} are working in Canada under the authority of section
  186 and are not a business visitor within the meaning of section 187;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} hold a study permit;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} hold a temporary resident permit issued under
  subsection 24(1) of the Act that is valid for at least six months;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} are a family member of a person described in any of
  paragraphs (a) to (d);

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} are in a situation described in section 206 or 207;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} applied for a work permit before entering Canada and
  the application was approved in writing but they have not been issued
  the permit;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} are applying as a trader or investor, intra-company
  transferee or professional, as described in Section B, C or D of Annex
  1603 of the Agreement, within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
  [North American Free Trade Agreement Implementation
  Act](/eng/acts/N-23.8), and their country of citizenship --- being a
  country party to that Agreement --- grants to Canadian citizens who
  submit a similar application within that country treatment equivalent
  to that accorded by Canada to citizens of that country who submit an
  application within Canada, including treatment in respect of an
  authorization for multiple entries based on a single application; or

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} hold a written statement from the Department of
  Foreign Affairs and International Trade stating that it has no
  objection to the foreign national working at a foreign mission in
  Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 55
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Issuance of Work Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688682 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permits

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3)
  --- and, in respect of a foreign national who makes an application for
  a work permit before entering Canada, subject to section 87.3 of the
  Act --- an officer shall issue a work permit to a foreign national if,
  following an examination, it is established that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national applied for it in accordance
    with Division 2;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national will leave Canada by the end
    of the period authorized for their stay under Division 2 of Part 9;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is described in section 206 or 208,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} intends to perform work described in section 204
      or 205 but does not have an offer of employment to perform that
      work or is described in section 207 or 207.1 but does not have an
      offer of employment,

    - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} intends to perform work described in section
      204 or 205 and has an offer of employment to perform that work or
      is described in section 207 and has an offer of employment, and an
      officer has determined, on the basis of any information provided
      on the officer's request by the employer making the offer and any
      other relevant information, that the offer is genuine under
      subsection (5), or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has been offered employment, and an officer has
      made a positive determination under paragraphs 203(1)(a) to (g);
      and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2004-167, s. 56\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of subsections 30(2) and (3) are
    met, if they must submit to a medical examination under paragraph
    16(2)(b) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of par. (1)(b)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(b) does not apply to a foreign national
  who satisfies the criteria set out in section 206 or paragraph 207(c)
  or (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall not issue a work permit to a foreign
  national if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds to believe that the
    foreign national is unable to perform the work sought;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who intends to
    work in the Province of Quebec and does not hold a *[Certificat
    d'acceptation du Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}*, a determination
    under section 203 is required and the laws of that Province require
    that the foreign national hold a *[Certificat d'acceptation du
    Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}*;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the work that the foreign national intends to
    perform is likely to adversely affect the settlement of any labour
    dispute in progress or the employment of any person involved in the
    dispute;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2017-78, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has engaged in unauthorized
    study or work in Canada or has failed to comply with a condition of
    a previous permit or authorization unless

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period of six months has elapsed since the
      cessation of the unauthorized work or study or failure to comply
      with a condition,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the study or work was unauthorized by reason
      only that the foreign national did not comply with conditions
      imposed under paragraph 185(a), any of subparagraphs 185(b)(i)
      to (iii) or paragraph 185(c);

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 206 applies to them; or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national was subsequently issued a
      temporary resident permit under subsection 24(1) of the Act;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national referred to in
    subparagraphs (1)(c)(i) to (iii), the issuance of a work permit
    would be inconsistent with the terms of a federal-provincial
    agreement that apply to the employment of foreign nationals;

  - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national referred to in
    subparagraph (1)(c)(ii.1), the fee referred to in section 303.1 has
    not been paid or the information referred to in section 209.11 has
    not been provided before the foreign national makes an application
    for a work permit;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2018-61, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national intends to work for an
    employer who, on a regular basis, offers striptease, erotic dance,
    escort services or erotic massages; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national intends to work for an
    employer who is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-142, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ineligible under paragraph 209.95(1)(b), or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in default of any amount payable in respect of
      an administrative monetary penalty, including if the employer
      fails to comply with a payment agreement for the payment of that
      amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of paragraph (3)(e)

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (3)(e) does not apply to a foreign
  national referred to in subsection 207.1(1) who engaged in
  unauthorized work in Canada or failed to comply with a condition of a
  previous permit or authorization.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2018-61, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Genuineness of job offer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A determination of whether an offer of employment is
  genuine shall be based on the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether the offer is made by an employer that is
    actively engaged in the business in respect of which the offer is
    made, unless the offer is made for employment as a live-in
    caregiver;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the offer is consistent with the reasonable
    employment needs of the employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the terms of the offer are terms that the
    employer is reasonably able to fulfil; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the past compliance of the employer, or any person
    who recruits the foreign national for the employer, with the federal
    or provincial laws that regulate the employment or recruitment of
    employees, including foreign nationals, in the province in which it
    is intended that the foreign national will work.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for renewal

- **[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may apply for the renewal of their work permit if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made before their work permit
    expires; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have complied with all conditions imposed on
    their entry into Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renewal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall renew the foreign national's work
  permit if, following an examination, it is established that the
  foreign national continues to meet the requirements of section 200.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-172, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-201-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 201"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary resident status

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national who
is issued a work permit under section 206 or paragraph 207(c) or (d)
does not, by reason only of being issued a work permit, become a
temporary resident.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of employment offered

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application under Division 2 for a work permit made by a foreign
  national other than a foreign national referred to in subparagraphs
  200(1)(c)(i) to (ii.1), an officer must determine, on the basis of an
  assessment provided by the Department of Employment and Social
  Development, of any information provided on the officer's request by
  the employer making the offer and of any other relevant information,
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the job offer is genuine under subsection 200(5);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employment of the foreign national is likely to
    have a neutral or positive effect on the labour market in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the issuance of a work permit would not be
    inconsistent with the terms of any federal-provincial agreement that
    apply to the employers of foreign nationals;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who seeks to
    enter Canada as a live-in caregiver,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national will reside in a private
      household in Canada and provide child care, senior home support
      care or care of a disabled person in that household without
      supervision,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer will provide the foreign national
      with adequate furnished and private accommodations in the
      household, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the employer has sufficient financial resources
      to pay the foreign national the wages that are offered to the
      foreign national;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the employer

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has not, directly or indirectly, charged or
      recovered from the foreign national the fees referred to in
      subsection 315.2(1) or any fees related to the recruitment of the
      foreign national, with the exception of the fees referred to in
      subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1) and fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national that are authorized to be
      charged or recovered under an international agreement between
      Canada and one or more countries concerning seasonal agricultural
      workers,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensured that any person who recruits the foreign
      national for the employer did not, directly or indirectly, charge
      or recover from the foreign national the fees referred to in
      subsection 315.2(1) or any fees related to the recruitment of the
      foreign national, with the exception of the fees referred to in
      subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1) and fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national that are authorized to be
      charged or recovered under an international agreement between
      Canada and one or more countries concerning seasonal agricultural
      workers;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the employer has not employed a
    foreign national referred to in subparagraph 200(1)(c)(iii) during
    the six years before the day on which the request for an assessment
    made under subsection (2) is received by the Department of
    Employment and Social Development, during the period beginning two
    years before the day on which the request for an assessment is
    received and ending on the day that an assessment is provided, when
    the employer was actively engaged in the business for which the
    employer has provided information under this subsection and
    subsection (2.1), the employer

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} made reasonable efforts to provide a workplace
      that is free of abuse, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} was not an affiliate of an employer referred to
      in subparagraphs 200(3)(h)(ii) or (iii); and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the employer committed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to conclude with the foreign national, on or
      before the first day of work of the foreign national that is
      during the period of employment for which the work permit is
      issued to them, an employment agreement that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} provides for employment in the same occupation
        and the same wages and working conditions as those set out in
        the offer of employment,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is drafted in the foreign national's chosen
        official language of Canada, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} is signed by both the employer and the foreign
        national,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to provide the foreign national, on or before
      the first day of work of the foreign national that is during the
      period of employment for which the work permit is issued to them,
      a copy of the employment agreement referred to in subparagraph
      (i),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to not, directly or indirectly, charge or
      recover from the foreign national the fees referred to in
      subsection 315.2(1) or any fees related to the recruitment of the
      foreign national, with the exception of the fees referred to in
      subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1) and fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national that are authorized to be
      charged or recovered under an international agreement between
      Canada and one or more countries concerning seasonal agricultural
      workers, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that any person who recruits the
      foreign national for the employer does not, directly or
      indirectly, charge or recover from the foreign national the fees
      referred to in subsection 315.2(1) or any fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national, with the exception of the
      fees referred to in subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1) and fees
      related to the recruitment of the foreign national that are
      authorized to be charged or recovered under an international
      agreement between Canada and one or more countries concerning
      seasonal agricultural workers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect on labour market --- language

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(b), the
  employment of a foreign national is unlikely to have a positive or
  neutral effect on the labour market in Canada if the offer of
  employment requires the ability to communicate in a language other
  than English or French, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer or group of employers demonstrates
    that the ability to communicate in the other language is a bona fide
    requirement for performing the duties associated with the
    employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offer of employment relates to work to be
    performed under an international agreement between Canada and one or
    more countries concerning seasonal agricultural workers; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the offer of employment relates to other work to be
    performed in the primary agriculture sector, within the meaning of
    subsection 315.2(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect on labour market

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(b), the
  employment of the foreign national is unlikely to have a positive or
  neutral effect on the labour market in Canada if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the wages set out in the offer of employment are
    not consistent with the prevailing wage rate for the occupation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employment of the foreign national is likely to
    adversely affect the settlement of any labour dispute in progress or
    the employment of any person involved in the dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment on request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2.02), the Department of
  Employment and Social Development must provide the assessment referred
  to in subsection (1) on the request of an officer or an employer or
  group of employers, none of whom is an employer who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on a regular basis, offers striptease, erotic
    dance, escort services or erotic massages; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is referred to in subparagraph 200(3)(h)(ii) or
    (iii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer of employment

  [(2.01)]{.lawlabel} A request may be made in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offer of employment to a foreign national; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} offers of employment made, or anticipated to be
    made, by an employer or group of employers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of processing of requests

  [(2.02)]{.lawlabel} If any of the circumstances set out in section
  209.5 exist, the processing of a request for an assessment under
  subsection (2) is suspended so long as the Department of Employment
  and Social Development has a reason to suspect that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer who made the request is not complying
    with or has not complied with any of the conditions set out in
    subparagraph 209.3(1)(a)(i), 209.3(1)(a)(iv) with regard to the
    working conditions referred to in that subparagraph, 209.3(1)(a)(v)
    or 209.3(1)(a)(vii) to 209.3(1)(a)(xii); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer's failure to comply with any of the
    conditions referred to in paragraph (a) would put at serious risk
    the health or safety of the foreign national, if the work permit was
    issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Basis of Assessment

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The assessment provided by the Department of
  Employment and Social Development on the matters set out in paragraphs
  (1)(a) to (g) must be based on any information provided by the
  employer making the offer and any other relevant information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors --- effect on labour market

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assessment provided by the Department of
  Employment and Social Development with respect to the matters referred
  to in paragraph (1)(b) must, unless the employment of the foreign
  national is unlikely to have a positive or neutral effect on the
  labour market in Canada as a result of the application of subsection
  (1.01) or (1.1) be based on the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether the employment of the foreign national will
    or is likely to result in direct job creation or job retention for
    Canadian citizens or permanent residents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the employment of the foreign national will
    or is likely to result in the development or transfer of skills and
    knowledge for the benefit of Canadian citizens or permanent
    residents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the employment of the foreign national is
    likely to fill a labour shortage;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the working conditions offered to the
    foreign national meet generally accepted Canadian standards;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the employer will hire or train Canadian
    citizens or permanent residents or has made, or has agreed to make,
    reasonable efforts to do so;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-142, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} whether the employer has fulfilled or has made
    reasonable efforts to fulfill any commitments made, in the context
    of any assessment that was previously provided under subsection (2),
    with respect to the matters referred to in paragraphs (a), (b) and
    (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of validity of assessment

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} An assessment provided by the Department of
  Employment and Social Development shall indicate the period during
  which the assessment is in effect for the purposes of subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Province of Quebec

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign national who intends to work
  in the Province of Quebec, the assessment provided by the Department
  of Employment and Social Development shall be made in concert with the
  competent authority of that Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affiliate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, an affiliate
  includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an employer that is controlled by another employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two employers that are under common control; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} employers that are not operated at arm's length.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (5), control, whether
  direct or indirect, exercised or not, includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} common ownership;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} common management;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} common interests;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} shared facilities or equipment; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} common use of services of employees.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements or arrangements

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** A work permit may be
issued under section 200 to a foreign national who intends to perform
work under

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an agreement or arrangement between Canada and the
  government of a foreign state or an international organization, other
  than an agreement or arrangement concerning seasonal agricultural
  workers;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an agreement entered into by one or more countries
  and by or on behalf of one or more provinces;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an agreement entered into by the Minister with a
  province or group of provinces under subsection 8(1) of the Act; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a youth mobility agreement or arrangement between
  Canada and the government of a foreign state or the competent
  authority of any other foreign territory, or between Canada and an
  international or domestic organization, that would create or maintain
  reciprocal employment for Canadian citizens in a foreign state or
  territory.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-26, s. 2
- [SOR/2024-240, s. 2](#1496880-1497825){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1496880-1497825.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20180227.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian interests

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** A work permit may be
issued under section 200 to a foreign national who intends to perform
work that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} would create or maintain significant social, cultural
  or economic benefits or opportunities for Canadian citizens or
  permanent residents;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} would create or maintain reciprocal employment of
  Canadian citizens or permanent residents of Canada in other countries;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is designated by the Minister as being work that can
  be performed by a foreign national on the basis of the following
  criteria, namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the work is related to a research program,

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} the work is an essential part of a post-secondary
    academic, vocational or professional training program offered by a
    designated learning institution as defined in section 211.1,

  - [(i.2)]{.lawlabel} the work is an essential part of a program at the
    secondary level

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} that is a vocational training program offered by
      a designated learning institution in Quebec, or

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} that is a program offered by a designated
      learning institution that requires students to work in order to
      obtain their secondary or high school diploma or certificate of
      graduation, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} limited access to the Canadian labour market is
    necessary for reasons of public policy relating to the
    competitiveness of Canada's academic institutions or economy; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is of a religious or charitable nature.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 205"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No other means of support

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  work permit may be issued under section 200 to a foreign national in
  Canada who cannot support themself without working, if the foreign
  national

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has made a claim for refugee protection that has
    been referred to the Refugee Protection Division but has not been
    determined; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is subject to an unenforceable removal order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a work permit must not be
  issued to a claimant referred to in subsection 111.1(2) of the Act
  unless at least 180 days have elapsed since their claim was referred
  to the Refugee Protection Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-252, s. 2
- SOR/2014-139, s. 6(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicants in Canada

**[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** A work permit may be
issued under section 200 to a foreign national in Canada who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2017-78, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a member of the spouse or common-law partner in
  Canada class set out in Division 2 of Part 7;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a protected person within the meaning of
  subsection 95(2) of the Act;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} has applied to become a permanent resident and the
  Minister has granted them an exemption under subsection 25(1), 25.1(1)
  or 25.2(1) of the Act; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} is a family member of a person described in any of
  paragraphs (a) to (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-252, s. 3
- SOR/2017-78, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20101104.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vulnerable workers

- **[[207.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A work permit may be issued under
  section 200 to a foreign national in Canada if there are reasonable
  grounds to believe that the foreign national is experiencing or is at
  risk of experiencing abuse in the context of their employment in
  Canada and if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold a work permit issued under subparagraph
    200(1)(c)(ii.1) or (iii); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} previously held a work permit issued under
    subparagraph 200(1)(c)(ii.1) or (iii), have applied for a renewal of
    that permit under subsection 201(1) and are authorized to work in
    Canada under paragraph 186(u).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family member of vulnerable worker

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A work permit may be issued under section 200 to a
  foreign national in Canada who is a family member of a person
  described in paragraph (1)(a) or (b).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-148, s. 2](#1130251-1138143){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1130251-1138143.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Humanitarian reasons

**[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** A work permit may be
issued under section 200 to a foreign national in Canada who cannot
support themself without working, if the foreign national

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} holds a study permit and has become temporarily
  destitute through circumstances beyond their control and beyond the
  control of any person on whom that person is dependent for the
  financial support to complete their term of study; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} holds a temporary resident permit issued under
  subsection 24(1) of the Act that is valid for at least six months.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 58
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** A work permit becomes
invalid when it expires or when it is cancelled under section 209.01,
209.02 or 243.2.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-11, s. 2](#1441340-1448678){.wb-lbx

  ajax-after="1508533-1508764.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-209-20240315.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation due to error

**[[209.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.01 .sectionLabel}** An officer may
cancel a work permit that was issued to a foreign national if the
officer is satisfied that the work permit was issued based on an
administrative error.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 5](#1508533-1508770){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508533-1508770.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

**[[209.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.02 .sectionLabel}** A work permit
that was issued to a foreign national is cancelled if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national becomes a permanent resident; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is deceased.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 5](#1508533-1508776){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508533-1508776.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Conditions Imposed on Employers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-688843 .Subheading}

Definition of [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[209.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means anything on which
information that is capable of being understood by a person, or read by
a computer or other device, is recorded or marked.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-245, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign national --- subparagraph
200(1)(c)(ii.1)

- **[[209.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer who has made an offer of
  employment to a foreign national referred to in subparagraph
  200(1)(c)(ii.1) must, before the foreign national makes an application
  for a work permit in respect of that employment, provide the following
  information to the Minister using the electronic means that is made
  available or specified by the Minister for that purpose:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their name, address and telephone number and their
    fax number and electronic mail address, if any;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the business number assigned to the employer by the
    Minister of National Revenue, if applicable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} information that demonstrates that the foreign
    national will be performing work described in section 204 or 205 or
    is a foreign national described in section 207;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the offer of employment made in the form
    made available by the Department; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an attestation that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the employer has entered into an employment
      agreement with the foreign national that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} provides for employment in the same occupation
        and the same wages and working conditions as those set out in
        the offer of employment,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is drafted in the foreign national's chosen
        official language of Canada, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} is signed by both the employer and the foreign
        national,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer has provided a copy of the
      employment agreement referred to in subparagraph (i) to the
      foreign national,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the employer has not, directly or indirectly,
      charged or recovered from the foreign national the fee referred to
      in subsection 303.1(1) or any fees related to the recruitment of
      the foreign national, with the exception of the fees referred to
      in subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1), and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the employer has ensured that any person who
      recruited the foreign national for the employer did not, directly
      or indirectly, charge or recover from the foreign national the fee
      referred to in subsection 303.1(1) or any fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national, with the exception of the
      fees referred to in subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2019-174, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2019-174, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign national referred to in
subparagraph 200(1)(c)(ii.1)

- **[[209.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer who has made an offer of
  employment to a foreign national referred to in subparagraph
  200(1)(c)(ii.1) must comply with the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during the period of employment for which the work
    permit is issued to the foreign national,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the employer must be actively engaged in the
      business in respect of which the offer of employment was made,
      unless the offer was made for employment as a live-in caregiver,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer must comply with the federal or
      provincial laws that regulate the employment or recruitment of
      employees, including foreign nationals, in the province in which
      the foreign national works,

    - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make available to the
      foreign national, in both official languages of Canada, the
      information referred to in paragraph (a.1),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} subject to subparagraph (vii), the employer
      must provide the foreign national with employment in the same
      occupation as that set out in the foreign national's offer of
      employment and with wages and working conditions that are
      substantially the same as --- but not less favourable than ---
      those set out in that offer,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make reasonable efforts to
      provide a workplace that is free of abuse;

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who is subject to an order or
      regulation made under the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or
      the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), must not do anything that
      prevents the foreign national from complying with the requirements
      of the order or regulation, including requiring the foreign
      national to do anything that is contrary to those requirements,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who is subject to a provincial law that
      governs public health in response to COVID-19, must not do
      anything that prevents the foreign national from complying with
      the requirements of the law, including requiring the foreign
      national to do anything that is contrary to the law,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who, in accordance with an order made
      under section 58 of the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), is
      required to isolate or quarantine themselves for a period on entry
      into Canada, must provide the foreign national with wages during
      that period that are substantially the same as those set out in
      the offer of employment; and

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make reasonable efforts to
      provide access to health care services when the foreign national
      is injured or becomes ill at the workplace,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} the employer must not, directly or indirectly,
      charge or recover from the foreign national the fee referred to in
      subsection 303.1(1) or any fees related to the recruitment of the
      foreign national, with the exception of the fees referred to in
      subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1), and

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure that any person who
      recruited the foreign national for the employer does not, directly
      or indirectly, charge or recover from the foreign national the fee
      referred to in subsection 303.1(1) or any fees related to the
      recruitment of the foreign national, with the exception of the
      fees referred to in subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1); and

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} on or before the foreign national's first day of
    work that is during the period of employment for which the work
    permit is issued to the foreign national, the employer must provide
    the foreign national with a copy in their chosen official language
    of Canada of the most recent information with respect to the foreign
    national's rights in Canada that is made available by the Government
    of Canada for that purpose; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during a period of six years beginning on the first
    day of the period of employment for which the work permit is issued
    to the foreign national, the employer must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be able to demonstrate that any information they
      provided under subparagraph 200(1)(c)(ii.1) or section 209.11 was
      accurate, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} retain any document that relates to compliance
      with the conditions set out in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of employment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the period of
  employment for which the work permit is issued includes any period
  during which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national may, under paragraph 186(u),
    work in Canada without a permit after the expiry of their work
    permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is required to isolate or
    quarantine themselves on entry into Canada in accordance with an
    order made under the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with any of the conditions set
  out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(i) to (viii) and paragraph (1)(a.1) is
  justified if it results from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change in federal or provincial law;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a change to the provisions of a collective
    agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of measures by the employer in
    response to a dramatic change in economic conditions that directly
    affected the business of the employer, provided that the measures
    were not directed disproportionately at foreign nationals employed
    by the employer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an error in interpretation made in good faith by
    the employer with respect to its obligations to a foreign national,
    if the employer subsequently provided compensation --- or if it was
    not possible to provide compensation, made sufficient efforts to do
    so --- to all foreign nationals who suffered a disadvantage as a
    result of the error;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an accounting or administrative error made by the
    employer, if the employer subsequently provided compensation --- or
    if it was not possible to provide compensation, made sufficient
    efforts to do so --- to all foreign nationals who suffered a
    disadvantage as a result of the error;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} circumstances similar to those set out in
    paragraphs (a) to (e);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} superior force; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an error in interpretation made in good faith by
    the employer with respect to its compliance with the conditions set
    out in any of subparagraphs (1)(a)(v) and (vi).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with any of the conditions set
  out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(ix) and (x) is justified if the employer

  - [a)]{.lawlabel} made all reasonable efforts to comply with the
    conditions; and

  - [b)]{.lawlabel} subsequently provided full compensation to the
    foreign national for the fees incorrectly charged or recovered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with either of the conditions set
  out in paragraph (1)(b) is justified if the employer made all
  reasonable efforts to comply with the condition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign national referred to in
subparagraph 200(1)(c)(iii)

- **[[209.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer who has made an offer of
  employment to a foreign national referred to in subparagraph
  200(1)(c)(iii) must comply with the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during the period of employment for which the work
    permit is issued to the foreign national,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the employer must be actively engaged in the
      business in respect of which the offer of employment was made,
      unless the offer was made for employment as a live-in caregiver,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer must comply with the federal or
      provincial laws that regulate the employment or recruitment of
      employees, including foreign nationals, in the province in which
      the foreign national works,

    - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make available to the
      foreign national, in both official languages of Canada, the
      information referred to in paragraph (a.1),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national as a live-in caregiver, must

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the foreign national resides in a
        private household in Canada and provides child care, senior home
        support care or care of a disabled person in that household
        without supervision,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} provide the foreign national with adequate
        furnished and private accommodations in the household, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} have sufficient financial resources to pay the
        foreign national the wages that were offered to the foreign
        national,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} subject to subparagraph (xii), the employer must
      provide the foreign national with employment in the same
      occupation as that set out in the foreign national's offer of
      employment and with wages and working conditions that are
      substantially the same as --- but not less favourable than ---
      those set out in that offer,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make reasonable efforts to
      provide a workplace that is free of abuse,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national to perform work under an international
      agreement between Canada and one or more countries concerning
      seasonal agricultural workers, must provide the foreign national
      with adequate accommodations,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who is subject to an order or
      regulation made under the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or
      the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), must not do anything that
      prevents the foreign national from complying with the requirements
      of the order or regulation, including requiring the foreign
      national to do anything that is contrary to those requirements,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who is subject to a provincial law that
      governs public health in response to COVID-19, must not do
      anything that prevents the foreign national from complying with
      the requirements of the law, including requiring the foreign
      national to do anything that is contrary to the law,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      provides accommodations to a foreign national, must --- during any
      period that the foreign national is required to quarantine
      themselves in accordance with an order or regulation made under
      the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine
      Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1) --- provide the foreign national with
      accommodations that are separate from those provided to persons
      who are not in quarantine and that permit the foreign worker to
      remain at least two metres away from any other person,

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      provides accommodations to a foreign national who is subject to an
      order or regulation made under the [Emergencies
      Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1),
      must provide the foreign national with cleaning products for the
      purposes of cleaning and disinfecting the accommodations
      regularly,

    - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      provides accommodations to a foreign national who becomes infected
      with or develops any signs or symptoms of COVID-19, must provide
      the foreign national with accommodations that have a bedroom and a
      bathroom that are solely for the use of the foreign national while
      they isolate themselves,

    - [(xii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, in the case of an employer who
      employs a foreign national who, in accordance with an order made
      under section 58 of the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), is
      required to isolate or quarantine themselves for a period on entry
      into Canada, must provide the foreign national with wages during
      that period that are substantially the same as those set out in
      the offer of employment,

    - [(xiii)]{.lawlabel} the employer, except in the case of an
      employer who employs a foreign national to perform work under an
      international agreement between Canada and one or more countries
      concerning seasonal agricultural workers and the agreement
      includes health insurance, must obtain and pay for private health
      insurance that covers emergency medical care, for any period
      during which the foreign national is not covered by the applicable
      provincial health insurance system,

    - [(xiv)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make reasonable efforts to
      provide access to health care services when the foreign national
      is injured or becomes ill at the workplace,

    - [(xv)]{.lawlabel} the employer must not, directly or indirectly,
      charge or recover from the foreign national the fees referred to
      in subparagraph (xiii) or subsection 315.2(1) or any fees related
      to the recruitment of the foreign national, with the exception of
      the fees referred to in subsections 296(1), 298(1) and 299(1) and
      fees related to the recruitment of the foreign national that are
      authorized to be charged or recovered under an international
      agreement between Canada and one or more countries concerning
      seasonal agricultural workers, and

    - [(xvi)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure that any person who
      recruited the foreign national for the employer does not, directly
      or indirectly, charge or recover from the foreign national the
      fees referred to in subparagraph (xiii), subsection 315.2(1) or
      any fees related to the recruitment of the foreign national, with
      the exception of the fees referred to in subsections 296(1),
      298(1) and 299(1) and fees related to the recruitment of the
      foreign national that are authorized to be charged or recovered
      under an international agreement between Canada and one or more
      countries concerning seasonal agricultural workers;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} on or before the foreign national's first day of
    work that is during the period of employment for which the work
    permit is issued to the foreign national, the employer must provide
    the foreign national with a copy in their chosen official language
    of Canada of the most recent information with respect to the foreign
    national's rights in Canada that is made available by the Government
    of Canada for that purpose; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during the period of employment for which the work
    permit is issued to the foreign national or any other period that
    was agreed on by the employer and the Department of Employment and
    Social Development at the time the assessment referred to in
    subsection 203(2) was provided,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure that the employment of
      the foreign national will result in direct job creation or job
      retention for Canadian citizens or permanent residents, if that
      was one of the factors that led to the issuance of the work
      permit,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure that the employment of
      the foreign national will result in the development or transfer of
      skills and knowledge for the benefit of Canadian citizens or
      permanent residents, if that was one of the factors that led to
      the issuance of the work permit,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the employer must hire or train Canadian
      citizens or permanent residents, if that was one of the factors
      that led to the issuance of the work permit, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the employer must make reasonable efforts to
      hire or train Canadian citizens or permanent residents, if that
      was one of the factors that led to the issuance of the work
      permit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} during a period of six years beginning on the first
    day of the period of employment for which the work permit is issued
    to the foreign national, the employer must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be able to demonstrate that any information they
      provided under subsections 203(1) and (2.1) was accurate, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} retain any document that relates to compliance
      with the conditions set out in paragraphs (a) and (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of employment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the period of
  employment for which the work permit is issued includes any period
  during which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national may, under paragraph 186(u),
    work in Canada without a permit after the expiry of their work
    permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is required to isolate or
    quarantine themselves on entry into Canada in accordance with an
    order made under the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with any of the conditions set
  out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(i) to (xiv) and paragraphs (1)(a.1)
  and (b) is justified if it results from a:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change in federal or provincial law;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a change to the provisions of a collective
    agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of measures by the employer in
    response to a dramatic change in economic conditions that directly
    affected the business of the employer, provided that the measures
    were not directed disproportionately at foreign nationals employed
    by the employer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an error in interpretation made in good faith by
    the employer with respect to its obligations to a foreign national,
    if the employer subsequently provided compensation --- or if it was
    not possible to provide compensation, made sufficient efforts to do
    so --- to all foreign nationals who suffered a disadvantage as a
    result of the error;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an accounting or administrative error made by the
    employer, if the employer subsequently provided compensation --- or
    if it was not possible to provide compensation, made sufficient
    efforts to do so --- to all foreign nationals who suffered a
    disadvantage as a result of the error;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} circumstances similar to those set out in
    paragraphs (a) to (e);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} superior force; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an error in interpretation made in good faith by
    the employer with respect to its compliance with the conditions set
    out in any of subparagraphs (1)(a)(vii) to (xi).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with either of the conditions set
  out in paragraph (1)(c) is justified if the employer made all
  reasonable efforts to comply with the condition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with any of the conditions set
  out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(xv) and (xvi) is justified if the employer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} made all reasonable efforts to comply with the
    condition; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsequently provided full compensation to the
    foreign national for the fees that were incorrectly charged or
    recovered.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions imposed on all employers

- **[[209.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer referred to in section
  209.2 or 209.3 must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} report at any specified time and place to answer
    questions and provide documents, in accordance with section 209.6;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide any documents that are required under
    paragraphs 209.7(1)(a) and (b); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} attend any inspection referred to in section 209.8
    or 209.9, unless the employer was not notified of it, give all
    reasonable assistance to the person conducting that inspection and
    provide that person with any document or information that the person
    requires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with any of the conditions set
  out in subsection (1) is justified if the employer made all reasonable
  efforts to comply with the condition or if it results from anything
  done or omitted to be done by the employer in good faith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances for exercise of powers --
sections 209.6 to 209.9

**[[209.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.5 .sectionLabel}** The powers set out
in sections 209.6 to 209.9 may be exercised in the following
circumstances:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer or the Minister of Employment and Social
  Development has a reason to suspect that the employer is not complying
  or has not complied with any of the conditions set out in section
  209.2 or 209.3;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer has not complied with any of the
  conditions set out in section 209.2 or 209.3 in the past;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employer is chosen as part of a random
  verification of compliance with the conditions set out in sections
  209.2 or 209.3;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an officer or the Minister of Employment and Social
  Development is notified that there has been the introduction or spread
  of a [[communicable disease]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
  section 2 of the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), at any premises or
  place in which a foreign national performs work; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the employer employs a foreign national who is or was
  subject to an order or regulation made under the [Emergencies
  Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answering questions and providing
documents

- **[[209.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If any of the circumstances set out
  in section 209.5 exists,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer may, for the purpose of verifying
    compliance with the conditions set out in section 209.2, require an
    employer to report at any specified time and place to answer
    questions and provide documents that relate to compliance with those
    conditions; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister of Employment and Social Development
    may, for the purpose of verifying compliance with the conditions set
    out in section 209.3, require an employer to report at any specified
    time and place to answer questions and provide documents that relate
    to compliance with those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister of Employment and Social
  Development

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Employment and Social Development may
  exercise the powers set out in paragraph (1)(a) on the request of an
  officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of documents

- **[[209.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If any of the circumstances set out
  in section 209.5 exists,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer may, for the purpose of verifying
    compliance with the conditions set out in section 209.2, require an
    employer to provide them with any document that relates to
    compliance with those conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister of Employment and Social Development
    may, for the purpose of verifying compliance with the conditions set
    out in section 209.3, require an employer to provide him or her with
    any document that relates to compliance with those conditions; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an officer may, for the purpose of verifying
    compliance with the conditions set out in section 209.2, require any
    individual or entity to provide them with any document in their
    possession that relates to the employer's compliance with those
    conditions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister of Employment and Social Development
    may, for the purpose of verifying compliance with the conditions set
    out in section 209.3, require any individual or entity to provide
    them with any document in their possession that relates to the
    employer's compliance with those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister of Employment and Social
  Development

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Employment and Social Development may
  exercise the powers set out in paragraphs (1)(a) and (c) on the
  request of an officer.
