[Previous
Version](section-209.997-20151201.html "Link to previous version of section 209.997"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 12]{.HLabel1}[Students]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689169 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General Rules]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689170 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** The student class is
prescribed as a class of persons who may become temporary residents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Student

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national is a
student and a member of the student class if the foreign national has
been authorized to enter and remain in Canada as a student.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[designated learning
institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[211.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[designated learning institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following learning institutions:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a learning institution that is administered by a
    federal department or agency,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if a province has entered into an agreement or
    arrangement with the Minister in respect of post-secondary learning
    institutions in Canada that host international students, a
    post-secondary learning institution located in the province that is
    designated by the province for the purposes of these Regulations on
    the basis that the institution meets provincial requirements in
    respect of the delivery of education,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if a province has entered into an agreement or
    arrangement with the Minister in respect of primary or secondary
    learning institutions in Canada that host international students, a
    primary or secondary learning institution located in the province
    that is designated by the province for the purposes of these
    Regulations on the basis that the institution meets provincial
    requirements in respect of the delivery of education, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} if a province has not entered into an agreement or
    arrangement with the Minister in respect of primary or secondary
    learning institutions in Canada that host international students,
    any primary or secondary level learning institution in the province;
    and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of Quebec, the following additional
  learning institutions:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any educational institution within the meaning of
    section 36 of the Education Act of Quebec, R.S.Q. c. I-13.3,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any college established in accordance with section
    2 of the General and Vocational Colleges Act of Quebec, R.S.Q. c.
    29,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any private educational institution for which a
    permit is issued under section 10 of the Act respecting private
    education of Quebec, R.S.Q. c. E-9.1,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any educational institution operated under an Act
    of Quebec by a government department or a body that is a mandatary
    of the province,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the Conservatoire de musique et d'art dramatique du
    Québec established by the Act respecting the Conservatoire de
    musique et d'art dramatique du Québec of Quebec, R.S.Q. c. C-62.1,
    and

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any educational institution at the university
    level referred to in section 1 of the Act respecting educational
    institutions at the university level, R.S.Q. c. E-14.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of provinces

**[[211.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211.2 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
publish a list of those provinces with which the Minister has entered
into an agreement or arrangement in respect of learning institutions
that host international students.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

**[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national may
not study in Canada unless authorized to do so by the Act, a study
permit or these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Application for Study Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689200 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application before entry

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections 214
and 215, in order to study in Canada, a foreign national shall apply for
a study permit before entering Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application on entry

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national may
apply for a study permit when entering Canada if they are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a national or a permanent resident of the United
  States;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who has been lawfully admitted to the United
  States for permanent residence;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a resident of Greenland; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a resident of St. Pierre and Miquelon.

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-14, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 10
- [SOR/2019-212, s. 11(F)](#1138244-1145987){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138244-1145987.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application after entry

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may apply for a study permit after entering Canada if
  they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold a study permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} apply within the period beginning 90 days before
    the expiry of their authorization to engage in studies in Canada
    under subsection 30(2) of the Act, or paragraph 188(1)(a) of these
    Regulations, and ending 90 days after that expiry;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hold a work permit;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} are subject to an unenforceable removal order;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} hold a temporary resident permit issued under
    subsection 24(1) of the Act that is valid for at least six months;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} are a temporary resident who

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is studying at the preschool, primary or
      secondary level,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is a visiting or exchange student who is
      studying at a designated learning institution, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has completed a course or program of study that
      is a prerequisite to their enrolling at a designated learning
      institution; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} are in a situation described in section 207.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Family members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A family member of a foreign national may apply for a
  study permit after entering Canada if the foreign national resides in
  Canada and the foreign national

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holds a study permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} holds a work permit;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds a temporary resident permit issued under
    subsection 24(1) of the Act that is valid for at least six months;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is subject to an unenforceable removal order;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} is a member of the armed forces of a country that
    is a designated state described in paragraph 186(d);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} is an officer of a foreign government described in
    paragraph 186(e);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} is a participant in sports activities or events, as
    described in paragraph 186(h);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} is an employee of a foreign news company as
    described in paragraph 186(i); or

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a person who is responsible for assisting a
    congregation or group, as described in paragraph 186(l).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 215"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement --- post-secondary
institution

**[[215.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215.1 .sectionLabel}** An application for
a study permit that names a designated learning institution that is a
post-secondary institution must be returned to the applicant without
being processed, along with all documents submitted in support of the
application and the fee for processing it, if that designated learning
institution does not provide the confirmation referred to in paragraph
222.1(1)(a) to the Minister in accordance with that paragraph, subject
to any extension granted under subsection 222.1(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 3](#1494617-1494877){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494617-1494877.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Issuance of Study Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689238 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Study permits

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  an officer shall issue a study permit to a foreign national if,
  following an examination, it is established that the foreign national

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applied for it in accordance with this Part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} will leave Canada by the end of the period
    authorized for their stay under Division 2 of Part 9;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of this Part;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of subsections 30(2) and
    (3), if they must submit to a medical examination under paragraph
    16(2)(b) of the Act; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} has been accepted to undertake a course or program
    of study at a designated learning institution and, in the case of a
    designated learning institution that is a post-secondary
    institution, that designated learning institution has provided the
    confirmation referred to in paragraph 222.1(1)(a) to the Minister in
    accordance with that paragraph, subject to any extension granted
    under subsection 222.1(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(b) does not apply to persons described
  in section 206 and paragraphs 207(c) and (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Study in Quebec

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall not issue a study permit to a
  foreign national who intends to study in the Province of Quebec ---
  other than under a federal assistance program for developing countries
  --- and does not hold a *[Certificat d'acceptation du
  Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}*, if the laws of that Province require
  that the foreign national hold a *[Certificat d'acceptation du
  Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}*.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 59
- SOR/2012-154, s. 11
- SOR/2014-14, s. 12
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 4](#1494619-1494879){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494619-1494879.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for renewal

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may apply for the renewal of their study permit if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made before the expiry of their
    study permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have complied with all conditions imposed on
    their entry into Canada.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-14, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renewal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer shall renew the foreign national's study
  permit if, following an examination, it is established that the
  foreign national continues to meet the requirements of section 216.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 60
- SOR/2014-14, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of designated learning institution

**[[217.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217.1 .sectionLabel}** If the holder of a
study permit in Canada whose permit names a designated learning
institution is accepted to undertake a course or program of study at a
different designated learning institution and intends to attend that new
institution, they must submit an application for a new study permit that
names the new designated learning institution.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 5](#1494621-1494883){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494621-1494883.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary resident status

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national
referred to in paragraph 215(1)(d) and their family members do not, by
reason only of being issued a study permit, become temporary residents.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Restrictions on Studying in Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689265 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation of acceptance

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  study permit must not be issued to a foreign national unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application for a study permit
    that names a designated learning institution that is a
    post-secondary institution, that designated learning institution has
    provided confirmation to the Minister in accordance with paragraph
    222.1(1)(a), subject to any extension granted under subsection
    222.1(2), that the designated learning institution has accepted the
    foreign national to the course or program of study that is indicated
    in the application for the study permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the foreign national has
    provided written documentation from the designated learning
    institution where they intend to study that states that they have
    been accepted to study there.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a foreign national
  who applies for a study permit before entering Canada if they are an
  accompanying family member of a foreign national whose application for
  a work permit or a study permit is approved in writing before their
  entry into Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 61
- SOR/2014-14, s. 14
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 6](#1494623-1494891){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494623-1494891.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-219-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 219"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Financial resources

**[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** An officer shall not
issue a study permit to a foreign national, other than one described in
paragraph 215(1)(d) or (e), unless they have sufficient and available
financial resources, without working in Canada, to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay the tuition fees for the course or program of
  studies that they intend to pursue;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} maintain themself and any family members who are
  accompanying them during their proposed period of study; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} pay the costs of transporting themself and the family
  members referred to in paragraph (b) to and from Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- study permit holder

- **[[220.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a study permit in
  Canada is subject to the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they shall enroll at the designated learning
    institution that is named in their permit and remain enrolled there
    until they complete their studies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they shall actively pursue their course or program
    of study.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event that the learning institution at which
  the holder of a study permit is enrolled loses its designated status
  after the issuance of the permit by virtue of any of the following
  events, subsection (1) shall apply to that holder for the duration of
  their permit as if the learning institution at which they are enrolled
  continues to be a designated learning institution:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} termination of an agreement or arrangement between
    the province and the Minister in respect of learning institutions
    that host international students under which the learning
    institution had been designated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the coming into force of an agreement or
    arrangement between the province and the Minister in respect of
    learning institutions that host international students under which
    the learning institution no longer qualifies for designation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} revocation of the designation by the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person described in any of paragraphs 300(2)(a)
    to (i); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a family member of a foreign national who resides
    in Canada and is described in any of paragraphs 215(2)(a) to (i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of compliance with conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a study permit must provide evidence to
  an officer of their compliance with the conditions set out in
  subsection (1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer requests the evidence because the
    officer has reason to believe that the permit holder is not
    complying or has not complied with one or more of the conditions; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the officer requests the evidence as part of a
    random assessment of the overall level of compliance with those
    conditions by permit holders who are or were subject to them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 15
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 7](#1494625-1494893){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494625-1494893.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-220.1-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 220.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with conditions

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** Despite Division 2, a
study permit shall not be issued to a foreign national who has engaged
in unauthorized work or study in Canada or who has failed to comply with
a condition of a permit unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a period of six months has elapsed since the
  cessation of the unauthorized work or study or failure to comply with
  a condition;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the work or study was unauthorized by reason only
  that the foreign national did not comply with conditions imposed under
  paragraph 185(a), any of subparagraphs 185(b)(i) to (iii) or paragraph
  185(c); or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national was subsequently issued a
  temporary resident permit under subsection 24(1) of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 62
:::

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Validity and Expiry of Study Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689308 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  study permit becomes invalid upon the first to occur of the following
  days:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day that is 90 days after the day on which the
    permit holder completes their studies,

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the permit holder is no longer
    enrolled at the designated learning institution that is named in the
    permit, other than as a result of completing their studies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the study permit is cancelled
    under section 222.7, 222.8 or 243.2; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the permit expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person described in any of paragraphs 300(2)(a)
    to (i); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a family member of a foreign national who resides
    in Canada and is described in any of paragraphs 215(2)(a) to (i).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-14, s. 16
- [SOR/2024-11, s. 3](#1441342-1448680){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1441342-1448680.html"}
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 8](#1494627-1494913){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494627-1494913.html"}
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 6](#1508535-1508777){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508535-1508777.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20241108.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Conditions Imposed on Post-Secondary Designated Learning Institutions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1494894 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

- **[[222.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A designated learning institution
  that is a post-secondary institution must comply with the following
  conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must, using the electronic means that are made
    available or specified by the Minister for that purpose, within 10
    days after the day on which it is requested to do so by the
    Minister, confirm whether the designated learning institution has
    accepted a foreign national to the course or program of study that
    is indicated in their application for a study permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it must, using the electronic means that are made
    available or specified by the Minister for that purpose, within 60
    days after the day on which it is requested to do so by the
    Minister, provide a compliance report that sets out the enrolment
    status of each foreign national who has been accepted to the
    designated learning institution;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it must, using the electronic means that are made
    available or specified by the Minister for that purpose, within 10
    days after the day on which it is requested to do so by the
    Minister, provide any additions or corrections to the information in
    the compliance report; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it must, using the electronic means that are made
    available or specified by the Minister for that purpose, within 10
    days after the day on which it is requested to do so by the
    Minister, provide any further information that the Minister requires
    in relation to study permits or applications for study permits that
    name the designated learning institution, or otherwise in relation
    to the administration of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on request from a designated
  learning institution or on their own initiative, extend the time
  within which a confirmation, a report or information must be provided
  under this section if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a confirmation required under
    paragraph (1)(a), the Minister determines that the designated
    learning institution's provision of that confirmation has been
    prevented or impeded by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a prolonged failure of power, communications or
      other infrastructure systems,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a natural disaster,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a public health emergency, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a labour dispute; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a report required under paragraph
    (1)(b) or information required under paragraph (1)(c) or (d), the
    Minister determines that the designated learning institution's
    provision of the report or information has been prevented or impeded
    by exceptional circumstances.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1494930){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1494930.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification of compliance with
conditions

- **[[222.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An officer may verify a designated
  learning institution's compliance with the conditions set out in
  subsection 222.1(1) in the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer has a reason to suspect that the
    designated learning institution is not complying or has not complied
    with those conditions, including by providing inaccurate
    information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the officer has a reason to suspect that a letter
    of acceptance that purports to be from the designated learning
    institution was improperly issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the designated learning institution is chosen as
    part of a random verification of compliance with the conditions; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the designated learning institution has not
    complied with those conditions in the past.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents and questions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The officer may, for the purpose of verifying
  compliance with the conditions, require the designated learning
  institution to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide any relevant documents; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make a representative available to respond to
    questions at a time and by a means specified by the officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A failure to comply with a condition is justified if
  the designated learning institution made all reasonable efforts to
  comply with it or if the failure results from anything done or omitted
  to be done by the designated learning institution in good faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1494945){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1494945.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of preliminary finding

- **[[222.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An officer who determines that a
  designated learning institution has failed to comply with a condition
  set out in subsection 222.1(1) must, unless they are satisfied that
  the failure is justified, issue to the designated learning institution
  a notice of preliminary finding that sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the designated learning institution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the condition with which the designated learning
    institution failed to comply;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the details of the designated learning
    institution's failure to comply with the condition;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the officer recommends that
    the designated learning institution be placed on the suspension list
    referred to in subsection 222.6(1);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for the determination and for the
    recommended period of placement on the suspension list; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an indication that the designated learning
    institution may make written submissions with respect to the
    information referred to in paragraphs (b) to (e) or any
    justification under subsection 222.2(3) within 30 days after the day
    on which it receives the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Receipt of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 9.3(2), the notice of preliminary
  finding is deemed to have been received 10 days after the day on which
  it is sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction or cancellation of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The officer may, at any time before the notice of
  final determination is issued, cancel a notice of preliminary finding
  or issue a corrected notice of preliminary finding.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1494960){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1494960.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written submissions

- **[[222.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A designated learning institution to
  which is issued a notice of preliminary finding under subsection
  222.3(1) or a corrected notice of preliminary finding under subsection
  222.3(3) may, within 30 days after the day on which it receives the
  notice, make written submissions with respect to the information
  referred to in paragraphs 222.3(1)(b) to (e) or any justification
  under subsection 222.2(3) and include with those submissions any
  relevant document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on the request of a designated
  learning institution, grant a single extension of not more than 30
  days for the designated learning institution to make written
  submissions under subsection (1) if the Minister determines that
  exceptional circumstances have prevented or impeded the designated
  learning institution from making those submissions.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1494967){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1494967.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of final determination

- **[[222.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, after the time for making
  written submissions has elapsed, the Minister determines that a
  designated learning institution has failed to comply with a condition
  set out in subsection 222.1(1) and that the failure is not justified,
  they must issue to the designated learning institution a notice of
  final determination that sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the designated learning institution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the condition with which the designated learning
    institution failed to comply;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the designated learning
      institution is to be placed on the suspension list referred to in
      subsection 222.6(1), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a warning advising the designated learning
      institution that it is not to be placed on the suspension list but
      that the determination of non-compliance may be considered in
      determining whether, in the event of any subsequent failure to
      comply with a condition set out in subsection 222.1(1), the
      designated learning institution is to be placed on the list; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for the determination and, if
    applicable, for the designated learning institution's placement on
    the suspension list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations --- suspension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether the designated learning
  institution is to be placed on the suspension list referred to in
  subsection 222.6(1) and, if applicable, the period for which it is to
  be placed on that list, the Minister must consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the frequency with which the designated learning
    institution has failed to comply with the conditions set out in
    subsection 222.1(1), including any such failures for which a warning
    was issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the seriousness of the designated learning
    institution's failure to comply with the conditions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the designated learning institution's efforts to
    comply with the conditions;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the designated learning institution's cooperation
    during the verification referred to in subsection 222.2(2); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any written submissions made by the designated
    learning institution under subsection 222.4(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period of suspension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The maximum period for which a designated learning
  institution may remain on the suspension list is 12 consecutive
  months.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1494987){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1494987.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension list

- **[[222.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish and
  maintain a suspension list that is accessible to the public and that
  sets out, for each designated learning institution that received a
  notice of final determination indicating that it was to be placed on
  the list,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the designated learning institution's name, mailing
    address and website;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conditions set out in subsection 222.1(1) with
    which the designated learning institution failed to comply;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the designated learning
    institution was placed on the list; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the designated learning
    institution is to remain on the list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application not to be processed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application for a study permit that names a
  designated learning institution and that is received during a period
  in which that designated learning institution is on the suspension
  list must be returned to the applicant without being processed, along
  with all documents submitted in support of the application and the fee
  for processing it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (2) does not apply
  to an application for the renewal of a study permit to allow the
  applicant to complete their course or program of study.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-219, s. 9](#1494629-1495000){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1494629-1495000.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Cancellation --- Study Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1508779 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation due to error

**[[222.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.7 .sectionLabel}** An officer may
cancel a study permit that was issued to a foreign national if the
officer is satisfied that the study permit was issued based on an
administrative error.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 7](#1508537-1508793){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508537-1508793.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

**[[222.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222.8 .sectionLabel}** A study permit that
was issued to a foreign national is cancelled if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national becomes a permanent resident; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is deceased.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-11, s. 7](#1508537-1508799){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1508537-1508799.html"}
:::

## [PART 13]{.HLabel1}[Removal]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689321 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Removal Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689322 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of removal order

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** There are three types
of removal orders, namely, departure orders, exclusion orders and
deportation orders.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Departure order

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, an enforced departure
  order is a circumstance in which the foreign national is exempt from
  the requirement to obtain an authorization in order to return to
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is issued a departure order
  must meet the requirements set out in paragraphs 240(1)(a) to (c)
  within 30 days after the order becomes enforceable, failing which the
  departure order becomes a deportation order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- stay of removal and
  detention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the foreign national is detained within the 30-day
  period or the removal order against them is stayed, the 30-day period
  is suspended until the foreign national's release or the removal order
  becomes enforceable.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2011-126, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-224-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 224"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion order

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, and subject to
  subsections (3) and (4), an exclusion order obliges the foreign
  national to obtain a written authorization in order to return to
  Canada during the one-year period after the exclusion order was
  enforced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, the
  expiry of a one-year period following the enforcement of an exclusion
  order, or a five-year period if subsection (3) applies, is a
  circumstance in which the foreign national is exempt from the
  requirement to obtain an authorization in order to return to Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misrepresentation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is issued an exclusion order
  as a result of the application of paragraph 40(2)(a) of the Act must
  obtain a written authorization in order to return to Canada within the
  five-year period after the exclusion order was enforced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of par. 42(1)(b) of the Act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, the
  making of an exclusion order against a foreign national on the basis
  of inadmissibility under paragraph 42(1)(b) of the Act is a
  circumstance in which the foreign national is exempt from the
  requirement to obtain an authorization in order to return to Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2011-126, s. 6
- SOR/2014-269, ss. 4, 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20110616.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deportation order

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, and subject to subsection
  (2), a deportation order obliges the foreign national to obtain a
  written authorization in order to return to Canada at any time after
  the deportation order was enforced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of par. 42(1)(b) of the Act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, the
  making of a deportation order against a foreign national on the basis
  of inadmissibility under paragraph 42(1)(b) of the Act is a
  circumstance in which the foreign national is exempt from the
  requirement to obtain an authorization in order to return to Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal order --- certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 52(1) of the Act, a
  removal order referred to in section 80 of the Act obliges the foreign
  national to obtain a written authorization in order to return to
  Canada at any time after the removal order was enforced.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2011-126, s. 7
- SOR/2014-269, s. 7
- SOR/2016-136, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-226-20141121.html "Link to previous version of section 226"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report --- family members

- **[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 42 of the Act, a report prepared under
  subsection 44(1) of the Act against a foreign national is also a
  report against the foreign national's family members in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal --- family members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A removal order made by the Immigration Division
  against a foreign national is also a removal order against their
  family members in Canada to whom subsection (1) applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer informed the family members of the
    report, that they are the subject of an admissibility hearing and of
    their right to make submissions and be represented, at their own
    expense, at the admissibility hearing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the family members are subject to a decision of the
    Immigration Division that they are inadmissible under section 42 of
    the Act on grounds of the inadmissibility of the foreign national.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-136, s. 6(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 227"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Specified Removal Order]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689363 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 44(2) of the Act --- foreign
nationals

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 44(2) of the Act, and subject to
  subsections (3) and (4), if a report in respect of a foreign national
  does not include any grounds of inadmissibility other than those set
  out in the following circumstances, the report shall not be referred
  to the Immigration Division and any removal order made shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    paragraph 36(1)(a) or (2)(a) of the Act on grounds of serious
    criminality or criminality, a deportation order;

  - [(a.01)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible on
    grounds of transborder criminality under subsection 36(2.1) of the
    Act for having committed one of the following offences on their
    entry into Canada, a deportation order:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an offence under section 86, 87, 90, 91, 95 or
      104 or subsection 320.15(1) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an offence under paragraph 124(1)(b) of the
      [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an offence under section 110 of the [Firearms
      Act](/eng/acts/F-11.6),

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an offence under subsection 159(1) of the
      [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) with respect to goods referred to
      in subparagraph 19(1)(d)(ii) of these Regulations,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} an offence under subsection 160(1) of the
      [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) that is one of the following
      contraventions:

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} a contravention of section 12 of that Act, with
        respect to goods referred to in clause 19(1)(e)(i)(B) of these
        Regulations, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a contravention of section 156 of that Act;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    paragraph 40(1)(a) of the Act on grounds of misrepresentation
    relating to their exemption from the requirement to obtain a
    temporary resident visa when applying for an electronic travel
    authorization, an exclusion order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    paragraph 40(1)(c) of the Act on grounds of misrepresentation, a
    deportation order;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    subsection 40.1(1) of the Act on grounds of the cessation of refugee
    protection, a departure order;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    section 41 of the Act on grounds of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} failing to appear for further examination or an
      admissibility hearing under Part 1 of the Act, an exclusion order,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} failing to obtain the authorization of an
      officer required by subsection 52(1) of the Act, a deportation
      order,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} failing to establish that they hold the visa or
      other document as required under section 20 of the Act, an
      exclusion order,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} failing to leave Canada by the end of the period
      authorized for their stay as required by subsection 29(2) of the
      Act, an exclusion order,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} failing to comply with subsection 29(2) of the
      Act as a result of non-compliance with any condition set out in
      paragraph 183(1)(d), section 184 or subsection 220.1(1), an
      exclusion order,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} failing to comply with the requirement under
      subsection 20(1.1) of the Act to not seek to enter or remain in
      Canada as a temporary resident while being the subject of a
      declaration made under subsection 22.1(1) of the Act, an exclusion
      order,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} failing to comply with the condition set out in
      paragraph 43(1)(e), an exclusion order,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} failing to submit to a medical examination as
      required by paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act, an exclusion order, or

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} failing to appear for examination as required by
      subsection 18(1) of the Act when seeking to enter Canada at a port
      of entry, an exclusion order;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (e), if the foreign national
    is inadmissible under section 42 of the Act on grounds of an
    inadmissible family member, the same removal order as was made in
    respect of the inadmissible family member;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible on grounds
    of an inadmissible family member in accordance with paragraph
    42(2)(a) of the Act, a deportation order; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign national is inadmissible under
    subsection 35.1(1) of the Act on grounds of sanctions, a deportation
    order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direct back

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 44(2) of the Act,
  and subject to subsection (4), if a foreign national is the subject of
  a report and the following circumstances apply, the report shall not
  be referred to the Immigration Division and the removal order made
  shall be an exclusion order:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer directed the foreign national to return
    temporarily to the United States under section 41; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national is inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(a) of the Act for failing to establish that they will
    leave Canada by the end of the period authorized for their stay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsection 44(2) of the Act ---
  permanent residents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection 44(2) of the Act, if a
  removal order is made against a permanent resident who fails to comply
  with the residency obligation under section 28 of the Act, the order
  shall be a departure order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligible claim for refugee protection

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a claim for refugee protection is made and the
  claim has been determined to be eligible to be referred to the Refugee
  Protection Division or no determination has been made, a departure
  order is the applicable removal order in the circumstances set out in
  paragraph (1)(a.1) or any of subparagraphs (1)(c)(i), (iii) to
  (v), (viii) and (ix).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports in respect of certain foreign
  nationals

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (1) and (1.1), a
  report in respect of a foreign national does not include a report in
  respect of a foreign national who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is under 18 years of age and not accompanied by a
    parent or an adult legally responsible for them; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is unable, in the opinion of the Minister, to
    appreciate the nature of the proceedings and is not accompanied by a
    parent or an adult legally responsible for them.

::: HistoricalNote

- SOR/2013-210, s. 4

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paragraph 45(d) of the Act --- applicable
removal order

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 45(d) of the Act, the applicable removal
  order to be made by the Immigration Division against a person is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible under
    subsection 34(1) of the Act on security grounds;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible under
    subsection 35(1) of the Act on grounds of violating human or
    international rights;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, in the case of a permanent
    resident inadmissible under subsection 36(1) of the Act on grounds
    of serious criminality or a foreign national inadmissible under
    paragraph 36(1)(b) or (c) of the Act on grounds of serious
    criminality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 36(2)(b) or (c) of the Act on grounds of criminality;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible
    under subsection 36(2.1) of the Act on grounds of transborder
    criminality for having committed, on their entry into Canada, an
    offence other than one referred to in any of subparagraphs
    228(1)(a.01)(i) to (v);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible under
    subsection 37(1) of the Act on grounds of organized criminality;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    subsection 38(1) of the Act on health grounds, unless subsection (2)
    or (3) applies;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    section 39 of the Act for financial reasons, unless subsection (2)
    or (3) applies;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 40(1)(a) or (b) of the Act for misrepresentation, unless
    subsection (3) applies;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a deportation order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 40(1)(d) of the Act for misrepresentation;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(a) of the Act for failing to comply with the
    requirement to appear for examination, unless subsection (2) or (3)
    applies;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a departure order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(b) of the Act;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(a) of the Act for failing to establish that they have
    come to Canada in order to establish permanent residence, unless
    subsection (3) applies;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(a) of the Act for failing to establish that they will
    leave Canada by the end of the period authorized for their stay,
    unless subsection (2) applies; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} an exclusion order, if they are inadmissible under
    paragraph 41(a) of the Act for any other failure to comply with the
    Act, unless subsection (2) or (3) applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligible claim for refugee protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a claim for refugee protection is made and the
  claim has been determined to be eligible to be referred to the Refugee
  Protection Division or no determination has been made, a departure
  order is the applicable removal order in the circumstances set out in
  paragraph (1)(f), (g), (j), (m) or (n).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The applicable removal order in the circumstances set
  out in paragraph (1)(f), (g), (h), (j), (l) or (n) is a deportation
  order if the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was previously subject to a removal order and they
    are inadmissible on the same grounds as in that order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has failed to comply with any condition or
    obligation imposed under the Act or the Immigration Act, chapter I-2
    of the Revised Statutes of Canada, 1985, unless the failure is the
    basis for the removal order; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has been convicted in Canada of an offence under an
    Act of Parliament punishable by way of indictment or of two offences
    under any Act of Parliament not arising out of a single occurrence,
    unless the conviction or convictions are the grounds for the removal
    order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishable by way of indictment

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (3)(c), an offence
  that may be prosecuted either summarily or by way of indictment is
  deemed to be an offence punishable by way of indictment, even if it
  has been prosecuted summarily.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Section 228 circumstances

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Division makes a removal order
  against a foreign national with respect to any grounds of
  inadmissibility that are circumstances set out in section 228, the
  Immigration Division shall make

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal order that would have been applicable
    under subsection 228(1) or (3) if the report had not been referred
    to the Immigration Division under subsection 44(2) of the Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national described in
    paragraph 228(4)(a) or (b), the removal order that would have been
    applicable under subsection 228(1) or (3) if the foreign national
    had not been described in that paragraph.

::: HistoricalNote

- SOR/2016-136, s. 7

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Stay of Removal Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689421 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may impose a stay on removal orders with respect to a country
  or a place if the circumstances in that country or place pose a
  generalized risk to the entire civilian population as a result of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an armed conflict within the country or place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an environmental disaster resulting in a
    substantial temporary disruption of living conditions; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any situation that is temporary and generalized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may cancel the stay if the circumstances
  referred to in subsection (1) no longer pose a generalized risk to the
  entire civilian population.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The stay does not apply to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is inadmissible under subsection 34(1) of the Act
    on security grounds;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is inadmissible under subsection 35(1) of the Act
    on grounds of violating human or international rights;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} is inadmissible under subsection 35.1(1) of the
    Act on grounds of sanctions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is inadmissible under subsection 36(1) of the Act
    on grounds of serious criminality, under subsection 36(2) of the Act
    on grounds of criminality or under subsection 36(2.1) of the Act on
    grounds of transborder criminality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is inadmissible under subsection 37(1) of the Act
    on grounds of organized criminality;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} is a person referred to in section F of Article 1
    of the Refugee Convention; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} informs the Minister in writing that they consent
    to their removal to a country or place to which a stay of removal
    applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-136, s. 8(F)
- [2023, c. 19, s. 21](#1411958-1414407){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1411958-1414407.html"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 69](#1433253-1433956){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1433253-1433956.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-230-20230622.html "Link to previous version of section 230"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of removal --- judicial review

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4), a
  removal order is stayed if the subject of the order makes an
  application for leave for judicial review in accordance with section
  72 of the Act with respect to a decision of the Refugee Appeal
  Division that rejects, or confirms the rejection of, a claim for
  refugee protection, and the stay is effective until the earliest of
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application for leave is refused,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the application for leave is granted, the
    application for judicial review is refused and no question is
    certified for the Federal Court of Appeal,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a question is certified by the Federal Court,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the appeal is not filed within the time limit, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court of Appeal decides to dismiss
      the appeal, and the time limit in which an application to the
      Supreme Court of Canada for leave to appeal from that decision
      expires without an application being made,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an application for leave to appeal is made to
    the Supreme Court of Canada from a decision of the Federal Court of
    Appeal referred to in paragraph (c), the application is refused, and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the application referred to in paragraph (d) is
    granted, the appeal is not filed within the time limit or the
    Supreme Court of Canada dismisses the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if, when leave is
  applied for, the subject of the removal order is a designated foreign
  national.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} There is no stay of removal if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is subject to a removal order because
    they are inadmissible on grounds of serious criminality; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the subject of the removal order resides or
    sojourns in the United States or St. Pierre and Miquelon and is the
    subject of a report prepared under subsection 44(1) of the Act on
    their entry into Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the person applies
  for an extension of time to file an application referred to in that
  subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 8, s. 183
- SOR/2012-272, s. 1
- SOR/2016-136, s. 9(F)
- [SOR/2022-113, s. 1](#1345472-1348701){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1345472-1348701.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-231-20160613.html "Link to previous version of section 231"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of removal --- pre-removal risk
assessment

**[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** A removal order is
stayed when a person is notified by the Department under subsection
160(3) that they may make an application under subsection 112(1) of the
Act, and the stay is effective until the earliest of the following
events occurs:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Department receives confirmation in writing from
  the person that they do not intend to make an application;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person does not make an application within the
  period provided under section 162;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the application for protection is rejected;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2012-154, s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if a decision to allow the application for protection
  is made under paragraph 114(1)(a) of the Act, the decision with
  respect to the person's application to remain in Canada as a permanent
  resident is made; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person to whom subsection 112(3) of
  the Act applies, the stay is cancelled under subsection 114(2) of the
  Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of removal --- humanitarian and
compassionate or public policy considerations

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** A removal order made
against a foreign national, and any family member of the foreign
national, is stayed if the Minister is of the opinion that the stay is
justified by humanitarian and compassionate considerations, under
subsection 25(1) or 25.1(1) of the Act, or by public policy
considerations, under subsection 25.2(1) of the Act. The stay is
effective until a decision is made to grant, or not grant, permanent
resident status.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-252, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-233-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 233"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of par. 50(a) of the Act

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty
and for the purposes of paragraph 50(a) of the Act, a decision made in a
judicial proceeding would not be directly contravened by the enforcement
of a removal order if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is an agreement between the Canada Border
  Services Agency and the Attorney General of Canada or the attorney
  general of a province that criminal charges will be withdrawn or
  stayed on the removal of the person from Canada; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an agreement between the Canada Border
  Services Agency and the Attorney General of Canada or the attorney
  general of a province to withdraw or cancel any summons or subpoena on
  the removal of the person from Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-136, s. 10(E)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 4
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Enforcement of Removal Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-689480 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not void

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
and subject to section 51 of the Act, a removal order does not become
void by reason of any lapse of time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Providing copies

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** A person against whom a
removal order is made shall be provided with a copy of the order when it
is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modality of enforcement

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** A removal order is
enforced by the voluntary compliance of a foreign national with the
removal order or by the removal of the foreign national by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-136, s. 11(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-237-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 237"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary compliance

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national who wants to voluntarily comply with a removal order
  must appear before an officer who shall determine if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has sufficient means to effect
    their departure to a country that they will be authorized to enter;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national intends to voluntarily comply
    with the requirements set out in paragraphs 240(1)(a) to (c) and
    will be able to act on that intention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Choice of country

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Following the appearance referred to in subsection
  (1), the foreign national must submit their choice of destination to
  the officer who shall approve the choice unless the foreign national
  is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a danger to the public;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a fugitive from justice in Canada or another
    country; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} seeking to evade or frustrate the cause of justice
    in Canada or another country.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal by Minister

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** If a foreign national
does not voluntarily comply with a removal order, a negative
determination is made under subsection 238(1) or the foreign national's
choice of destination is not approved under subsection 238(2), the
removal order shall be enforced by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When removal order is enforced

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  removal order against a foreign national, whether it is enforced by
  voluntary compliance or by the Minister, is enforced when the foreign
  national

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appears before an officer at a port of entry to
    verify their departure from Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} obtains a certificate of departure from the Canada
    Border Services Agency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} departs from Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is authorized to enter, other than for purposes of
    transit, their country of destination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When removal order is enforced by
  officer outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a foreign national against whom a removal order
  has not been enforced has departed from Canada and applies outside
  Canada for a visa, an electronic travel authorization or an
  authorization to return to Canada, an officer shall enforce the order
  if, following an examination, the foreign national establishes that
  they are the person described in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When removal order is enforced by
  officer in Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A removal order against a foreign national is
  enforced by an officer in Canada when the officer confirms that the
  foreign national has departed from Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections (2) and (3)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsections (2) and (3) apply
  in respect of any removal order made before the day on which those
  subsections come into force.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-77, s. 7
- SOR/2016-136, s. 12(F)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 5
- SOR/2018-232, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20171018.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Country of removal

- **[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  removal order is enforced under section 239, the foreign national
  shall be removed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the country from which they came to Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the country in which they last permanently resided
    before coming to Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a country of which they are a national or citizen;
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the country of their birth.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal to another country

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If none of the countries referred to in
  subsection (1) is willing to authorize the foreign national to enter,
  the Minister shall select any country that will authorize entry within
  a reasonable time and shall remove the foreign national to that
  country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 238 and subsection (1), the Minister
  shall remove a person who is subject to a removal order on the grounds
  of inadmissibility referred to in paragraph 35(1)(a) of the Act to a
  country that the Minister determines will authorize the person to
  enter.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-212, s. 11(F)](#1138244-1145989){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138244-1145989.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-241-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 241"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal
Matters Act](/eng/acts/M-13.6)

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** A person transferred
under an order made under the [Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal
Matters Act](/eng/acts/M-13.6) is not, for the purposes of paragraph
240(1)(d), a person who has been authorized to enter their country of
destination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of removal costs

- **[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless expenses incurred by His
  Majesty in right of Canada have been recovered from a transporter, a
  foreign national who is removed from Canada at His Majesty's expense
  shall not return to Canada if the foreign national has not paid to His
  Majesty the removal costs of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} \$3,840 for removal without escort or under escort
    other than by air; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} \$12,880 for removal under escort by air.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- medical escort

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph 1(b), a foreign national who is
  removed by air under medical escort is subject to the removal cost set
  out in paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- under 18

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply to a foreign
  national who is under 18 years of age at the time the removal order is
  made.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-284, s. 1](#1502523-1526376){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1502523-1526376.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-243-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 243"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Cancellation of Immigration Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1448689 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Making of a removal order

**[[243.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
documents, held by a foreign national, are cancelled when a removal
order is made against that foreign national :

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a temporary resident visa;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an electronic travel authorization; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a temporary resident permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-11, s. 4](#1441344-1448696){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1441344-1448696.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforceable removal order

**[[243.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243.2 .sectionLabel}** The following
documents, held by a foreign national, are cancelled when a removal
order made against that foreign national becomes enforceable:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a work permit; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a study permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-11, s. 4](#1441344-1448702){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1441344-1448702.html"}
:::

## [PART 14]{.HLabel1}[Detention and Release]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689533 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
Division 6 of Part 1 of the Act, the factors set out in this Part shall
be taken into consideration when assessing whether a person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is unlikely to appear for examination, an
  admissibility hearing, removal from Canada, or at a proceeding that
  could lead to the making of a removal order by the Minister under
  subsection 44(2) of the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a danger to the public; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a foreign national whose identity has not been
  established.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flight risk

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
paragraph 244(a), the factors are the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} being a fugitive from justice in a foreign
  jurisdiction in relation to an offence that, if committed in Canada,
  would constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} voluntary compliance with any previous departure
  order;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} voluntary compliance with any previously required
  appearance at an immigration or criminal proceeding;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} previous compliance with any conditions imposed in
  respect of entry, release or a stay of removal;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any previous avoidance of examination or escape from
  custody, or any previous attempt to do so;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} involvement with a people smuggling or trafficking in
  persons operation that would likely lead the person to not appear for
  a measure referred to in paragraph 244(a) or to be vulnerable to being
  influenced or coerced by an organization involved in such an operation
  to not appear for such a measure; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the existence of strong ties to a community in
  Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Danger to the public

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
paragraph 244(b), the factors are the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the fact that the person constitutes, in the opinion
  of the Minister, a danger to the public in Canada or a danger to the
  security of Canada under paragraph 101(2)(b), subparagraph 113(d)(i)
  or (ii) or paragraph 115(2)(a) or (b) of the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} association with a criminal organization within the
  meaning of subsection 121(2) of the Act;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} engagement in people smuggling or trafficking in
  persons;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} conviction in Canada under an Act of Parliament for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sexual offence, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an offence involving violence or weapons;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} conviction for an offence in Canada under any of the
  following provisions of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances
  Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8), namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 5 (trafficking),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 6 (importing and exporting), and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 7 (production);

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} conviction outside Canada, or the existence of
  pending charges outside Canada, for an offence that, if committed in
  Canada, would constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sexual offence, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an offence involving violence or weapons;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} conviction outside Canada, or the existence of
  pending charges outside Canada, for an offence that, if committed in
  Canada, would constitute an offence under any of the following
  provisions of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances
  Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8), namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 5 (trafficking),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 6 (importing and exporting), and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 7 (production);

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} conviction for an offence in Canada under any of the
  following provisions of the [Cannabis Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5), namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 9 (distribution),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 10 (selling),

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 11 (importing and exporting), and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} section 12 (production); and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} conviction outside Canada, or the existence of
  pending charges outside Canada, for an offence that, if committed in
  Canada, would constitute an offence under any of the following
  provisions of the [Cannabis Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5), namely,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 9 (distribution),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 10 (selling),

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 11 (importing and exporting), and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} section 12 (production).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-136, s. 13(F)
- SOR/2018-170, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-246-20160613.html "Link to previous version of section 246"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity not established

- **[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 244(c), the factors are the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national's cooperation in providing
    evidence of their identity or assisting the Department or the Canada
    Border Services Agency in obtaining evidence of their identity, in
    providing the date and place of their birth as well as the names of
    their mother and father, in providing detailed information on the
    itinerary they followed in travelling to Canada or in completing an
    application for a travel document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national who makes a claim
    for refugee protection, the possibility of obtaining identity
    documents or information without divulging personal information to
    government officials of their country of nationality or, if there is
    no country of nationality, their country of former habitual
    residence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national's destruction of their
    identity or travel documents, or the use of fraudulent documents by
    the foreign national in order to mislead the Department or the
    Canada Border Services Agency, and the circumstances under which the
    foreign national acted;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the provision of contradictory information by the
    foreign national with respect to their identity during the
    processing of an application by the Department or the Canada Border
    Services Agency; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the existence of documents that contradict
    information provided by the foreign national with respect to their
    identity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application to minors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Consideration of the factors set out in paragraph
  (1)(a) shall not have an adverse impact with respect to minor children
  referred to in section 249.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 65(E)
- SOR/2016-136, s. 14(E)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-247-20160613.html "Link to previous version of section 247"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other factors

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** If it is determined
that there are grounds for detention, the following factors shall be
considered before a decision is made on detention or release:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reason for detention;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the length of time in detention;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether there are any elements that can assist in
  determining the length of time that detention is likely to continue
  and, if so, that length of time;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any unexplained delays or unexplained lack of
  diligence caused by the Department, the Canada Border Services Agency
  or the person concerned;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the existence of alternatives to detention; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the best interests of a directly affected child who
  is under 18 years of age.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-214, s. 7
- [SOR/2019-213, s. 1](#1138259-1145993){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138259-1145993.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-248-20171018.html "Link to previous version of section 248"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Best interests of the child

- **[[248.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph 248(f)
  and for the application, in respect of children who are under 18 years
  of age, of the principle affirmed in section 60 of the Act, that a
  minor child shall be detained only as a measure of last resort, the
  following factors must be considered when determining the best
  interests of the child:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the child's physical, emotional and psychological
    well-being;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the child's healthcare and educational needs;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the importance of maintaining relationships and the
    stability of the family environment, and the possible effect on the
    child of disrupting those relationships or that stability;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the care, protection and safety needs of the child;
    and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the child's views and preferences, provided the
    child is capable of forming their own views or expressing their
    preferences, taking into consideration the child's age and maturity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Degree of dependence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph 248(f), the level of
  dependency of the child on the person for whom there are grounds to
  detain shall also be considered when determining the best interests of
  the child.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-213, s. 2](#1138261-1146005){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1138261-1146005.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special considerations for minor children

**[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** For the application of
the principle affirmed in section 60 of the Act that a minor child shall
be detained only as a measure of last resort, the special considerations
that apply in relation to the detention of minor children who are less
than 18 years of age are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the availability of alternative arrangements with
  local child-care agencies or child protection services for the care
  and protection of the minor children;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated length of detention;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the risk of continued control by the human smugglers
  or traffickers who brought the children to Canada;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the type of detention facility envisaged and the
  conditions of detention;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the availability of accommodation that allows for the
  segregation of the minor children from adult detainees who are not the
  parent of or the adult legally responsible for the detained minor
  children; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the availability of services in the detention
  facility, including education, counselling and recreation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications for travel documents

**[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** If a completed
application for a passport or travel document must be provided as a
condition of release from detention, any completed application provided
by a foreign national who makes a claim for refugee protection shall not
be divulged to government officials of their country of nationality or,
if there is no country of nationality, their country of previous
habitual residence, as long as the removal order to which the foreign
national is subject is not enforceable.

## [PART 15]{.HLabel1}[Prescribed Conditions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689610 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadmissibility on grounds of security
--- conditions

**[[250.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsections 44(4), 56(3), 58(5), 58.1(4), 77.1(1) and 82(6) of the Act,
the conditions that must be imposed on a foreign national or permanent
resident are the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to inform the Canada Border Services Agency in
