  writing of their address and, in advance, of any change in that
  address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to inform the Canada Border Services Agency in
  writing of their employer's name and the address of their place of
  employment and, in advance, of any change in that information;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} unless they are otherwise required to report to the
  Canada Border Services Agency because of a condition imposed under
  subsection 44(3), 56(1), 58(3) or 58.1(3) or paragraph 82(5)(b) of the
  Act, to report once each month to the Agency;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to present themselves at the time and place that an
  officer, the Immigration Division, the Minister or the Federal Court
  requires them to appear to comply with any obligation imposed on them
  under the Act;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} to produce to the Canada Border Services Agency
  without delay the original of any passport and travel and identity
  documents that they hold, or that they obtain, in order to permit the
  Agency to make copies of those documents;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} if a removal order made against them comes into
  force, to surrender to the Canada Border Services Agency without delay
  any passport and travel document that they hold;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if a removal order made against them comes into force
  and they do not hold a document that is required to remove them from
  Canada, to take without delay any action that is necessary to ensure
  that the document is provided to the Canada Border Services Agency,
  such as by producing an application or producing evidence verifying
  their identity;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} to not commit an offence under an Act of Parliament
  or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would constitute an
  offence under an Act of Parliament;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} if they are charged with an offence under an Act of
  Parliament or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would
  constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament, to inform the Canada
  Border Services Agency of that charge in writing and without delay;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} if they are convicted of an offence under an Act of
  Parliament or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would
  constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament, to inform the Canada
  Border Services Agency of that conviction in writing and without
  delay; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} if they intend to leave Canada, to inform the Canada
  Border Services Agency in writing of the date on which they intend to
  leave Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-214, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immigration Appeal Division ---
conditions

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 68(2) of the Act, the conditions that must be imposed on a
foreign national or permanent resident by the Immigration Appeal
Division are the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to inform the Canada Border Services Agency and the
  Immigration Appeal Division in writing, in advance, of any change in
  their address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to provide to the Canada Border Services Agency a
  copy of any passport and travel document that they hold and, if they
  do not hold any such documents, to complete an application for a
  passport or travel document and provide that application to the
  Agency;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to apply for an extension of the validity period of
  any passport and travel document that they hold before it expires and
  to provide a copy of the extended document to the Canada Border
  Services Agency;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to not commit an offence under an Act of Parliament
  or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would constitute an
  offence under an Act of Parliament;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if they are charged with an offence under an Act of
  Parliament or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would
  constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament, to inform the Canada
  Border Services Agency of that charge in writing and without delay;
  and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} if they are convicted of an offence under an Act of
  Parliament or an offence that, if committed in Canada, would
  constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament, to inform the Canada
  Border Services Agency and the Immigration Appeal Division of that
  conviction in writing and without delay.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-140, s. 17(F)
- SOR/2017-214, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-251-20140529.html "Link to previous version of section 251"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 16]{.HLabel1}[Seizure]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689636 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Custody of seized thing

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** A thing seized under
subsection 140(1) of the Act shall be placed without delay in the
custody of the Department or the Canada Border Services Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2017-214, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-252-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 252"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of seizure --- person from whom
seized

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer who seizes a thing under subsection 140(1) of the Act shall
  provide written notice to the person from whom it was seized,
  including the grounds for the seizure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of seizure --- lawful owner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person from whom the thing was seized is not
  the lawful owner, the officer shall make reasonable efforts to
  identify the lawful owner and to give the lawful owner written notice
  of, and the grounds for, the seizure. If the notice is provided by
  mail, notification is deemed to have been provided on the seventh day
  after the day on which the notice was mailed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315822){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315822.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-253-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 253"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for return --- person from
whom seized

- **[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  thing was seized on the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly
  obtained or used, or that the seizure was necessary to prevent its
  fraudulent or improper use, the person from whom it was seized may
  apply for its return in writing within 60 days after the day on which
  the notification referred to in subsection 253(1) was provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return --- improperly or fraudulently
  obtained

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly obtained, in order
  to have the thing returned, the applicant shall demonstrate that they
  were entitled to have that thing in their possession at the time of
  the seizure and that they are still entitled to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return --- improperly or fraudulently
  used

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly used, in order to
  have it returned, the applicant shall demonstrate that they did not
  participate in the fraudulent or improper use of the thing, that at
  the time of the seizure they were entitled to have that thing in their
  possession and that they are still entitled to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return --- prevention of improper or
  fraudulent use

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that the seizure was necessary to prevent its fraudulent or
  improper use, in order to have it returned, the applicant shall
  demonstrate that the seizure is no longer necessary to prevent its
  fraudulent or improper use, that at the time of the seizure they were
  entitled to have that thing in their possession and that they are
  still entitled to it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315833){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315833.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-254-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 254"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for return --- lawful owner

- **[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  thing was seized on the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly
  obtained or used, or that the seizure was necessary to prevent its
  fraudulent or improper use, the lawful owner may apply for its return
  in writing within 60 days after the day on which the notification
  referred to in subsection 253(2) was provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to lawful owner --- improperly
  or fraudulently obtained

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly obtained, in order
  to have the thing returned, the applicant shall demonstrate that at
  the time of the seizure they were the lawful owner and that they are
  still the lawful owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to lawful owner --- improperly
  or fraudulently used

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that it was fraudulently or improperly used, in order to
  have it returned, the applicant shall demonstrate that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they did not participate in the fraudulent or
    improper use of the thing, that at the time of the seizure they were
    the lawful owner and that they are still the lawful owner; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they exercised all reasonable care to satisfy
    themselves that the person to whom they gave possession of the thing
    was not likely to fraudulently or improperly use it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to lawful owner --- prevention
  of improper or fraudulent use

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the application is in respect of a thing seized on
  the ground that the seizure was necessary to prevent its fraudulent or
  improper use, the thing shall be returned to the applicant if they
  demonstrate that the seizure is no longer necessary to prevent its
  fraudulent or improper use, that at the time of the seizure they were
  the lawful owner and that they are still the lawful owner.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315846){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315846.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-255-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 255"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrent applications

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** If the person from whom
a thing was seized makes an application under section 254 for the return
of the thing and the lawful owner makes an application under section 255
for the same thing, the application of the lawful owner will be
processed first. If the application of the lawful owner is granted, the
application of the person from whom the thing was seized shall not be
processed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315850){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315850.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-256-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 256"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** The decision on the
application under section 254 or 255 and the reasons for it shall be
given in writing and provided to the applicant as soon as feasible. If
the applicant is notified by mail, notification is deemed to have been
provided on the seventh day after the day on which the notice was
mailed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315854){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315854.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 257"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic return

- **[[257.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the seizure of a thing was
  necessary to carry out the purposes of the Act, the thing shall be
  returned to its lawful owner without delay if the seizure is no longer
  necessary for those purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return --- seizure in error

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the seizure of a thing was made in error, the
  thing shall be returned without delay to the person from whom the
  thing was seized or, if that is not possible, to its lawful owner.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315861){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315861.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditional return

**[[257.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257.2 .sectionLabel}** A thing seized
shall be returned only if its return would not be contrary to the
purposes of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315865){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315865.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale or destruction of seized thing

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  thing seized, other than a document, is not returned to its lawful
  owner or the person from whom it was seized, the thing shall be sold
  unless the costs of the sale would exceed the monetary value of the
  thing, in which case the thing shall be destroyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale or destruction suspended

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A thing seized shall not be sold or destroyed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within the 15 days after the day on which the
    notification of a decision not to return it was provided; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before a final decision is made in any judicial
    proceeding in Canada affecting the seizure or the return of the
    thing seized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return or disposal of documents

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a document is not returned to its lawful owner or
  the person from whom it was seized, the document shall be held for as
  long as is necessary for the administration of the laws of Canada,
  after which it will either be returned to the authority that issued it
  or disposed of in accordance with the laws of Canada.\

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-202, s. 1](#1315164-1315876){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1315164-1315876.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-258-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 258"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 17]{.HLabel1}[Transportation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689700 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed persons

**[[258.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
paragraph 148(1)(a) of the Act, the following persons are prescribed:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any foreign national who is the subject of a
  declaration made under subsection 22.1(1) of the Act, unless they hold
  a temporary resident permit issued under section 24 of the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any foreign national who is not authorized under
  subsection 52(1) of the Act to return to Canada; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any foreign national who is prohibited from entering
  Canada by an order or regulation made by the Governor in Council under
  the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5) or the [Quarantine
  Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-210, s. 5
- SOR/2016-37, s. 3
- [SOR/2020-55, s. 6](#1241244-1241855){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1241244-1241855.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-258.1-20160311.html "Link to previous version of section 258.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed documents

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 148(1) of the Act, the following documents that a person
requires under the Act to enter Canada are prescribed:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a travel document referred to in subsection 31(3) of
  the Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} refugee travel papers issued by the Minister;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a document referred to in subsection 50(1) or 52(1);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a temporary travel document referred to in section
  151;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a visa referred to in section 6 or subsection 7(1);

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a permanent resident card; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} an electronic travel authorization referred to in
  section 7.01 or 7.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-46, s. 1
- SOR/2016-37, ss. 4(F), 14
- SOR/2017-53, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-259-20160930.html "Link to previous version of section 259"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holding prescribed documentation

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  commercial transporter has reasonable grounds to believe that the
  prescribed documents of a person whom it carries to Canada may not be
  available for examination at a port of entry, the commercial
  transporter must give the person a receipt for the documents and hold
  the documents until examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presenting documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A commercial transporter that holds the documents of
  a person must, when presenting the person for examination under
  paragraph 148(1)(b) of the Act, present the documents and a copy of
  the receipt.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-260-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 260"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to hold a person

- **[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 148(1)(b) of the Act, a transporter has
  complied with the obligation to hold a person until the examination is
  completed when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer informs the transporter that the
    examination of the person is completed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized to enter Canada under
    section 23 of the Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person is detained under any Canadian law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A transporter must notify an officer without delay if
  a person whose examination has not been completed leaves or attempts
  to leave the transporter's vehicle for any other purpose than
  examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stowaway notification

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** On the arrival of a
vessel at its first port of call in Canada, the transporter must notify
an officer at the nearest port of entry of the presence of any stowaway
and, on request of the officer, must without delay provide a written
report concerning the stowaway.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical examination and treatment

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  commercial transporter must arrange for the medical examination of a
  foreign national who is required to submit to one under paragraph
  16(2)(b) of the Act and for any medical examination, surveillance or
  treatment that is imposed under section 32.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national holds a temporary or permanent
    resident visa at the time of their examination and the foreign
    national's health condition is not a result of the commercial
    transporter's negligence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national has been authorized to enter
    and remain in Canada and is not a member of the crew.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed medical costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph 148(1)(g) of the Act,
  any medical costs incurred with respect to the foreign national are
  prescribed costs and are to be calculated on the basis of the
  applicable provincial health insurance system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-154, s. 13
- SOR/2016-37, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-263-20120815.html "Link to previous version of section 263"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed information

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** A transporter must
provide without delay any of the following documents that are requested
by an officer within 72 hours after the presentation for examination of
a person carried by the transporter to Canada:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of any ticket issued to the person;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a document specifying the person's itinerary,
  including the place of embarkation and dates of travel; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a document identifying the document number and type
  of passport, travel document or identity document carried by the
  person, the country of issue and the name of the person to whom it was
  issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crew list

- **[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  arrival at the first port of call in Canada of a vessel registered in
  a foreign country, the transporter must provide an officer at the
  nearest port of entry with a list of all members of the crew.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amended crew list

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The transporter must maintain on board a current list
  of all members of the crew while the vessel is in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final crew list

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before the vessel's departure from its final port of
  call in Canada, the transporter must provide an officer with a copy of
  the list referred to in subsection (1) that includes any changes made
  while the vessel was in Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assembly

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** On the request of an
officer, a transporter must assemble without delay aboard the vessel all
members of the crew.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian registered vessels

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** On the arrival of a
vessel registered in Canada at its first port of call in Canada, the
transporter must notify an officer at the nearest port of entry of all
members of the crew who are not Canadian citizens or permanent residents
and, on request, provide the officer with a list of all crew members.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting obligation

- **[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  transporter must, without delay, notify an officer at the nearest port
  of entry of any foreign national who ceases to be a member of the crew
  for a reason listed in paragraph 3(1)(b). The transporter must record
  that information and provide it in writing on the request of the
  officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to join means of transportation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A transporter must, without delay, notify an officer
  at the nearest port of entry when a foreign national who entered
  Canada to become a member of the crew of the transporter's vessel
  fails to join the means of transportation within the period provided
  in paragraph 184(2)(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 66
- SOR/2016-37, s. 7(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-268-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 268"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed information

- **[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the request of an official of the Canada Border Services Agency, a
  commercial transporter that carries or expects to carry persons to
  Canada on board its commercial vehicle must provide the Canada Border
  Services Agency with the following information about each person whom
  it expects to carry:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their surname, first name and any middle names,
    their date of birth, their citizenship or nationality and their
    gender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type and number of each passport or other
    travel document that identifies them and the name of the country or
    entity that issued it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} their reservation record locator number, if any;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the unique passenger reference assigned to them by
    the commercial transporter, if any, or, in the case of a crew member
    who has not been assigned one, notice of their status as a crew
    member;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any information about the person that is in a
    reservation system of the commercial transporter or its agent; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the following information about their carriage on
    board the commercial vehicle:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the person is carried or is expected to be
      carried on board the commercial vehicle by air, the date and time
      of take-off from the last point of embarkation of persons before
      the commercial vehicle arrives in Canada or, if the person is
      carried or is expected to be carried on board the vehicle by water
      or land, the date and time of departure from the last point of
      embarkation of persons before the commercial vehicle arrives in
      Canada,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the last point of embarkation of persons before
      the commercial vehicle arrives in Canada,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of arrival of the commercial
      vehicle at the first point of disembarkation of persons in Canada,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the first point of disembarkation of persons in
      Canada, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a commercial vehicle that carries
      persons or goods by air, the flight code identifying the
      commercial transporter and the flight number.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic means

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in subsection (1) must be
  provided by electronic means in accordance with the technical
  requirements, specifications and procedures for electronic data
  interchange set out in the document entitled CBSA Carrier Messaging
  Requirements issued by the Canada Border Services Agency, as amended
  from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of transmission --- paragraphs
  (1)(a) to (d)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in paragraphs (1)(a)
  to (d) must be provided

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not later than one hour before the time of
    departure, if the information relates to a member of the crew; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not later than the time of check-in, if the
    information relates to any other person who is expected to be on
    board the commercial vehicle.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of transmission --- paragraph
  (1)(e)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in paragraph (1)(e) must
  be provided not later than at the time of departure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of transmission --- paragraph
  (1)(d)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in paragraph (1)(d) must
  also be provided, for each passenger who is on board the commercial
  vehicle at the time of departure, not later than 30 minutes after the
  time of departure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incomplete or inaccurate information

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A commercial transporter that becomes aware before or
  at the time of departure that information they have provided under
  paragraph 148(1)(d) of the Act is incomplete or inaccurate must, in
  the manner described in subsection (2) and without delay, provide the
  Canada Border Services Agency with the missing or accurate
  information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- paragraph (1)(e)

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (6) does not apply in respect of
  information referred to in paragraph (1)(e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of transmission --- paragraph
  (1)(f)

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in paragraph (1)(f) must
  be provided at the same time that any information referred to in
  subsections (3) to (6) is provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum retention period

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Canada Border Services Agency may retain
  information referred to in paragraphs (1)(a) to (d) about a person for
  up to three years and six months after the day of departure of the
  commercial vehicle that carried or was to carry the person to Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention period --- investigation

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} After the period referred to in subsection (9), the
  Canada Border Services Agency may retain the information referred to
  in that subsection about a person for as long as it is required as
  part of an investigation, but in no case longer than six years after
  the day of departure of the commercial vehicle that carried or was to
  carry the person to Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-269-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 269"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice by Canada Border Services Agency

- **[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Canada Border Services Agency may notify a commercial transporter that
  a person whom it expects to carry to Canada may be a person who is
  prescribed under section 258.1 or may be a person who does not hold
  the necessary documents prescribed under section 259.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations unchanged

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1) does not
  relieve a commercial transporter of its obligation to comply with any
  requirement imposed by the Act or these Regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-270-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 270"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facilities for holding and examination

- **[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  commercial transporter, and a transporter who operates an airport or
  an international bridge or tunnel, must without cost to Her Majesty in
  right of Canada provide and maintain facilities, including areas,
  offices and laboratories, that are adequate for the proper holding and
  examination of persons being carried to Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The facilities referred to in subsection (1) are
  adequate if they satisfy the applicable requirements of Part II of the
  [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), are secure and, if necessary,
  sterile, and include equipment and furnishings that permit officers to
  discharge their duties under the Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination on vessels

**[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** A commercial
transporter carrying persons to Canada aboard its vessel must provide
facilities aboard the vessel that permit an officer to conduct
examinations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to carry from Canada

- **[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  commercial transporter that has carried a foreign national referred to
  in this subsection to Canada or caused such a foreign national to be
  carried to Canada must, without delay, carry the foreign national from
  Canada

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to any other country, in the case of a foreign
    national directed to leave under subsection 40(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the United States, in the case of a foreign
    national directed back to that country under section 41;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to any other country, in the case of a foreign
    national allowed to withdraw their application under section 42; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to the country to which the foreign national is
    removed under section 241, in the case of a foreign national who is
    subject to an enforceable removal order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conveyance to vehicle

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The transporter must transport a foreign national who
  is subject to an enforceable removal order from wherever the foreign
  national is situated in Canada to the vehicle in which they will be
  carried to another country.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-273-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 273"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of a crew

- **[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  transporter carries, or causes to be carried, a foreign national to
  Canada as a member of its crew or to become a member of its crew, and
  the foreign national is subject to an enforceable removal order, the
  transporter must carry that foreign national from Canada to the
  applicable country as determined under Division 4 of Part 13.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conveyance to vehicle

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The transporter must transport the foreign national
  referred to in subsection (1) from wherever the foreign national is
  situated in Canada to the vehicle in which they will be carried to
  another country.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** A transporter must
notify an officer without delay if a foreign national referred to in
section 273 or 274 whom they are carrying from Canada leaves or attempts
to leave a vehicle before they are carried from Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notifying commercial transporter

- **[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a foreign national who seeks to enter Canada is made subject to a
  removal order and a commercial transporter is or may be required under
  the Act to carry that foreign national from Canada, an officer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify the commercial transporter that it is or may
    be required to carry the foreign national from Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the removal order is enforceable, notify the
    commercial transporter of its obligation to carry the foreign
    national from Canada and, if an escort is necessary, request the
    commercial transporter to provide an escort or to make arrangements
    for the carriage of an escort assigned by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrangements and notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After being notified under paragraph (1)(b), the
  commercial transporter must, without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make arrangements to carry the foreign national
    from Canada and notify an officer of those arrangements; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify an officer that it is unable to make such
    arrangements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The commercial transporter must carry the foreign
  national from Canada within 48 hours after giving the notice required
  by paragraph (2)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-compliance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a commercial transporter does not comply with
  paragraph 2(a) or subsection (3) or notifies an officer that it is
  unable to comply with those provisions, or if an officer notifies the
  commercial transporter in writing that the proposed arrangements are
  not acceptable, an officer must cause the foreign national to be
  carried from Canada and the commercial transporter must pay the costs
  referred to in section 278.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for acceptance of
  arrangements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} To be acceptable, the arrangements referred to in
  subsection (2) must meet the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the itinerary must begin where the foreign national
    is situated in Canada and end in the country and city to which the
    foreign national is to be removed, following the most direct routing
    possible;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the itinerary must not include a country through
    which transit has not been approved; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period of time between any connections must not
    exceed 12 hours.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for escort

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A commercial transporter that has been notified under
  paragraph 1(b) must comply with any request by an officer to provide
  an escort or to arrange for the carriage of the escort assigned by the
  Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-276-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 276"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief from obligations

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** Despite sections 273
and 276, a commercial transporter is relieved of its obligation to carry
a foreign national from Canada, except for a member of its crew or a
foreign national who entered Canada to become a member of its crew, if
the foreign national at the time of their examination

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was authorized to enter and remain in Canada on a
  temporary basis;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a temporary or permanent resident visa; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} was a person prescribed under section 258.1 and the
  commercial transporter was not, before the person was carried to
  Canada, notified under section 270 that the person may have been a
  prescribed person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, ss. 10, 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-277-20160311.html "Link to previous version of section 277"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal costs

**[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** A transporter that is
required under the Act to carry a foreign national from Canada must pay
the following costs of removal and, if applicable, attempted removal:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} expenses incurred within or outside Canada with
  respect to the foreign national's accommodation and transport,
  including penalties for changes of date or routing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} accommodation and travel expenses incurred by any
  escorts provided to accompany the foreign national;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} fees paid in obtaining passports, travel documents
  and visas for the foreign national and any escorts;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the cost of meals, incidentals and other expenses as
  calculated in accordance with the rates set out in the Travel
  Directive published by the Treasury Board Secretariat, as amended from
  time to time;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any wages paid to escorts and other personnel; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the costs or expenses incurred with respect to
  interpreters and medical and other personnel engaged for the removal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of administration fee

- **[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  administration fee must be assessed against a commercial transporter
  in respect of any of the following foreign nationals it carried or
  caused to be carried to Canada:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is inadmissible under
    section 41 of the Act for failing to meet the requirements of
    section 6, subsection 7(1), section 7.01 or 7.1 or subsection 50(1)
    or 52(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is prescribed under section
    258.1 or whom the commercial transporter has been directed under
    paragraph 148(1)(a) of the Act not to carry to Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is exempt, under subsection
    52(2), from the requirement to hold a passport or travel document
    but who fails to produce sufficient evidence of their identity;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who failed to appear for an
    examination on entry into Canada;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who entered Canada as a member
    of a crew or to become a member of a crew and is inadmissible; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is subject to a removal
    order, or is allowed under section 42 to withdraw their application
    to enter Canada, and who fails to leave Canada immediately.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An administration fee shall not be assessed against a
  commercial transporter in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person referred to in section 39;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who is prescribed under section
    258.1 and in respect of whom notice under section 270 was not given
    before the foreign national was carried to Canada but who holds the
    necessary documents prescribed under section 259;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national who does not hold an electronic
    travel authorization when one is required under section 7.01 or 7.1
    and in respect of whom the Canada Border Services Agency did not
    give notice under section 270 to the commercial transporter before
    the foreign national was carried to Canada, but who holds one of the
    required prescribed documents set out in paragraphs 259(a) to (f);
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a foreign national, other than a foreign national
    referred to in paragraph 190(3)(c), who seeks to enter Canada to
    obtain permanent residence and is inadmissible under paragraph 41(a)
    of the Act for failing to obtain a permanent resident visa as
    required under section 6, but who is exempted under Division 1 of
    Part 2 from the requirement to obtain an electronic travel
    authorization required under section 7.01 or 7.1 or exempted under
    Division 5 of Part 9 from the requirement to obtain a temporary
    resident visa.

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2016-37, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 68
- SOR/2016-37, ss. 11, 16
- SOR/2017-53, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-279-20160930.html "Link to previous version of section 279"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administration fee

- **[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  administration fee assessed under section 279 is \$3,200.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Memorandum of understanding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a memorandum of understanding in accordance with
  subsection (3) is in effect between the commercial transporter and the
  Minister, the administration fee assessed is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} \$3,200, if the commercial transporter does not
    demonstrate that it complies with the memorandum of understanding,
    if the commercial transporter carried to Canada a foreign national
    who is prescribed under section 258.1 or whom the commercial
    transporter has been directed under paragraph 148(1)(a) of the Act
    not to carry to Canada, or if the administration fee is assessed in
    respect of a member of the crew of the transporter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} \$2,400, if the commercial transporter demonstrates
    that it complies with the memorandum of understanding;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} \$1,600, if the commercial transporter demonstrates
    that it complies with the memorandum of understanding and has a
    number of administration fee assessments that is equal to or less
    than the number specified in the memorandum, for the period
    specified in the memorandum, for reducing the administration fee by
    50%;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} \$800, if the commercial transporter demonstrates
    that it complies with the memorandum of understanding and has a
    number of administration fee assessments that is equal to or less
    than the number specified in the memorandum, for the period
    specified in the memorandum, for reducing the administration fee by
    75%; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} \$0, if the commercial transporter demonstrates
    that it complies with the memorandum of understanding and has a
    number of administration fee assessments that is equal to or less
    than the number specified in the memorandum, for the period
    specified in the memorandum, for reducing the administration fee by
    100%.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of memorandum of understanding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A memorandum of understanding referred to in
  subsection (2) shall include provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} document screening;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the training of personnel in document screening;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the use of technological aids;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} fraud prevention;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} gate checks;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} information exchange;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} performance standards and administration fee
    assessments;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} compliance monitoring of the provisions of the
    memorandum of understanding;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} holding documents under section 260;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} providing information referred to in subsections
    269(1) and (2);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} stowaways; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} security screening of members of a crew.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-280-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 280"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of assessment

- **[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  assessment of an administration fee shall be served personally, by
  registered mail, by facsimile with acknowledgement of receipt or by
  electronic transmission on a representative of the commercial
  transporter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service effected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Service of the assessment by registered mail is
  deemed to have been effected on the seventh day after the day on which
  the assessment was mailed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submissions concerning assessment

- **[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  commercial transporter may submit written submissions to the Minister
  within 30 days after being served with an assessment of an
  administration fee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final decision and notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If submissions are made, the Minister must consider
  the submissions, confirm, vary or cancel the assessment and give
  written notice of the final decision to the commercial transporter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Elements considered

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} In considering the submissions, the Minister must
  take into account whether the commercial transporter was, before the
  foreign national was carried to Canada, notified under section 270
  that the foreign national may have been a person prescribed under
  section 258.1 or a person who did not hold an electronic travel
  authorization when one was required under section 7.01 or 7.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If no submissions are made within the 30-day period,
  the assessment is final and the commercial transporter is liable for
  the assessment at the end of that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister confirms an assessment under
  subsection (2), the commercial transporter is liable for the
  assessment on the date the notice is sent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-37, ss. 13, 17
- SOR/2017-53, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-282-20160930.html "Link to previous version of section 282"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

- **[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on the basis of the following factors, require a
  commercial transporter to provide security for compliance with its
  obligations under paragraphs 148(1)(a) to (g) of the Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and regularity of arrival, or
    anticipated arrival, of the transporter's vehicles carrying persons
    to Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of persons carried, or anticipated to be
    carried, to Canada aboard the transporter's vehicles;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the transporter has carried an inadmissible
    foreign national to Canada; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated risk of inadmissible foreign
    nationals being carried to Canada by the transporter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount of security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister requires security to be provided, the
  Minister shall determine the amount of security on the basis of the
  following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the commercial transporter's record of compliance
    with the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated risk of inadmissible foreign
    nationals being carried to Canada by the transporter and the
    estimated removal costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of security

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A commercial transporter who is required to provide
  security must provide it in the form of a cash deposit unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the transporter has entered into a memorandum of
    understanding referred to in subsection 280(2) that provides for
    another form of security; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the transporter demonstrates that there is no
    significant risk of a debt not being paid if they were to provide
    another form of security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of security

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister determines on the basis of the
  factors set out in subsection (1) that security is no longer required,
  the Minister shall return the security to the commercial transporter.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of s. 148(2) of the Act

**[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 148(2) of the Act, a prescribed good is a good that is not
land, a building or a transportation facility.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Object detained or seized

**[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** If an object is
detained or seized under subsection 148(2) of the Act, that object shall
remain detained or seized until

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the transporter complies with its obligations under
  section 148 of the Act; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the transporter's obligations are discharged by
  another person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of seizure

- **[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Following a seizure under subsection
  148(2) of the Act, an officer shall make reasonable efforts to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify the lawful owner of the object seized; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} give notice of the seizure to that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition after seizure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A thing seized under subsection 148(2) of the Act
  shall be disposed of by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} returning the object to the transporter on receipt
    of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the value of the object at the
      time of seizure and any expenses incurred in the seizure and, if
      applicable, detention,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the security required under the Act or any costs
      and fees for which the transporter is liable, as well as an amount
      equal to any expenses incurred in the seizure and, if applicable,
      detention, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} evidence that the transporter is in compliance
      with its obligations under subsection 148(1) of the Act and has
      reimbursed Her Majesty in right of Canada for any expenses
      incurred in the seizure and, if applicable, detention; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disposing of the object under section 287.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale of a seized object

- **[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  transporter does not comply with paragraph 286(2)(a) within a
  reasonable time, an officer shall give notice to the transporter that
  the object will be sold. The object shall then be sold for the benefit
  of Her Majesty in right of Canada and the proceeds of the sale shall
  be applied to the transporter's outstanding debt to Her Majesty under
  the Act. Any surplus shall be returned to the transporter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs incurred in seizure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any expenses incurred by Her Majesty in right of
  Canada in selling the object, and any expenses incurred in the seizure
  or, if applicable, detention of the object, shall be deducted from the
  proceeds of the sale.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-167, s. 69(E)
:::

## [PART 18]{.HLabel1}[Loans]{.HTitleText1} {#h-689978 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[beneficiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[beneficiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a person, means

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person's spouse, common-law partner or conjugal
  partner;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a dependent child of the person or of the person's
  spouse, common-law partner or conjugal partner; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other person who, at the time of their
  application for a permanent resident visa or their application to
  remain in Canada as a permanent resident, is in a relationship of
  dependency with the person by virtue of being cared for by or
  receiving emotional and financial support from the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 10(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-288-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 288"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of loans

**[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may make
loans to the following persons for the following purposes:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to a foreign national referred to in Part 1 of the
  Act for the purpose of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to the foreign national and
    their beneficiaries of transportation from their point of departure
    outside Canada to their point of destination in Canada, and related
    administrative charges,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} assisting the foreign national and their
    beneficiaries to become established in Canada, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} defraying the fee, referred to in subsection
    303(1), payable for the acquisition by the foreign national and
    their beneficiaries of permanent resident status;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to a foreign national referred to in Part 2 of the
  Act for the purpose of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to the foreign national and
    their beneficiaries of transportation from their point of departure
    outside Canada to their point of destination in Canada, and related
    administrative charges,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to the foreign national and
    their beneficiaries of transportation to attend any interview
    relating to their application, and related administrative charges,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to the foreign national and
    their beneficiaries of a medical examination under paragraph
    16(2)(b) of the Act, and related costs and administrative charges,
    or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} assisting the foreign national and their
    beneficiaries to become established in Canada; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to a permanent resident or a Canadian citizen for the
  purpose of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to their beneficiaries of
    transportation from their point of departure outside Canada to their
    point of destination in Canada, and related administrative charges,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} defraying the cost to their beneficiaries of a
    medical examination under paragraph 16(2)(b) of the Act, and related
    costs and administrative charges, if the beneficiaries are protected
    persons within the meaning of subsection 95(2) of the Act, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} defraying the fee, referred to in subsection
    303(1), payable for the acquisition by their beneficiaries of
    permanent resident status.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 11(F)
- SOR/2012-154, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-289-20090604.html "Link to previous version of section 289"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum amount

- **[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  maximum amount of advances that may be made under subsection 88(1) of
  the Act is \$400 million.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Total loans

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The total amount of all loans made under this Part
  plus accrued interest on those loans shall not at any time exceed the
  maximum amount of advances prescribed by subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-22, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-149, s. 1](#1358374-1362562){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1358374-1362562.html"}
- [SOR/2023-279, s. 1](#1435030-1435283){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1435030-1435283.html"}
- [SOR/2024-127, s. 1](#1455894-1457561){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455894-1457561.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-290-20231219.html "Link to previous version of section 290"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repayment

- **[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 292, a loan made
  under section 289 becomes payable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a loan for the purpose of defraying
    transportation costs, one year after the day on which the person for
    whose benefit the loan was made enters Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a loan for any other purpose, one
    year after the day on which the loan was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repayment terms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 292, a loan made under section
  289, together with all accrued interest, if applicable, must be repaid
  in full, in consecutive monthly instalments, within

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 36 months after the day on which the loan becomes
    payable, if the amount of the loan is not more than \$1,200;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 48 months after the day on which the loan becomes
    payable, if the amount of the loan is more than \$1,200 but not more
    than \$2,400;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 60 months after the day on which the loan becomes
    payable, if the amount of the loan is more than \$2,400 but not more
    than \$3,600;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 72 months after the day on which the loan becomes
    payable, if the amount of the loan is more than \$3,600 but not more
    than \$4,800; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 96 months after the day on which the loan becomes
    payable, if the amount of the loan is more than \$4,800.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 12(F)
- SOR/2018-22, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-291-20090604.html "Link to previous version of section 291"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deferred repayment

- **[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  repaying a loan, in accordance with the requirements of section 291,
  that was made to a person under section 289 would, by reason of the
  person's income, assets and liabilities, cause the person financial
  hardship, an officer may, to the extent necessary to relieve that
  hardship but subject to subsection (2), defer the commencement of the
  repayment of the loan, defer payments on the loan, vary the amount of
  the payments or extend the repayment period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A repayment period shall not be extended beyond

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an additional 24 months, in the case of a loan
    referred to in paragraph 289(b); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an additional six months, in the case of any other
    loan.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 13(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-292-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 292"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No interest on loan

- **[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  loan made under this Part bears no interest as of the day on which
  this section comes into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid loan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The interest on any loan that has not been repaid
  before that day continues to accrue until the day preceding that day.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-116, s. 1
- SOR/2018-22, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-293-20060601.html "Link to previous version of section 293"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 19]{.HLabel1}[Fees]{.HTitleText1} {#h-690040 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-690041 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[294]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a fee payable under this Part is payable not per
  application but for each person in respect of whom an application is
  made;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections 295(3), 301(2), 304(2) and
  314(3), a fee payable under this Part for processing an application is
  payable at the time the application is made;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections 295(4) and 301(3), if the
  requirement to pay a fee depends on a person's age or the amount of a
  fee is calculated in accordance with their age, the age of the person
  shall be determined as of the day the application in respect of which
  the fee is payable is made; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the fees set out in subsections 295(1) and 301(1),
  section 302, subsection 304(1) and section 307 shall be indexed at
  09:00:00 a.m. Eastern daylight time on April 30, 2022, and every two
  years after that on April 30 at that same time, in accordance with the
  cumulative percentage increase to the Consumer Price Index for Canada,
  published by Statistics Canada, for the two previous years, rounded to
  the nearest five dollars.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-163, s. 14
- SOR/2013-73, s. 3
- SOR/2014-19, s. 1
- [SOR/2020-45, s. 1](#1239275-1245339){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1239275-1245339.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-294-20140206.html "Link to previous version of section 294"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Fees for Applications for Authorizations, Visas and Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-690049 .Subheading}

#### [Electronic Travel Authorizations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690050 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee --- \$7

- **[[294.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A fee of \$7 is payable for
  processing an application for an electronic travel authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person whose application for a work permit or study
  permit or for renewal of a work permit or study permit is considered,
  as the case may be, under subsection 12.04(5) or (6), to constitute an
  application for an electronic travel authorization is not required to
  pay the fee referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-77, s. 8
- SOR/2017-53, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-294.1-20150801.html "Link to previous version of section 294.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Permanent Resident Visas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690058 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent resident visa

- **[[295]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-295 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following fees are payable for processing an application for a
  permanent resident visa:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the application is made by a person as a member
    of the family class

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a principal applicant, other than a
      principal applicant referred to in subparagraph (ii), \$475,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a principal applicant who is a
      foreign national referred to in any of paragraphs 117(1)(b),
      (f), (g) or (h), is less than 22 years of age and is not a spouse
      or common-law partner, \$75,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a spouse or common-law partner, \$550, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a dependent child, \$150;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application is made by a person as a member
    of the Quebec investor class, the Quebec entrepreneur class, the
    start-up business class, the self-employed persons class or the
    Quebec self-employed persons class

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a principal applicant, \$1,575,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a spouse or common-law partner, \$825, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a dependent child, \$225; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the application is made by a person as a member
    of any other class or by a person referred to in section 71

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a principal applicant, \$825,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a spouse or common-law partner, \$825, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a family member of the principal
      applicant who is a dependent child, \$225.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- refugees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following persons are not required to pay the
  fees referred to in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who makes an application as a member of
    the Convention refugees abroad class and the family members included
    in the member's application; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who makes an application as a member of a
    humanitarian-protected persons abroad class and the family members
    included in the member's application.

- [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2019-174, s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2016-316, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment by sponsor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A fee payable under subsection (1) in respect of a
  person who makes an application as a member of the family class or
  their family members

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is payable, together with the fee payable under
    subsection 304(1), at the time the sponsor files the sponsorship
    application; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be repaid in accordance with regulations
    referred to in subsection 20(2) of the [Financial Administration
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11) if, before the processing of the application
    for a permanent resident visa has begun, the sponsorship application
    is withdrawn by the sponsor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(a), the age of the
  person in respect of whom the application is made shall be determined
  as of the day the sponsorship application is filed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2003-383, s. 6

#### [Temporary Resident Visas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690092 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single or multiple entry --- \$100

- **[[296]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-296 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fee of \$100 is payable for processing an application for a temporary
  resident visa to enter Canada one or more times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following persons are not required to pay the fee
  referred to in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a properly accredited diplomat, consular officer,
    representative or official of a country other than Canada, of the
    United Nations or any of its agencies or of any intergovernmental
    organization of which Canada is a member, the members of the suite
    of such a person and the family members of such a person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a member of the armed forces of a country that is a
    designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as a
    civilian component of that visiting force under paragraph 4(c) of
    that Act, and their family members;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is a member of the clergy, a member of
    a religious order or a lay person who is to assist a congregation or
    a group in the achievement of its spiritual goals, if the duties to
    be performed by the person are to consist mainly of preaching
    doctrine, presiding at liturgical functions or spiritual
    counselling, and their family members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} persons, other than a group of performing artists
    and their staff, who apply at the same time and place for a work
    permit or a study permit;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person who is seeking to enter Canada

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting hosted by
      the Government of Canada, an organization of the United Nations or
      the Organization of American States, as a participant,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting as a
      representative of the Organization of American States or the
      Caribbean Development Bank, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting hosted
      by the Government of Canada, an organization of the United Nations
      or the Organization of American States, at the invitation of the
      Government of Canada;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person who is seeking to enter Canada as a
    competitor, coach, judge, team official, medical staff member or
    member of a national or international sports organizing body
    participating in the Pan-American Games, when held in Canada, or as
    a performer participating in a festival associated with any of those
    Games; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person who is seeking to enter Canada for a
    period of less than 48 hours and who is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} travelling by transporter's vehicle to a
      destination other than Canada, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} transiting through or stopping over in Canada
      for refuelling or for the continuation of their journey in another
      transporter's vehicle.

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-19, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum fee --- family

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The total amount of fees payable under subsection (1)
  by an applicant and their family members who apply together and at the
  same time must not exceed \$500.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-63, s. 1
- SOR/2010-121, s. 1
- SOR/2014-19, s. 2
- [SOR/2019-174, s. 13](#1135491-1138059){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1135491-1138059.html"}
:::

#### [Temporary Resident Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690116 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee --- \$200

- **[[298]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-298 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fee of \$200 is payable for processing an application for a temporary
  resident permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following persons are not required to pay the fee
  referred to in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person referred to in subsection 295(2) or any of
    paragraphs 296(2)(c) and (d), 299(2)(a), (b), (d) to (f) and (h)
    to (k) and 300(2)(f) to (i);

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} a properly accredited diplomat, consular officer,
    representative or official of a country other than Canada, of the
    United Nations or any of its agencies or of any intergovernmental
    organization of which Canada is a member, the members of the suite
    of such a person and the family members of such a person;

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} a member of the armed forces of a country that is
    a designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as a
    civilian component of that visiting force under paragraph 4(c) of
    that Act, and their family members;

  - [(a.3)]{.lawlabel} a person whose work in Canada would create or
    maintain reciprocal employment for Canadian citizens or permanent
    residents of Canada in other countries and who is a family member of
    a person referred to in subparagraph 299(2)(g)(iii);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person in respect of whom an application for a
    permanent resident visa, an application to remain in Canada as a
    permanent resident, or an application under subsection 25(1) of the
    Act is pending, or in respect of whom a decision under subsection
    25.1(1) or 25.2(1) of the Act is pending;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a citizen of Costa Rica seeking to enter and remain
    in Canada during the period beginning on May 11, 2004 and ending on
    May 12, 2004, if the person does not hold a temporary resident visa
    but is not otherwise inadmissible;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who is seeking to enter Canada

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting hosted by
      the Government of Canada, an organization of the United Nations or
      the Organization of American States, as a participant,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting as a
      representative of the Organization of American States or the
      Caribbean Development Bank, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of attending a meeting hosted
      by the Government of Canada, an organization of the United Nations
      or the Organization of American States, at the invitation of the
      Government of Canada; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person who, while they are in transit to Canada,
    ceases to be exempt under paragraph 190(1)(a) from the requirement
    for a temporary resident visa, if, during the first 48 hours after
    they cease to be exempt from that requirement, they seek to enter
    and remain in Canada and are inadmissible to Canada for the sole
    reason that they do not have a temporary resident visa.

- SOR/2003-197, s. 3

#### [Work Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690135 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee --- \$155

- **[[299]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-299 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fee of \$155 is payable for processing an application for a work
  permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following persons are not required to pay the fee
  referred to in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in Canada who has made a refugee claim
    that has not yet been determined by the Refugee Protection Division,
    and their family members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person in Canada on whom refugee protection has
    been conferred, and their family members;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is a member of the Convention refugees
    abroad class or a member of a humanitarian-protected persons abroad
    class, and their family members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who holds a study permit and is
    temporarily destitute, as described in paragraph 208(a);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person whose work in Canada is designated under
    subparagraph 205(c)(i);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person who works in Canada for a Canadian
    religious or charitable organization, without remuneration;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person whose work in Canada would create or
    maintain reciprocal employment for Canadian citizens or permanent
    residents of Canada in other countries and who is a family member of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a properly accredited diplomat, consular officer,
      representative or official of a country other than Canada, of the
      United Nations or any of its agencies or of any intergovernmental
      organization of which Canada is a member,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a member of the armed forces of a country that
      is a designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
      Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as
      a civilian component of that visiting force under paragraph 4(c)
      of that Act, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an officer of a foreign government sent, under
      an exchange agreement between Canada and one or more countries, to
      take up duties with a federal or provincial agency;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a person who works in Canada under an agreement
    entered into with a country by Canada or by or on behalf of a
    province, that provides for reciprocal employment opportunities of
    an artistic, cultural or educational nature;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person whose work in Canada is pursuant to an
    international student or young workers reciprocal employment
    program;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a person who works in Canada as an officer of the
    United States Immigration and Naturalization Service or of United
    States Customs carrying out pre-inspection duties, as an American
    member of the International Joint Commission or as a United States
    grain inspector, and their family members;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a United States Government official in possession
    of an official United States passport who is assigned to a temporary
    posting in Canada, and their family members; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} a person described in section 207.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum fee --- group

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The total amount of fees payable under subsection (1)
  by a group of three or more persons consisting of performing artists
  and their staff who apply together and at the same time for a work
  permit is \$465.

#### [Study Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-690159 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee --- \$150

- **[[300]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-300 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fee of \$150 is payable for processing an application for a study
  permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following persons are not required to pay the fee
  referred to in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in Canada who has made a refugee claim
    that has not yet been determined by the Refugee Protection Division,
    and their family members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person in Canada on whom refugee protection has
    been conferred, and their family members;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is a member of the Convention refugees
    abroad class or a humanitarian-protected persons abroad class, and
    their family members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a properly accredited diplomat, consular officer,
    representative or official of a country other than Canada, of the
    United Nations or any of its agencies or of any intergovernmental
    organization of which Canada is a member, the members of the suite
    of such a person and the family members of such a person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a member of the armed forces of a country that is a
    designated state for the purposes of the [Visiting Forces
    Act](/eng/acts/V-2), including a person who has been designated as a
    civilian component of that visiting force under paragraph 4(c) of
    that Act, and their family members;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person who holds a study permit and is
    temporarily destitute, as described in paragraph 208(a);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person whose study in Canada is under an
    agreement or arrangement between Canada and another country that
    provides for reciprocity of student exchange programs;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a person who works in Canada as an officer of the
    United States Immigration and Naturalization Service or of United
    States Customs carrying out pre-inspection duties, as an American
    member of the International Joint Commission or as a United States
    grain inspector, and their family members; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a United States Government official in possession
    of an official United States passport who is assigned to a temporary
    posting in Canada, and their family members.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2011-222, s. 9
- SOR/2014-19, s. 5
- SOR/2015-25, s. 5(E)
:::
